## Mod Identity

- **Mod Name:** Towards Victory (胜利条件)
- **Mod ID:** `eu5mp.towards_victory`
- **Version:** Build date (`YYMMDD`)
- **Target:** EU5 `1.*.*`
- **Status:** In Development — All 6 Victory paths fully implemented: Conquest, Prosperity, Trade, Diplomatic, Cultural, and Scientific
- **Language:** English + Simplified Chinese

## Development Policy

Towards Victory is an unreleased project with date-based `YYMMDD` versions. No saved-game compatibility, published
mod-version compatibility, or public API compatibility is required. Active code and data
should represent one current architecture. When a refactor improves clarity, performance,
generator ownership, or data flow, update all producers and consumers to the new shape and
delete stale branches rather than keeping migrations, aliases, duplicate paths, wrapper
entry points, or defensive fallbacks for old internal states. Existing helpers should be
reused only when they fit the current scope/lifecycle/data model; otherwise split, replace,
or remove them.

Existing mentions of compatibility launchers, legacy schema blocks, migrated old variables,
startup/migration repair, or fields retained for compatibility describe cleanup debt, not
contracts. When touching those areas, convert callers/data to the current shape and remove
the old path instead of preserving the compatibility layer.

## Summary

**Standalone Engineering Department mod (2026-07):** the entire Engineering
Department IO / Wonder Construction subsystem (conception, debate, survey,
organization, logistics, construction, ceremony, finalization, ownership
transfer, map mode, Europedia cards, all 54 generic + 123 unique wonders) was
split out of the main "Towards Victory" mod into its own standalone,
independently-deployed mod at repo-root `src_engineering_department/`
(mirroring `src/`'s `in_game/`/`main_menu/` layout), with its own generator
tree at `scripts_engineering_department/` (mirroring `scripts/`). It has no
dependency on the main mod and is fully playable without it, but it requires
Community Mod Framework (CMF) 2.x because CMF supplies the custom
`on_game_load` callback; the main mod instead
declares a hard dependency on it (`src/.metadata/metadata.json`), since
Prosperity Victory's establishment effect calls
`tv_engineering_department_create_effect`, which now lives only there —
that call is already idempotent (guards on `any_international_organizations_member_of`),
so it needs no changes now that the new mod also creates the IO unconditionally.
`build.bat` deploys it as a fourth step (`tv_engineering_department` folder).
Shared cross-IO files were split
so the new mod is self-contained: `gen_tv_io_leader_actions.py` and
`gen_tv_pulse_registry.py` became multi-output generators (like `gen_victory.py`)
emitting a second file filtered by the `wonder`/`engineering_department`
substring convention already used for filename-based domain routing, applied
here to IO-type names and on_action ids; `tv_io_role_modifiers.txt`,
`tv_game_concepts.txt` (+ loc), and `tv_io_chief_alert_triggers.txt` had their
Engineering-Department-specific entries hand-moved. The singleton
`character_title.txt` and `messagetypes.txt` databases are generated as full
vanilla copies with root-specific insertions: the main TV root carries the TV +
Engineering Department union, the Engineering Department root carries only its
own entries, and Court Positions carries only its own entries. `TV` must be
sorted after Engineering Department when both are enabled so the TV copy wins.
`scripts/validate.py` verifies the vanilla-copy prefixes plus each root's own
singleton coverage (see anti-pattern `eu5_vanilla_copy_singleton_file_cannot_split_across_mods`).
The Engineering
Department CMF alerts are also fully standalone: the missing-Great-Engineer
alert owns its monthly sync, while four explicit wonder-action alerts synchronize
directly from the relevant phase transitions for proposal choice, survey site
choice, construction-site lock-in, and generic-ceremony style choice. The Engineering
Department root owns the shared `cmf_on_callback` handler, trigger/effects,
scripted-GUI request clearer, hidden GUI bridge/widget registration, and EN/ZH localization.
Its Community Mod Menu integration also registers a host-global Wonder Control tab
with ten Boolean settings, three 0--50k direct-build price sliders, and three 1--500k
module-requirement sliders. These controls
can expose final wonder buildings in the ordinary building list, snapshot and skip
individual lifecycle phases, suppress survey/construction random events, override the
scale and worksite caps to 6 and 100, tune small/medium/large module progress, and give
directly built wonders size-based gold prices plus the five-year `huge_unique_build_time`. Phase
skips are captured when a project enters the affected phase, reuse the canonical
completion/finalization effects, and clear their project variables through the normal
lifecycle cleanup; absent CMM aliases retain the original 100k/200k/300k requirements
and default lifecycle behavior.
There is no accepted standalone cosmetic gap. `scripts/validate.py`, `scripts/ai_context.py`, and
`scripts/gen_index.py` all now iterate every deployable mod root. The new mod ships 3
bootstrap behaviors, registered via `data/pulse_registry.yaml` and
implemented in `tv_engineering_department_standalone_on_action.txt`/
`_events.txt`: an `on_game_start` hook queues a one-time intro event
(`tv_engineering_department_standalone.1`, EN/ZH, option "Understood"/"了解了")
for every country with `days = 1`, so it appears one day after game start
without waiting for `monthly_country_pulse`; the option effect creates the
Engineering Department IO for that country and sets a save-wide
`tv_global_variable` marker (`tv_engineering_department_standalone_initialized`,
via `set_global_variable`/`has_global_variable` — EU5's actual global-flag
equivalent, confirmed since this mod had no prior global-flag usage and EU4/CK2-style
`set_global_flag` does not exist in EU5); CMF's `on_game_load` callback schedules
the same event one day after load for every country when that marker is absent
(an old or foreign save), and unconditionally, silently backfills the Engineering Department IO for any
country still missing one via the same idempotent creation effect (a
corrupted save).

**Standalone Court Positions mod (2026-07):** `src_court_positions/`, mirrored
by `scripts_court_positions/`, is an independently deployed Court Positions
mod that depends only on CMF 2.x for the `on_game_load` lifecycle callback.
Its permanent, always-visible Court Positions situation initializes country
state at game start, reload, and situation start. Six country-owned office slots
(Court Physician, Court Tutor, Steward, Court Architect, Cultural Emissary, and
Court Chronicler) store their holders plus cached ability and salary values.
Eligible holders cannot be rulers, heirs, consorts, cabinet members, generals,
admirals, explorers, or other busy characters; appointment grants the native
`busy_modifier`, which is removed on dismissal or holder death. Office effects
are refreshed monthly and scale linearly from the selected character's ADM, DIP,
MIL, or artist skill (artist skill normalized from 0–1 to 0–100). The six
full-skill effects are +5% disease resistance, +10% heir education efficiency,
-5% estate enrichment, -10% building construction cost, +20% cultural influence,
and +0.10 monthly prestige. Each position has both a situation-card selector and
a direct character action; the situation cards use variable-backed portraits and
dynamic `ShowModifierEffect` displays. Monthly salary is
`ability / 100 × (1 + country_economical_base × 0.1)`, using the game's native
Economic Base value. Its shared pulse bridge, singleton character-title and
message-type copies, source index, validation, version stamping, and build step
all participate alongside the main and Engineering Department roots.

The root-level `towards_victory_editor_web/` (wonder-localization tab) now exposes the fixed eight-stage `ceremony` data for every framework-backed unique wonder (all except Pharos Lighthouse and Hagia Sophia): each stage edits its English/Simplified Chinese title and description, a verified vanilla-font `icon`, its flavor rationale, one or two costs, and per-wonder pay/decline option flavor text. Saving ceremony changes validates the canonical schema before writing `data/unique_wonders.yaml`, then regenerates the ceremony effects, events, options Customizable Localization, cost static modifiers, localization, GUI-card fragment, and merged Engineering Department panel in the required order.

Since 2026-07, each ceremony stage's `cost` is `{catalog, type, value}`, drawn from the standalone `data/cost_reward_units.yaml` catalog (`country_reward`/`local_reward`/`character_reward`/`country_modifier`/`local_modifier`) instead of the old ~39-type `STYLE_3_REWARD_EFFECTS`-derived vocabulary; `value` is always computed (never hand-authored) as `-1 × base_value × stage_multiplier` (odd stage → ×1, even → ×2), except the inverted-polarity `country_reward.inflation`. Two new per-stage fields, `option_pay_en/zh` and `option_decline_en/zh`, hold wonder-specific flavor text for the two event options at each stage (previously one generic "Pay the price."/"Not yet." pair shared by all 121 wonders × 8 stages); these are wired through 16 new Customizable Localization dispatch blocks (`scripts_engineering_department/in_game/common/customizable_localization/gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_options.py` → `src_engineering_department/in_game/common/customizable_localization/tv_wonder_ceremony_options.txt`) rather than any change to the ceremony event file itself. The `country_modifier`/`local_modifier` cost categories are applied as 5-year temporary modifiers (`years = 5 mode = add_and_extend`), backed by two data-driven static-modifier generators, `scripts_engineering_department/main_menu/common/static_modifiers/gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cost_country_modifiers.py` and `gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cost_local_modifiers.py`. Since 2026-07 (updated), each emits one modifier per `(wonder, stage)` — not deduped by `(id, tier)` as originally built — so every debuff's `STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_` label reads as that stage's own flavor title (`title_en`/`title_zh`) instead of a generic effect label shared across unrelated wonders; this grew the two generated files to 325 + 242 = 567 total modifier blocks. A new one-time/rerunnable script, `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/merge_ceremony_cost_options.py`, surgically splices authored content into `data/unique_wonders.yaml` via anchored line-based text splicing (not a full YAML round-trip — see `docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md` rule 26 for the full rationale and effect-syntax mapping per cost category).

Engineering Department survey actions rely on selector `none_available_msg_key` handling for empty site lists rather than country-level has-any-site pre-scans.

Academy Philosophy Debate update: domestic Philosophy debate is now generated from `data/philosophy_debates.yaml` plus `data/philosophy_debate_random_events/*.yaml` as an event-driven seat system with a local random-event layer. A local debate has a fixed Crown chair plus five non-Crown debate seats, visible through the Academy panel; monthly country pulse fires a hidden one-day tick event that cleans invalid special seats, decrements 60-month defection cooldowns, applies seated-group progress, independently rolls a 10% seat-change event pool and a 10% direct-progress event pool, and checks debate endpoints. Debate groups cover seven estates, 39 mutually exclusive estate variants (8 driven by literacy/policy/government-type/religion/culture-acceptance, 31 driven by a `societal_value: {axis, pole}` field covering both poles of every societal value axis in the game), artists, foreign powers, and the Great Scientist. Variant mutual exclusion is generated, not random: each variant's `_available_trigger` excludes its base estate and every lower-`id` sibling variant sharing its `base_group` (`variant_condition_lines()`/`emit_variant_exclusion()`/`variants_excluding_estate()` in `philosophy_debate_codegen.py`), so `tv_academy_debate_pick_unseated_*_group_effect`'s random_list only ever receives one eligible candidate among a base estate and its variants for a given country state. Stances come from the data-owned issue table, with foreign powers evaluated dynamically from rivalry, institution adoption, previous conservative/progressive choices, alliances, and opinion. Seated supporters raise progress, opponents lower it, neutral groups do not move progress until events shift their stance. Action triggers from the same YAML react to active local-debate hooks for the current issue across government, art, building completion, finance, exploration, colony, religion, war, peace, and policy activity; current runtime gates by hook and issue while preserving condition labels as generated comments, and every trigger rolls 25% for a clamped +5 or -5 progress shift followed by endpoint checks. The random-event source contributes 198 active monthly local-debate direct-progress events in general and issue-specific pools, generated as `tv_academy_debate.2000` through `tv_academy_debate.2197`; design IDs `G09` and `G12` remain explicitly excluded. Random-event options re-check the local debate guard, apply clamped progress deltas, may shift seated-group stance/cooldowns, affect estates/resources/artists/Chief Scientist/foreign-power prestige, and map temporary semantic country-modifier labels to generated static country modifiers rather than emitting unverified modifier effect names. End results apply winner/loser effects to estates or variants, artists, foreign powers, and Great Scientists before the existing Philosophy acceptance/rejection advancement effects run. World-stage Philosophy issues use a separate always-active `tv_academy_world_debate_situation`: when any Academy country reaches a world debate node, countries with an Academy join as one country-one seat, display their flag, and take support/oppose from their completed domestic result, neutral while local debate is unresolved, or fixed support after embracing the corresponding institution. The world debate strength is `support / (support + oppose)`, progress starts at 50, moves monthly by `(strength_percent - 50) / 100 * 5`, resolves at 0/100 as conservative/progressive, and times out after 120 months as neutral. Resolution mirrors the world result to eligible Academy countries and then applies the normal Philosophy advancement/reward effects.

The 2 unique wonders with real, loadable Unique Wonder Ritual source (Pharos Lighthouse `unique_pharos_lighthouse` id 101, Hagia Sophia `unique_hagia_sophia` id 102) are produced by one unified pipeline, `scripts_engineering_department/gen_unique_wonder_rituals.py`, reading per-wonder content modules under `scripts_engineering_department/unique_wonder_ritual_content/` (`pharos.py`, `hagia.py`). This replaced three previously separate pipelines: the hardcoded `scripts/wonder_unique_rituals/` Pharos/Hagia plugins (merged into shared engineering-department files) plus two standalone hardcoded event generators, `scripts/gen_repeated_row_pilot_wonders.py` + `data/repeated_row_pilot_wonders.yaml` (Dome/Bank/St Peter's generic row-set skeleton), and the Alhambra-only `gen_unique_wonder_ritual_code.py --write-alhambra-source` Harness vertical slice. All are deleted.

Alhambra `unique_alhambra` id 106, Dome of the Rock `unique_dome_of_the_rock` id 103, Bank of Saint George `unique_bank_of_saint_george` id 122, and St. Peter's Basilica `unique_st_peters_basilica` id 112 previously had bespoke rituals implemented through this same pipeline (see risk_cards/wonders.md rules 12-17 for that history) but had their bespoke implementation completely removed and their `data/unique_wonders.yaml` `ritual:` block reverted to the same generic immediate-mode shape used by every other non-bespoke unique wonder. Their `data/unique_wonder_ritual_specs.yaml` design entries remain as `stub`-status historical design documentation (design_ir/node_graph preserved, not deleted) in case a future pass wants to re-implement them.

Pharos and Hagia Sophia keep their existing bespoke mechanics unchanged (ported, not rewritten — they already matched their `data/unique_wonder_ritual_specs.yaml` node_graph with full per-entity/per-step fidelity), now emitting dedicated event, scripted-effect, and scripted-trigger files under `src_engineering_department/in_game/` instead of being merged into the shared `tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_*.txt` files. Their localization stays in its existing correct home (`data/wonder_localization.yaml` -> the shared `tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_l_*.yml` via `gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_l_*.py`).

Alhambra, Dome of the Rock, Bank of Saint George, and St. Peter's Basilica were originally implemented through a shared "entity ritual" engine, `scripts/unique_wonder_ritual_content/_entity_ritual.py` (identical opening/update/retry/resolve stages and `random_list` 60/40 + 80/20 dice for every wonder), then reworked 2026-07 into bespoke mechanics after `scripts/audit_unique_wonder_ritual_mechanic_similarity.py` confirmed the homogenization, then reworked a second time in 2026-07 after the audit found the dice-free versions still shared a higher-level narrative template (see risk_cards/wonders.md rule 13 for the full history of both rewrites, including Alhambra's `pre_winning_war`/`ending_war` war-validation gate). All four implementations, their dedicated per-wonder generated files, their `_entity_ritual.py` engine, and their `data/pulse_registry.yaml`/GUI wiring were subsequently removed in full and the wonders reverted to the generic immediate-mode ritual.

GUI cards for both wonders remain merged into the single shared `src_engineering_department/in_game/gui/panels/organization/tv_engineering_department.gui` panel via the existing `gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py` + `merge_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py` fragment mechanism.

The Harness's source-compiler ceremony (repeated-entity-row preflight/source-plan/readiness-ledger/bundle-gate machinery and the Alhambra "no-write" vertical slice, ~22,700 lines across `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_unique_ritual_harness.py`, plus `scripts/gen_unique_wonder_ritual_code.py` and `scripts/test_unique_wonder_ritual_harness.py`) has been retired: after 40+ commits it never produced loadable source for any of the 123 specs, and its one real generation attempt produced an unrelated generic 8-event skeleton. The design corpus (`design_ir`/`mechanic_signature`/`cadence_signature`/`compiler_gap_ledger` for all 123 specs in `data/unique_wonder_ritual_specs.yaml`) and the spec/node_graph/capability/archetype schema validation (`scripts_engineering_department/wonder_unique_ritual_harness.py`, now ~3,600 lines, still backs `scripts_engineering_department/gen_unique_wonder_ritual_specs.py` and `scripts_engineering_department/audit_unique_wonder_ritual_design_matrix.py`) are unchanged and continue to carry the other 121 unique wonders' design-only work. Implementation for future wonders is hand-written per `docs/guides/Unique_Wonder_Ritual_Harness.md`, gated by `scripts_engineering_department/audit_unique_wonder_ritual_mechanic_similarity.py` (flags `combined_ratio >= 0.15` or shared `random_list` weight-tuple reuse against any other implemented wonder).

All 121 unique wonders other than Pharos and Hagia Sophia carry a `ceremony` block in `data/unique_wonders.yaml`, validated by `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/_core.py` and generated by the shared `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_ceremony_lib.py` pipeline. Construction completion automatically initializes the selected framework ritual in progress at ceremony stage 0 with its quarter counter at 0; it neither spends a confirmation cost nor advances a tick. Three later monthly pulses fire the first of eight shared ceremony events, which charges the stage-specific `ceremony.stages[i].cost` and advances the ritual, or waits for the next quarter. The three reward channels intentionally mirror the matching generic mechanic: stage 2 derives `generic_rituals[mechanic_key].style_3.reward` (including site-scoped entries at `tv_wonder_site`), stage 4 creates the `tv_wonder_{mechanic_key}_ritual_annex` backed by prototype style 2 local modifiers, and stage 8 schedules hidden event `tv_engineering_department.9308`, which calls `tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect` to apply prototype `generic_rituals[mechanic_key].style_1.country_modifier` through the canonical permanent modifier, inauguration, world-news, and finalization chain. The ceremony remains a shared, wonder-id-generic exception to the ritual-similarity audit and excludes Pharos/Hagia's bespoke rituals, whose dedicated manual buttons remain intact. Its cards are merged by `merge_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py`: the outer card is 500px, each of the eight stage cards is 462px, and each badge is a real piechart whose `@icon!` is selected from the active wonder's own `ceremony.stages[i].icon` data and reviewed through `icon_rationale`; active/completed flavor text resolves dynamically with `Localize(Concatenate())`. There is no ready card, shared Hold Ceremony button, or global stage-to-icon fallback.

The Engineering Department wonder layer uses generated data for proposal/site rules, construction modules, final buildings, rituals, display routes, localization, and building-driven Country Auto modifiers. Ritual country effects are separated into real static modifiers granted on ritual completion, while generated display mirrors support GUI `ShowModifierEffect`. The Trade League header/action card, Governor's House regional governors and War Tent plans, IO chief mutual exclusion, and root-level wonder-localization editor remain part of the current gameplay/tooling surface.


Governor's House subject-chain lifecycle: only independent countries may own a Governor's House. When an owner becomes a subject, its owned house is dissolved and owner-only Grand General, Governor, War Tent, and automation state is cleared; player countries see a confirmation popup while AI countries resolve silently. A top overlord's owned Governor's House automatically includes every subject below it as IO members without setting `tv_govhouse_member` on those subject members; the locked `leader_country` receives `tv_govhouse_supreme_overlord_status` (Supreme Overlord / 最高宗主), while every attached subject-chain member receives `tv_govhouse_subject_status` (Subject / 附属). When a former subject becomes independent, Conquest score is recalculated; countries that newly meet Conquest Milestone 1 use the normal milestone reward path, while countries that already had that milestone rebuild only the Governor's House.

Diplomatic Alliance subject-chain lifecycle mirrors the same owner rule while preserving the alliance's ordinary member semantics: only independent countries can establish or restore their own Diplomatic Alliance, and an owned alliance automatically attaches every subject and lower subject as IO members. When an alliance owner becomes or transfers as a subject, its owned alliance is dissolved, owner-only Advocator/member-count/cache state is cleared, and the attached subject chain is removed from external Diplomatic Alliances before the new top overlord's alliance reattaches it. A former owner marked during dissolution rebuilds its Diplomatic Alliance after regaining independence. The leader-country `tv_diplomatic_alliance_member_count` cache is refreshed from the IO's `total_members` at creation, joining, leaving, subject-chain sync, and explicit cleanup points.

Governor's House regional administration lifecycle: `tv_govhouse_region_to_admin_location_map` is a stable country cache from region to the system-designated Local Administration location, not a scan result from existing buildings. Startup/load, ownership changes, capital moves, and Local Administration confirmation maintain the cache; monthly logic does not rebuild it. Non-capital regions select an admin point only when missing or when the old point leaves the country, preferring highest-population city/megalopolis, then town, then rural settlement. Selected locations carry `tv_govhouse_local_admin_owner` and `tv_govhouse_admin_region` markers so ownership-change hooks can delete transferred Local Administration buildings, clear the losing country's map/governor assignment, and let the winner refresh its own cache without inheriting the building. Governor display and modifier maintenance are split from admin-point selection: monthly random governor events read `tv_govhouse_has_valid_governor`, while yearly/law/lifecycle hooks refresh governor modifiers.

Finalization routing update: Engineering Department wonder finalization now dispatches through generated one-event-per-wonder inauguration events and one-event-per-wonder world-news events in `tv_wonder_finalization_events.txt`, with the long locked-wonder `trigger_event_non_silently` branch maintained in the separate `tv_wonder_finalization_effects.txt` generated file. Wonder-type dispatch happens only before the inauguration or broadcast event is fired; each per-wonder inauguration event may branch internally on ceremony style for its description, but no event performs wonder-type dispatch. The visible inauguration and world-news events use static `image = "gfx/interface/icons/towards_victory/wonders/{wonder_image_name}_cropped.dds"` fields, while the shared hidden executor event carries no image. The inauguration option now directly runs the current wonder/style final-building construction effect first, then the tooltip-visible per-wonder reward effect, and only then a light hidden scheduler, `tv_wonder_trigger_finalization_hidden_event_effect`. The scheduler silently fires the shared hidden executor event `tv_engineering_department.6999`; that hidden event's `immediate` calls `tv_wonder_execute_finalization_hidden_event_effect` for cleanup, intermediate destruction, world-news broadcast, finalization-time expansion-candidate registration from current project variables, project-state clear, and the next concept start. This clears `tv_wonder_locked` before the new concept substate appears and avoids same-tick building-level reads after `construct_building instant = yes`. It also keeps the player-facing construction and reward lines visible in the option tooltip while keeping non-tooltip bookkeeping out of high-frequency event-option rendering and keeping all event IDs below EU5's `< 10000` numeric limit.

Wonder ownership-transfer update: completed-wonder province ownership changes are isolated in `tv_wonder_ownership_on_action.txt`, `tv_wonder_ownership_effects.txt`, `tv_wonder_ownership_events.txt`, and `tv_wonder_ownership_l_*`. The centralized `tv_pulse_bridges.txt` bridge delegates `on_location_changed_owner` to both Governor's House and wonder ownership callbacks. `tv_wonder_ownership_handle_location_owner_changed_effect` scans final-building maps, unregisters the losing country's site/wonder priority row, unregisters any stale matching row for the winner, registers a mode-2 expansion row only when the inherited final building can still expand, and refreshes location display/map state. Only `tv_wonder_ownership_trigger_gain_event_effect` and `tv_wonder_ownership_trigger_loss_event_effect` perform per-wonder dispatch so gain/loss events can keep static ids and static wonder images. Ownership gain events inform the new owner that the completed local wonder is now benefiting them; ownership loss events run an `immediate` cleanup that calls `tv_wonder_ownership_compute_loss_retains_same_wonder_effect`, then silently removes that wonder's persistent national ritual or ceremony modifier only when the losing country no longer owns another final building of the same wonder type, with unique-wonder cleanup also clearing the completed-ritual key.

Wonder map-mode update: the `tv_wonder` economy map mode highlights fixed unique wonder sites and completed TV wonders. Location display refresh now maintains `tv_wonder_map_unique_level`, `tv_wonder_map_generic_level`, and `tv_wonder_map_has_potential_unique` on each location: ordinary land with no wonder level and no fixed unique site uses a light-gold baseline, fixed historical unique sites from `data/unique_wonders.yaml` render light red at level 0 when absent at start, game-start historical wonders initialize as completed level 1 unique wonders, completed unique wonders render increasingly saturated green by highest unique level and reach full saturation at level 6, and completed generic wonders render increasingly saturated blue by highest generic level and reach full saturation at level 6. Unique completed wonders take priority over generic completed wonders on the same location. The same startup/save-load world refresh and per-building/finalization location refresh paths that feed the location-window wonder display also keep these map-mode variables current.

Towards Victory adds 6 generalized victory paths to EU5: Conquest, Prosperity, Trade, Diplomatic, Cultural, and Scientific. Each path has 5 milestone nodes, and each milestone offers 3 independent permanent reward modifiers selected through the reward buttons. Reaching a milestone unlocks its reward slot and fires a popup event for notification/unlock-only side effects; permanent buffs are applied only through the generated three-choice reward buttons. Each path's progress is initially gated by `tv_<path>_victory_enabled` and normally unlocks independently only after that path's IO establishment flow is complete; Trade and Diplomatic Victory can also unlock immediately when a country joins another country's Trade League or Diplomatic Alliance. The main victory situation is a generated single-path page with a six-button top switcher backed by the player country variable `tv_victory_selected_path`; disabled paths show a three-step establishment card, while enabled paths show the per-path overview card with a concept-linked title, playstyle bullet, progress-formula bullet, progress and reward cards, and a per-path global top-five leaderboard.

The new Victory Path Tree task layer is data-driven by `data/victory_path_tasks.yaml`. Each enabled path gives human players three task slots between the illustrated node tree and the legacy path overview; enabling a path initializes its tree points to 0 and assigns all three slots, with no initial 1000-point grant and no monthly free-point trickle. All 104 tree nodes have a flat 5-point unlock cost. The current pool contains 77 fixed and objective tasks across the six paths (10 Conquest, 10 Prosperity, 11 Trade, 9 Diplomatic, 13 Cultural, and 24 Scientific). Fixed families snapshot the first threshold strictly above both the last claimed tier and the country's current value, while claimed objective tasks are permanently excluded. Assignment rejects tasks already complete at refresh time, excludes families used by the other two slots, prefers eligible national-special tasks, and otherwise chooses ordinary candidates with equal weight; national-special `x2xx` IDs and current-or-former-tag routing exist, but their candidate pools are intentionally empty for now. Empty slots use task ID 0 and retry during the human monthly pulse.

Task completion is latched in slot state and is claimed through one of 18 fixed Generic Actions. A claim grants exactly 1 point to the matching path's node tree, records the fixed tier or one-time objective completion, and refreshes only that slot; enabling a route refreshes all three slots. Every occupied task card also binds a separate right-click Generic Action: when the human player has at least 10 Prestige, it spends 10 Prestige and immediately refreshes only that card's slot, without awarding a tree point. Cheap state tasks and empty slots are checked only for active human slots on the monthly pulse, technology counts reuse the yearly scan, and ownership/building/institution/war/artwork/organization/MOD actions use targeted callbacks. Monthly task IDs are ordered first in each slot update router. Empty slots on the same path share one candidate-metric refresh pass, skip metrics for fixed families whose final tier is already claimed, reuse the prepared metrics when a task is assigned, and stop trying later empty slots after the first slot finds no eligible candidate; this preserves monthly retry behavior without repeating equivalent location, character, market, organization, or candidate-pool scans up to three times. Expensive cultural and geographic unification checks run only during assignment and ownership-change callbacks. The nine price-event objectives are appended through generated same-ID vanilla event copies; the three global-once objectives (Triangular Trade, European Merchants in China, and Spice Trade) complete for the first eligible holder, fail and refresh competing human holders, and never return to the pool after the global event flag is set. Italian War objectives complete for members of the actual winning alliance before vanilla destroys the alliance state, while losing or drawn holders fail and refresh; Black Death holders complete only if they actually experienced the situation, and other holders fail when it ends. Task failure is silent and never awards tree points.

IO establishment is data-driven by `data/io_establishment.yaml`. Every path uses the same three-step lifecycle: a monthly pulse completes the basic requirement flag, fires a player guidance event, and refreshes the headquarters-built cache; the guidance event or the capital-only `select_trigger` action can start that path's 300-ducat headquarters with themed maintenance goods; headquarters completion is marked from the building `on_construction_ended` hook and fires a second guidance event to appoint the chief; and the appoint-chief action initializes the country leader variable, applies the chief modifier, creates the matching non-unique IO, sets `tv_<path>_victory_enabled = 1`, fires the generated establishment event, and immediately refreshes that path's victory progress. AI countries use the same generated build-HQ and appoint-chief actions through `generic_action_ai_lists`, action-native `ai_tick`, and `ai_will_do`; the monthly pulse no longer simulates those action effects. Diplomatic AI countries complete step 1 at 10 diplomatic reputation like players, but only execute the step-2 headquarters build action at 20 diplomatic reputation or higher while fewer than 10 Diplomatic Alliances already exist worldwide; Trade AI countries complete step 1 at 50 monthly trade income like players, but only execute the step-2 headquarters build action at 100 monthly trade income or higher and while fewer than 10 Trade League leader countries exist globally. The step-3 appoint-chief actions have no extra diplomatic-reputation or trade-income gate once the headquarters exists, though Trade AI still obeys the global Trade League cap.

Engineering Department concept-stage update: Great Engineer proposal refresh now has an explicit `tv_wonder_stage = 0` substate marked by `tv_wonder_concept_in_progress`. Appointing a Great Engineer, refuting/bribing a proposal, or finishing an inaugurated wonder starts `tv_wonder_concept_progress` at 0 on the Engineering Department IO; monthly_change adds Great Engineer ADM x0.2 until it reaches 100, then `tv_engineering_department.30` lets the player acknowledge the completed concept. Only that event option runs proposal reroll work: appointment concepts rebuild/restore the feasible deck and roll three proposal slots, rejection/bribe concepts run the old rejection reroll, and post-finalization concepts roll the next proposals after finalization has already projected the current project variables into the priority candidate cache and cleared the finished project state. Finalization, abandonment, support-collapse aborts, and completed-wonder location owner changes all use the same priority-candidate lifecycle: mode 1 is kept when intermediate/module state remains, mode 2 is kept when an existing, newly finalized, or newly conquered final building still has scale-tier headroom, and stale rows are unregistered when no resume/upgrade state remains. The `on_location_changed_owner` bridge in `tv_pulse_bridges.txt` calls `tv_wonder_ownership_location_changed_owner` for locations with final-building map state, then the ownership handler scans the final-building maps and triggers the ownership events. Construction-stage module/materialization sync does not refresh the proposal priority cache. During active construction, country-owned unit variables are the authoritative progress state; building-to-unit sync runs only on site selection or explicit reinitialization and only raises units from existing helper/module/final buildings, never lowering a unit during monthly construction. The Engineering Department GUI shows the concept text, `@adm!` icon, progress bar, and `xx/100` value while hiding proposal buttons, accept/refute/bribe actions, and wonder previews during this substate.

Wonder-system localization pass (2026-07): the formal in-fiction display name for the `tv_wonder_construction` mechanic is now "Great Project" (English) / "伟大工程" (Simplified Chinese) — a proper noun, replacing the earlier verb-phrased "奇观建设"/"Wonder Construction". Internal `tv_wonder_*` identifiers are unchanged; only localized display text moved. Sibling concept titles were renamed to match: `tv_wonder_survey` → Survey/测绘, `tv_wonder_materials` → Building Materials/建材, `tv_wonder_ceremony` → Ceremony/仪式, `tv_wonder_small/medium/large` → Small/Medium/Large Project ("小型/中型/大型工程"). "奇观"/"Wonder" remains acceptable only as an occasional informal synonym inside narrative flavor prose (event bodies, proposal blurbs), never in titles, labels, or concept names. Two Game Concepts were added to close coverage gaps found during the pass: `tv_wonder_concept` ("Conception"/构想) documents the Great Engineer's ADM-driven proposal-generation meter described above (`tv_wonder_concept_progress`), and `tv_wonder_construction_part` ("Construction Part"/建设部件) documents the four parallel Foundation/Main Structure/Function/Crowning progress tracks whose minimum sets the wonder's level — both were previously explained only in body-text tooltips with no linkable encyclopedia entry. All 123 unique-wonder and 27 generic-wonder `game_concept_..._desc` entries in `data/wonder_localization.yaml` that read as exposed game-design text (e.g. "must be raised at its fixed historical site, follows the same site rules as its generic archetype, and culminates in the X ritual sequence") were rewritten into narrative flavor prose describing what each landmark/archetype actually is, sourced from `data/unique_wonders.yaml`'s per-wonder art `prompt:` field and matched to the tone of the mod's existing event flavor text; the other 27 already-good generic-wonder descriptions were left as-is.

Trade League law update: `data/trade_league_laws.yaml` now drives generated `tv_trade_league_laws.txt`, adding five three-level vote-based laws: Customs Union, Maritime Convoys, Merchant Courts, Staple Monopolies, and Investment Code. Each level-1 policy trades roughly one benefit package for one cost, level 2 gives about 1.5x the same benefits for the same cost, and level 3 gives about 3x the same benefits for two distinct costs. AI policy vote bias uses base weights 200/100/0 for levels 1/2/3; levels 2/3 also scale from opinion of the leader (-50..+50 / -100..+100), leader diplomatic reputation (+2/+5 per point), and leader monthly trade income (+5/+10 per 100 income). Trade League law votes use the Grand Merchant law-change availability trigger and mutually block commercial-intelligence and monopoly maintenance work; Trade Chain maintenance is leader-country state and no longer occupies the Grand Merchant.

The **Conquest Victory** path (征服胜利) is fully implemented: 5 triggers, 15 selectable reward modifiers, 5 events, and localization in English and Simplified Chinese. Score = direct locations + 0.5 × subject-or-below locations; thresholds at 200 / 400 / 600 / 1200 / 2500.

The **Prosperity Victory** path (繁荣胜利) is fully implemented: 5 triggers, 15 selectable reward modifiers, 5 events, and localization in English and Simplified Chinese. Score = (total_population + Σdev + country_tax_base) × 50 / (N + 50) × (1 + stability/100) × (gov_power/100), where N = num_owned_locations and gov_power = legitimacy/republican_tradition/devotion/tribal_cohesion/horde_unity by government type. The 50N/(N+50) effective scale term provides stronger diminishing returns on country size. Updated yearly; thresholds at 2,000 / 3,000 / 5,000 / 7,000 / 11,000 (provisional, calibrate after playtesting).

**Prosperity main mechanic:** Establishing the Prosperity IO creates the **Engineering Department IO** (`tv_engineering_department`), a non-unique founder-only IO with no parliament tab and no automatic leader election. Its appointed character leader is the **Great Engineer**, stored on the leader country as `tv_great_engineer_char` and managed by generated Appoint/Remove/Change leader actions. Wonder Construction implements **Conception, Debate, Survey, Organization, Logistics, Construction, Ceremony, and Finalization** for 54 universal wonder types, all managed through the same generated mechanics layer from `data/wonders.yaml` and `data/wonder_final_buildings.yaml + data/wonder_generic_rituals.yaml + data/wonder_base_modifiers.yaml + data/wonder_site_rules.yaml`: Sacred Mountain Complex through Pirate Port, spanning civil production, military defense, civic timekeeping, arts, diplomacy, law, commerce, finance, industry, maritime trade, civilizational memory, prehistoric ritual, cathedral, monastic, and urban building-cap expansion, civic tower, sculpture-group, coastal-artillery, naval-school, colonial-company, assembly-hall, mobile-fortress, ancient-ruins, summer-palace, public-credit/state-bank, and corsair/privateering-port themes. Appointing a Great Engineer rebuilds the country's feasible proposal deck from owned terrain/city/port/infrastructure/mining/urban-capital conditions, then rolls or restores three simultaneous proposal slots on that engineer. The overview shows three wonder buttons, each labeled by size icon and wonder name; selecting one changes the Great Engineer's short pitch to that wonder's size-aware summary. Accepting locks the currently selected wonder and enters debate, while refuting or bribing refreshes all three proposal slots at once; rejected proposals are removed from the current deck, and if rejection empties the feasible deck the country immediately rebuilds the feasible list and rolls again. Debate uses the Engineering Department IO variable `tv_wonder_domestic_support` (0-200): estate demands for Nobles, Burghers, and Clergy can be accepted for +20 support through `tv_change_wonder_domestic_support_effect`, whose effect localization renders the IO variable change. Refuting or bribing estate demands does not exclude that agenda type. Demand types include the baseline privilege/tax/levy/policy agenda set plus resource-loss demands for 10 Prestige, 7 Stability, 5 Legitimacy, 5% loyalty from the other two estates, 2.5% inflation, one cabinet member, one artist, or one artwork; the cabinet/artist/artwork demands reroll matching slots after execution so two estates cannot keep pointing at a just-consumed scarce target. Monthly debate events can swing support by +/-20, and the debate can end at 100 support. After debate ends, support decays by 1 per month until the wonder is finalized; reaching 0 before finalization automatically declares construction complete if at least one wonder level is complete and the project can enter ceremony, otherwise it forces project collapse. Abandoning or forced collapse applies upper-estate satisfaction penalties, removes the Great Engineer, costs 20 Prestige, and resets wonder project state.

After debate, the player starts a location-select survey through `tv_wonder_start_survey`. Valid sites depend on the locked wonder type: Sacred Mountain uses hills/plateau/mountains; Triumphal Axis uses city/megalopolis; Great Port and Great Lighthouse use ports; Giant Necropolis uses hills or adjacency to city/town; Hydraulic Workshop uses land; Mining City now requires a mineral-producing raw material site (iron, copper, tin, lead, silver, gold, or marble). Fresh survey and construction candidate checks no longer exclude locations that already contain other TV wonder intermediate, module, or final buildings, but they do exclude the same wonder's existing module/helper/final buildings so resume and upgrade projects stay on the saved priority-candidate path. Priority resume/upgrade projects are valid when the saved site still matches the current wonder's base site rules and either has that wonder's module/helper building or has a final building below the location's stored scale tier. Survey progress is the Engineering Department IO variable `tv_wonder_survey_progress`; each monthly tick adds 0.1 times the Great Engineer's MIL and can fire one survey event from a single random roll. The country tracks the current survey site and three visible competence values: `tv_wonder_scale_competence`, `tv_wonder_logistics_competence`, and `tv_wonder_organization_competence`, initialized at 0 when a new survey begins. At survey start, hidden target competence values are precomputed from 25 base competence, each field's random 0-25 roll, the site preference bonus, and up to 10 from the Great Engineer's ADM; the visible competence values then rise by fixed monthly increments until completion. Monthly survey events can still raise or lower one visible competence by 5, or offer resource tradeoffs for +5. Site preference bonuses use: Sacred Mountain topography/vegetation (mountains +15, plateau +7.5, hills +0, forest +10, woods +5); Triumphal Axis development (0-100 maps linearly to 0-20) plus +5 for megalopolis; Great Port and Great Lighthouse harbor capacity (`harbor_suitability`, 0-100% maps linearly to 0-25, capped above 100%); Giant Necropolis hills +15, adjacent city +10 or adjacent town +5; Hydraulic Workshop total local building levels 0-100 maps linearly to 0-25; Mining City development 0-100 maps linearly to 0-20, plus +5 when the mineral site is a rural settlement. The survey card's Suitability Knowledge section always shows the current wonder's country-scoped build/location trigger through the normal trigger condition list; its suitability rows remain visible as placeholders until surveys reveal them, and revealed rows display actual-vs-maximum preference bonus as `?/+x` before the current site survey completes or `+y/+x` after completion. Each fresh completed survey increments the country-scoped `tv_wonder_suitability_revealed` variable map entry keyed by the wonder mechanic id up to that mechanic's row count, so unique wonders and their prototype share reveal progress. Actual suitability row values are stored in the country-scoped `tv_wonder_suitability_actual` map by `mechanic_id * 10 + row`, then mirrored into `tv_wonder_suitability_current_actual_<row>` for the selected wonder's GUI. Completed survey results are stored on the location in `tv_wonder_surveyed`, `tv_wonder_survey_scale_competence`, `tv_wonder_survey_logistics_competence`, `tv_wonder_survey_organization_competence`, `tv_wonder_survey_scale_tier`, and `tv_wonder_survey_suitability_actual` maps keyed by wonder id or suitability composite row id; selecting an already surveyed site restores the stored result instead of restarting progress and does not advance suitability knowledge. Scale competence maps to maximum wonder construction level using thresholds 20/40/60/80/100, with level 6 requiring 100% competence; Organization Competence maps linearly from 0-100% to a 0-20 Wonder Worksite level cap; Logistics Competence maps linearly from 0-100% to a 50%-150% monthly construction progress multiplier. The UI displays these three effects as concise three-line text blocks with the numeric value on the middle line. `tv_wonder_select_construction_site` confirms the completed survey site, grants 0/20/40 domestic support from the average result (<50 / 50-74 / >=75), and unlocks organization, logistics, and construction.

The generated site rules cover all generic wonders: University City excludes rural settlements and favors cities plus local literacy; Sky Dome Grand Temple requires state-religion population and favors monasteries, cathedrals, and higher state-religion share; Giant Tower Temple has no hard site limit and uses the same religious preference profile; River Extension requires a river site and favors development, bridges, and adjacent River Extensions; National Shipyard requires coast and favors harbor suitability plus development; Star Fortress City excludes rural settlements and favors mountains, plateau, forts, and cities; Great Wall excludes cities and favors mountains, plateau, forts, and rural settlements; Giant Armory excludes rural settlements and favors existing armory infrastructure, cities, and development; Library of Nation is capital-only and favors development, cities, and megalopolises; National Bank requires the capital, a city, or a megalopolis and favors capitals, urban rank, and development; Pirate Port requires a port and favors harbor suitability, existing fortifications, and city/megalopolis sites.

Wonder scale rules are size-driven. Small wonders may be built more than once, but their inherited `base_modifiers` country effects may only use `monthly_towards_*` value-movement modifiers. Medium and large wonders cannot start a second fresh same-type project for the same country, while same-site expansion remains allowed, and their base country modifier keys are unrestricted. `buildings.*.final_local`, generic ritual modifiers, and unique ritual/ceremony modifiers are outside this base-effect restriction. Unique wonders use the same size rule as their inherited generic prototype; current unique data also fixes one `location` and one ceremony style, so unique wonders are effectively single-site.

Shared Engineering Department wonder mechanics are now split by data responsibility: `data/wonder_site_rules.yaml` owns site triggers, hidden preference scripts, and derived suitability rows; `data/wonder_base_modifiers.yaml` owns level-scaled country/base effects; `data/wonder_final_buildings.yaml` owns final local modifiers, maintenance, and final-building attributes; `data/wonder_generic_rituals.yaml` owns the three generic ritual branches; and `data/wonder_design_notes.yaml` keeps non-runtime design notes. These files feed final building local effects and maintenance, national base effects excluding Great Power Score, generic ritual data, proposal/survey/index bridges, site-rule dispatch, generated concepts/localization/GUI text, generated ceremony requirement/effect detail panels, finalization bridges, and the generated `tv_wonder_index_effects.txt` variable-map index. `buildings.*.final_local` is the shared authored local-effect map for final wonder buildings; it is not keyed by final branch, so the final-building generator applies the same local-effect package to all three generic final branches, and unique final buildings inherit that prototype package through their strict `base_effect_multiplier = 2`. Final building `modifier` blocks include only the default local culture values, the authored local-effect package, and the wonder's national base effects as display-only data. Actual base national/global effects are generated in `src_engineering_department/in_game/common/auto_modifiers/tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_auto_modifiers.txt` as level-gated Country Auto modifiers. Generated country and location static mirrors support Engineering Department and location-window `ShowModifierEffect` displays through id-based keys; real ritual country effects use existing ritual static modifiers and are granted at ritual completion. All generic wonders use the same three-style ritual framework: style 1 starts a one-year national burden and later grants a weaker permanent blessing static modifier, style 2 constructs a wonder-specific auxiliary annex building with unique local effects and +50% local estate power for the wonder's estate, and style 3 consumes one fixed decoration cost branch (one artwork, `scaled_gold = 5`, or 50 Prestige) for a large one-time reward. Display-only scripted effects feed the ceremony UI's original-style effect tooltip lists; style-2 annex rewards use generated location static modifiers in `src_engineering_department/main_menu/common/static_modifiers/` so the local modifier package can be shown through `add_location_modifier`. Generic ritual confirmation is split across `tv_wonder_confirm_ceremony`, `tv_wonder_confirm_ceremony_scaled_gold`, and `tv_wonder_confirm_ceremony_prestige`; unique wonders still auto-lock style 1 on construction completion, and their normalized `ritual` schema from `data/unique_wonders.yaml` can complete through configured listeners plus `completion_trigger_script`, optional `snapshot_effect_script` / `progress_effect_script`, and branch-specific runtime variables.

`data/unique_wonders.yaml` defines the unique historical wonder layer (123 entries, ids 101-223). Each entry maps to an existing generic wonder through `base_key`, inherits the generic size, site rules, derived suitability rows, buildings, and base country effect shape, then applies a 2x base-effect multiplier, a fixed `location`, an `initial_level` value (0 means absent at game start, 1-6 means constructed at that level), unique concept/name/flavor/history-intro/image prompt, and one unique ritual definition through either the legacy `ceremony` block or the newer `ritual` block. Recent German/HRE additions cover Schoenbrunn Palace in Vienna, the Electoral Conference Hall in Frankfurt, Aachen Cathedral, Nuremberg Imperial Castle, Augsburg Bank, Cologne Cathedral, the Hanseatic Kontor in Hamburg, the Hanseatic Town Hall in Lubeck, Dresden Art Gallery, Munich Royal Brewery, and Heidelberg University; Bank of Saint George and Augsburg Bank now use the National Bank archetype. Recent Maghreb/West Africa additions cover Fez Old City, Kairouan Holy City, Algiers Pirate Port, Sankore University, the Great Mosque of Djenne, and the Walls of Benin; all start constructed at level 1 except Algiers Pirate Port, which starts absent at level 0, and the Walls of Benin intentionally uses the Great Wall archetype because Benin is not a port. Unique wonders with `initial_level > 0` initialize idempotently on startup/save-load after the wonder index rebuild: `tv_wonder_initialize_existing_unique_wonders_effect` checks each fixed location, constructs the style-1 final building if it is absent, raises it to the configured level, and only when the final-building display row is missing writes `tv_wonder_final_building_level_by_type[building_type:<final>] = initial_level`; it does not seed surveyed/competence maps, so expanding a game-start wonder still requires a normal survey, but levels 1-5 are registered as saved expansion priority candidates until their first real survey exists. It does not grant unique ritual country modifiers or one-time rewards. The normalized unique `ritual` schema now supports `listeners`, `snapshot_effect_script`, `progress_effect_script`, `completion_trigger_script`, and `runtime_variables` in addition to the older start/completion hooks, so a single auto-locked ritual can complete on timer expiry, annex completion, war victory, ruler death, country building thresholds, or "N new buildings since the ritual began" baselines without requiring a multi-branch UI. Great Engineer proposal order is: unfinished resume/expand priority projects first, then feasible unique wonders before the generic random deck; proposal-slot removal still excludes already proposed unique wonders from the current reroll deck. Accepting a unique wonder applies +50 initial domestic support after IO progress reset. Unique survey target competence gains +100 Scale, +20 Logistics, and +20 Organization at survey start. When construction completes, unique wonders auto-select and lock ceremony style 1, so the GUI shows their single ritual text and Hold Ceremony action without the three style-choice buttons; that one branch can now still be authored as an immediate resolution, a timed ritual, an auxiliary ritual building flow, or a deferred event-driven ritual that waits on its configured listeners. The Pharos Lighthouse and Hagia Sophia are special deferred unique rituals: Pharos uses a generated route/event chain for harbor lighting, while Hagia Sophia uses the 8-step Justinianic Synaxis chain with role-assignment actions, staged event ids `tv_engineering_department.6301`-`.6308`, the Hagia wonder image on every step event, a clergy endowment privilege, an icon artwork, and a final Constantinople prosperity wait before wonder finalization. The Engineering Department ceremony UI hides generic unique requirement/effect cards for these special branches and shows Pharos/Hagia-specific progress cards instead. `data/wonder_localization.yaml` is the shared source for generated wonder-mechanics localization text. The merged `towards_victory_editor_web/` tool's wonder-localization tab reads/writes that same localization source and exposes structured mechanics editing for site rules/preference templates, base country modifiers, generic/unique rituals, final-building local effects, and each unique wonder's game-start initial level; unique pages show inherited prototype site/suitability data, inherited+doubled prototype local effects read-only, and an auxiliary design tab that lists original unique-ritual designs from `data/unique_wonder_ritual_designs.yaml`, displays Simplified Chinese translations from `data/unique_wonder_ritual_designs_zh.yaml`, and saves per-wonder AI implementation prompts to `data/unique_wonder_ritual_prompts.yaml`. The editor reads `data/wonder_editor_catalog.yaml` for precomputed vanilla country/local modifier options whose static modifiers are applied by vanilla events, plus Style 3 instant reward options generated from vanilla event effects. `scripts/towards_victory_editor.py` is the launcher, while hand-maintained wonder-concept and Engineering Department localization files stay as direct-edit side inputs for keys they still own.

`docs/design/Cost_Reward_Unit_Concepts.md` documents five reusable AI-design concepts, backed by the standalone, foundational, mechanic-agnostic `data/cost_reward_units.yaml` catalog. Its five top-level lists use `id`/`value`/`loc` entries. The three one-shot reward lists are `country_reward` (14 entries), `local_reward` (3), and `character_reward` (4); their values are positive and a cost is the matching reward's negated value, except that `inflation` has inverted polarity.

The persistent modifier lists use real EU5 modifier keys, distinct from the reward vocabulary. `country_modifier` now has 395 entries: 393 numeric per-level values and two non-scaling boolean unlocks stored directly as YAML `value: true`/`value: false`. It retains the 40 numeric entries extracted from `data/wonder_base_modifiers.yaml` and adds 353 numeric keys from current vanilla-supported `Country` keys in reference mod `3599735023` country blocks. Each added numeric key first resolves its vanilla script value, then uses the smallest nonzero absolute value across its source occurrences; modifier types introduced by the reference mod itself, including administrative-innovation-point types, are excluded. The 34 `monthly_towards_*` axes remain aggregated as one `monthly_towards_axis` entry, and the catalog includes `imperial_authority_modifier`. `local_modifier` remains a 43-entry numeric per-level catalog derived from `data/wonder_final_buildings.yaml`'s `final_local` values plus the universal `local_cultural_tradition`/`local_cultural_influence` baseline. The full catalog totals 459 entries.

Numeric modifier values may be negative when a negative direction is beneficial; the two country boolean values are flat switches and must never scale with level. The catalog is maintained independently, but as of 2026-07 has its first real consumer: the Unique Wonder Ceremony framework's per-stage `cost` (see above), which negates each entry's value for a cost and, for the two modifier categories, applies it as a 5-year temporary modifier rather than feeding back into the wonder data the catalog itself was derived from. The merged `towards_victory_editor_web/` tool's cost/reward tab (launched through `scripts/towards_victory_editor.py`, default port 8760) exposes all five categories. It edits numeric `value` fields and the two country unlock rows' YAML `value: true`/`value: false` switch values; numeric modifier values must be nonzero, while boolean values are valid only for `country_modifier`. It has no generator/regeneration step and remains independent of the wonder-localization tab, which owns live wonder data.

The wonder system's own per-mechanic modifier data (`data/wonder_base_modifiers.yaml` and `data/wonder_final_buildings.yaml`'s `final_local`) remains live and does not feed from this catalog. Separately, `data/wonder_construction_events.yaml` `non_engineering_tokens` carry an explicit `value` field alongside `weight`, matching `engineering_tokens`. Per-token magnitudes now read from that field, with formatting centralized in `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_construction_event_lib.py`'s `format_noneng_magnitude`; this independent wonder-side workflow is not edited by the cost/reward tab.

`docs/design/Cost_Reward_Unit_Concepts.md` section 6 documents a sixth concept, a **Task pool**, backed by a sibling standalone catalog `data/task_pool.yaml` (no reward stored, no generator; edited through two additional tabs in the same `towards_victory_editor_web/` cost/reward tab as the five `cost_reward_units.yaml` tabs). It has two top-level lists, 48 entries each after a bolder second wave doubled the initial 24: `on_action_task` (`id`/`on_action`/`wired`/`scope`/`category`/`completion_note`/`loc`, optionally `verify_in_game`) covering completion detected via a real vanilla EU5 on_action callback verified against `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/on_action/`, and `trigger_task` (`id`/`trigger`/`scope`/`comparison`/`representative_threshold`/`category`/`loc`, optionally `requires_target`/`verify_in_game`) covering completion detected by polling a real vanilla trigger verified against `reference_official_defines/docs/triggers.log` from the shared `monthly_country_pulse` dispatcher. Each `on_action_task` entry's `wired` flag records whether that on_action already has a bridge in `data/pulse_registry.yaml`/`tv_pulse_bridges.txt` (19 of 48 do, inherited from existing TV systems) or still needs a new bridge entry plus a `gen_tv_pulse_registry.py` run (29 of 48); `trigger_task` thresholds are illustrative "1 unit"-style placeholders for future playtesting, not tuned milestone tiers, and three deliberately mirror an existing victory path's own core metric (`monthly_trade_income`/Trade, `num_locations`/Conquest, `num_of_advances_researched`/Scientific) for cross-checking. The second wave reaches into character-scope, IO-scope, and unit-scope on_actions/triggers and percentage-of-maximum triggers; `requires_target` flags trigger entries needing an extra id parameter (culture/religion/reform/country) the catalog can't pin down generically, and `verify_in_game: true` flags entries (10 on_action, 5 trigger) whose task framing or scope resolution is design-complete but not yet confirmed by loading the game. Neither list is read by any system yet, matching the independence convention of the sibling `cost_reward_units.yaml` catalog.

The three previously-separate web editor tools (cost/reward, victory tree planner, wonder localization) are merged into one package, `towards_victory_editor_web/` (launched through `scripts/towards_victory_editor.py`, default port 8760), replacing the three standalone packages/ports/launchers entirely. Each tool's service class (`CostRewardEditorService`/`VictoryTreePlannerService`/`WonderLocalizationService`) and frontend business logic (`static/cost_reward.js`/`victory_tree.js`/`wonder_localization.js`, each still an independent ES module) moved over essentially unchanged; what actually merged: routes are namespaced (`/api/cost-reward/*`, `/api/victory-tree/*`, `/api/wonder-localization/*`, plus the unchanged `/tree-previews/*`/`/wonder-images/*` static mounts) instead of all three colliding on `/api/bootstrap`; the identical per-route `KeyError/ValueError/RuntimeError/Exception` exception-wrapping boilerplate that used to be repeated in every route is now one set of FastAPI exception handlers registered once in `server.py`; the three services' independently-duplicated bounded log-buffer implementations are now one shared `RollingLog` class in `services/common.py`; and the frontend is one shared page (`static/index.html`) with a tab bar showing/hiding three `<section>`s (no iframes) — the only real cross-tool collisions found (4 duplicate DOM ids between cost_reward and victory_tree: `tabs`/`save-btn`/`reload-btn`/`log`, now `cr-`/`vt-`-prefixed; CSS bare-selector clashes, now resolved via each tool's stylesheet being wrapped in `@scope (.tool-<name>)`; and wonder_localization's global Ctrl/Cmd+S keydown shortcut, now guarded to only fire while its tab is active). `WonderLocalizationService`'s constructor eagerly validates `data/wonder_localization.yaml`'s localization completeness and raises if keys are missing; `server.py` isolates that failure so only the wonder-localization tab shows a 503 instead of the whole app failing to start. `towards_victory_editor_web --check` runs all three tools' existing `build_check_report()` in sequence (via `services/common.py`'s `safe_check`, which normalizes wonder_localization's raise-on-failure style into the same `[FAIL]`-line format the other two already use), grouped by tool name.

`docs/design/Victory_Path_Tree_Variant_Design.md` records the reward-tree redesign for all six victory paths, which now runs alongside the live linear five-milestone/three-choice-reward model in `data/victory_paths.yaml`: a 5-node trunk plus 5 branches per path (branches 1-2 fork after trunk node 1, branches 3-4 after trunk node 2, branch 5 after trunk node 3; each branch then runs its own private chain of 2-4 further nodes), data-sourced from `data/victory_path_tree_variant.yaml` (trunk/branch node ids, `attach_after` fork points, effect name/value/cost per node — independent of `victory_paths.yaml`). Node screen positions are authored once via the merged `towards_victory_editor_web/` tool's victory-tree tab (launched through `scripts/towards_victory_editor.py`, default port 8760; decodes the six `.dds` victory-tree background art files under `src/main_menu/gfx/interface/icons/towards_victory/victory_trees/` to PNG previews in `data/generated_tree_previews/`, gitignored) and saved to `data/victory_tree_node_positions.yaml`. The tree overlay is now live inside `tv_victory_situation.gui`, generated by `scripts/in_game/gui/panels/situation/gen_tv_victory_situation_gui.py` + the shared `scripts/victory_tree_node_codegen.py`: each node renders one of three states — dark (locked, prerequisite not yet unlocked), bright (selectable or already unlocked), or bright with an extra outer path-tinted halo ring (already unlocked), reusing the same ring-icon halo style as the linear model's reward buttons. Clicking an eligible node's action_button only stages it (`tv_tree_select_{path}_{node}` sets the shared `tv_tree_pending_id` country variable, re-checking the same prerequisite/cost `allow` gate); a shared confirmation overlay then appears above whatever tab is active, showing that node's title/cost/prerequisite text with Confirm (`tv_tree_confirm_pending`, dispatches via an id-keyed `if`/`else_if` chain to the node's unlock effect) and Cancel (`tv_tree_cancel_pending`) buttons. The tree's own per-node unlock effects/modifiers/points economy remain placeholder-balanced (see `data/victory_path_tree_variant.yaml`'s header notes) and are not yet wired to replace the linear model as the actual victory-completion path.

Pharos Lighthouse (`unique_pharos_lighthouse`) is the first bespoke unique-wonder ritual implemented as a dedicated event chain. Its ritual starts by constructing a port-style auxiliary annex at Alexandria, then advances through quarterly checks that first suppress hostile privateers in Alexandria's sea-zone area and then certify eight Mediterranean route nodes through direct control, existing fleet-basing rights, or a paid bidirectional fleet-basing-rights agreement. The Engineering Department ceremony GUI switches only for this wonder: before the annex starts it shows a single flavor build/light action, during the privateer phase it shows a threat piechart, and during the route phase it shows an 8-node route ledger with location, owner, and Control/Basing/Unheld status rows.

Wonder construction currently relies on site-only `tv_wonder_labor_camp` support buildings plus the `tv_wonder_materials` market good. Monthly construction ticks recompute nominal and effective worksite levels, split the visible monthly progress into current worksite scale, missing labor, material input shortage, and logistics suitability adjustment, and store `tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress` as the final post-logistics rate; construction random events run from the generated 212-event monthly deck while `tv_wonder_stage = 3`. Each construction unit uses the IO variable `tv_wonder_construction_progress`; small/medium/large wonders require 100,000/200,000/300,000 progress per unit. Completing units materializes local module buildings, raises `tv_wonder_level` when all four parts catch up, and unlocks ceremony once at least one level is complete. Generic wonders share a generated three-style ritual structure: style 1 applies a one-year negative burden and later grants a permanent blessing static modifier, style 2 constructs a wonder-specific ritual annex building with local effects, and style 3 pays a fixed decoration cost for an immediate reward. Unique wonders use one auto-locked ritual branch through the same normalized execution layer and grant their ceremony static modifier when that branch completes. Finalization triggers the visible inauguration event, constructs or upgrades the matching local final wonder building at the selected site, stores the final-building level for display, grants non-modifier completion rewards and completion celebrations, refreshes location display state, broadcasts world news, clears project variables, and stops support decay. Finalized wonder buildings have `max_levels = 6`, cannot be destroyed, hide normal player construction behind `country_potential = { always = no }`, and carry designed local effects plus display-only national base effects in their `modifier` blocks. Actual base country effects are generated Country Auto modifiers whose `potential_trigger` checks owned final-building level; actual ritual country effects are existing static modifiers applied by the ritual completion path. Support and module buildings can still carry their own local effects. When wonder construction terminates in any form, completed survey data, final buildings, leftover module buildings, and completed annex buildings persist. Future Great Engineer agendas prioritize half-built wonders, extra modules, and finalized wonders below their stored site cap; upgrades reuse the previous ceremony style and can claim completion rewards again.

Engineering Department UI staging note: later-stage cards do not disappear while prerequisites are incomplete. Survey, Organization/Logistics, Construction, and Ceremony each show a locked informational card until the previous stage is complete. The header shows active IO construction progress when a unit is being built. The Construction/Ceremony tab includes a resume/upgrade hint, a unified active-unit progress bar, remaining-time text, and percent text. Generic ceremony style buttons behave as a toggle with the selected style shown in its pressed state; upgrade projects hide ceremony choices and show a locked-ceremony note. The detail panel shows only that branch's ritual requirement and wonder extra effect. The 121 shared unique ceremonies start automatically at stage 0 when construction finishes, then advance through their three-month event cadence; they show neither a ready card nor a shared Hold Ceremony action. Pharos and Hagia Sophia retain their dedicated manual controls.

The **Trade Victory** path (贸易胜利) is fully implemented with a Trade League layer: 5 triggers, 15 selectable reward modifiers, 5 events, and localization in English and Simplified Chinese. Score = monthly_trade_income (snapshot taken monthly by `tv_update_trade_score_effect`); thresholds at 100 / 200 / 400 / 1000 / 2000. M3 and M5 additionally require the country to be the strongest trader in at least 5 / 10 markets. **Trade League IO** (`tv_trade_league`): non-unique IO created by the Trade IO establishment flow; the founder is added as a member, receives `tv_trade_league_member`, and remains the locked leader country (`leader_change_trigger_type = none`, `leader_change_method = none`). The appointed **Grand Merchant** is stored on the leader country as `tv_grand_merchant_char` and managed by generated Appoint/Remove/Change leader actions. The leader can invite countries through `tv_invite_to_trade_league` (base AI acceptance -200, opinion scaling 0.5, republic +20, merchant republic +30, <=5 locations +20, leader diplomatic reputation x2, leader monthly trade income / 10) and expel non-leader members through `tv_expel_trade_league_member`. The `tv_trade_league_member_status` special status gives non-leader members `global_merchant_capacity_modifier = -0.10`, while the leader gains `global_merchant_capacity_modifier = 0.02` per non-leader member through `leader_modifier`. `tv_trade_league_laws.txt` adds five generated three-level vote-based law groups, but the generated IO definition starts with no enacted laws; the policies are enacted later through votes.

Trade League monopoly tracking and actions are generated from the pure goods list in `data/trade_league_goods.yaml`, with Trade Chain configuration in `data/trade_chain.yaml`. Per good, the final monopoly percentage, monopoly type, stage, origin/transit/consumer control percentages, representative market detail variables, persistent virtual-demand, virtual-supply, embargo state, and Trade Chain bargaining demand state are stored on the Trade League `leader_country`, not in the IO type `variables = {}` block; initializing thousands of IO variables caused severe in-game stutter. The generated Trade League IO type also avoids an IO `monthly_effect`; Trade League maintenance is routed through registered monthly and yearly country pulses in `data/pulse_registry.yaml` plus the manual `tv_trade_league_on_action.txt` bridge. Grand Merchant work is mutually exclusive through `tv_trade_league_triggers.txt`: commercial intelligence (`tv_trade_commercial_intelligence_active`), active `resolution:policy_vote`, and monopoly maintenance availability block one another as appropriate. Trade Chain is no longer a Grand Merchant busy state. When none of those implemented busy states is active, `tv_trade_league_assist_caravans_modifier` applies as a leader-country Country Auto modifier for +10% merchant maintenance efficiency and is shown as the header action-card fallback; the planned promote-monopoly action has no display state yet and is not surfaced.

Trade Chain is a 20-slot persistent leader-country market chain. Canonical state uses `tv_trade_chain_market_by_slot` as a variable map keyed by slot number with market-scope values, plus the ordered `tv_trade_chain_markets` variable list for GUI iteration; `tv_trade_chain_count` includes the capital node. Slot 1 is always the current capital market. `tv_trade_add_chain_node` selects one market at a time; the selector and final effect require trade range, no duplicate existing chain node, and for the next node's market center to be strictly closer to the previous node's market center than to any other existing chain node's center. Creation, monthly maintenance, and capital-change handling ensure or rebuild slot 1 from the current capital market. The `on_capital_moved` bridge resets the chain only when the old and new capital markets differ, clears old permanent temporary bargaining demands, and notifies human players through `tv_trade_league_chain.1`. Monthly maintenance computes `flow = n / Σ(1 / market_capacity)`, sets flow to 0 if any chain market has non-positive leader capacity, measures endpoint distance from the capital market center to the terminal market center, and stores `strength = floor(length × flow / 1000)`. Strength at 100 grants `tv_trade_chain_medium_modifier` (+10% merchant maintenance efficiency); strength at 1000 also grants `tv_trade_chain_strong_modifier` (+20% trade advantage). Each point of strength contributes 0.01% display-only `tv_trade_bargaining_efficiency`; the GUI dynamically resolves `ShowModifierEffect(Concatenate('tv_trade_chain_strength_display_', strength_display))` against generated `tv_trade_chain_strength_display_0..20000` static modifiers instead of emitting one display widget per strength. Each month the chain clears old permanent temporary `tv_trade_chain_bargaining_demand_<good>` demands, then clears and rebuilds `tv_trade_chain_demand_slot_1..20` variable maps keyed by `goods:<id>` scope. From back to front it records exports from node n to node n-1 and adds `trade_volume × strength × 0.0001` demand to every preceding node 1..n-1 for that good.

Monopoly tracking uses an annual global good refresh, yearly per-league full recalculation, and monthly slot-only recalculation. The annual global refresh writes keyed `global_variable_map` entries for each good index: global production, global consumption, representative origin/transit/consumer markets, representative static display metrics, and effective-market denominators. It also maintains three global lists per good: origin-effective markets whose production is at least 20% of the highest production market, transit-effective markets whose export is at least 20% of the highest export market, and consumer-effective markets whose demand is at least 20% of the highest demand market; zero-value markets are excluded. Origin and transit denominators are total export across their effective lists, and the consumer denominator is total import across its effective list. Representative markets are only detail-page decision support. The yearly Trade League pulse refreshes the global cache once per year through a global version/cooldown, then each eligible Trade League leader that has not seen that version recomputes every tracked good and refills monopoly slots. The monthly pulse recomputes only goods already stored in unlocked or previously-filled monopoly slots, using `every_in_global_list` over the effective market lists rather than scanning every world market. Origin and transit control divide member export from effective markets by the cached effective total export; consumer control divides member import from effective markets by the cached effective total import.
