The final `tv_trade_monopoly_level_pct_<good>` is the maximum of those three percentages, capped at 100, with `tv_trade_monopoly_type_<good>` set to 1 origin, 2 transit, or 3 consumer. `tv_trade_monopoly_stage_<good>` is 0 none, 1 potential at 25%, 2 low at 50%, 3 high at 75%, and 4 complete at 100%. Annual full recalculation auto-fills monopoly slots from goods at or above 25% by final percentage descending, with YAML order as the stable tie-breaker; monthly refresh updates the existing slot values but does not reselect goods. The Trade League has 1 slot by default and 2 slots once membership reaches 10; goods outside unlocked slots are not visible and provide no monopoly action benefit. `tv_trade_monopoly_<good>` is set only when the good is in an unlocked slot and has at least 50% final monopoly progress.

The monopoly/action layer generates two slot-select actions plus one fixed selected-good action set: set virtual demand or virtual production through market + value `select_trigger`s, increase/decrease/cancel either virtual action, set an embargo through market + country `select_trigger`s, cancel the embargo, and add one node to the persistent Trade Chain. Those fixed actions dispatch by `tv_trade_selected_good` into generated per-good script branches where needed, so the GUI does not instantiate per-good buttons. Each point of virtual demand or production locks 5% monopoly level, while each embargo locks 30%. Virtual demand uses generated `goods_demand` entries and is applied as `add_temporary_demand` with `months = -1`; set/increase/decrease/cancel actions remove and reapply it as needed, monthly monopoly maintenance removes it when the monopoly is lost, and the Trade League monthly fallback removes all virtual demand while the Grand Merchant cannot maintain monopolies. Virtual production tracks `tv_trade_virtual_supply_applied_<good>`, applies `add_goods_supply` once when active, removes it by applying a negative `add_goods_supply` amount before changing/canceling/suspending, and reapplies it when maintenance conditions recover. Embargo applies a 2-month `merchant_power_severe_penalty` through `add_merchant_power`. Action variables persist on the leader country when the monopoly is lost, but effects are applied only while `tv_trade_monopoly_<good> >= 1`. Every generated action is also registered in `tv_trade_league_list.txt` and `messagetypes.txt`. The Trade League overview shows a generated strength card with distance/flow/strength values and one dynamic `ShowModifierEffect(Concatenate('tv_trade_chain_strength_display_', ...))` row, followed by chain cards: the capital market card first, existing node cards in order, and one add-node card while count is below 20. The generated Trade League custom tab shows one rectangular slot card per unlocked monopoly slot, each with a progress piechart and centered good icon, good/stage labels, final percentage and origin/transit/consumer monopoly type, plus origin, transit network, and consumer percentage rows. Selecting a filled slot refreshes the existing projected detail card and leader-only fixed action card for the selected good; the detail card keeps representative origin/transit/consumer market tables for decision support.

The **Diplomatic Victory** path (外交胜利) is fully implemented: 5 triggers, 15 selectable reward modifiers, 5 milestone events, and localization in English and Simplified Chinese. M1 requires 1 DVP after the route has been enabled. The founder's non-unique Diplomatic Alliance IO is created by the Diplomatic IO establishment flow; founding or joining a Diplomatic Alliance enables Diplomatic Victory progress but does not grant any DVP by itself. Joining another country's Diplomatic Alliance immediately marks the joining country as an alliance member, initializes DVP to 0 if needed, refreshes progress, and runs the diplomatic milestone check for ordinary non-leader members.

Diplomatic score = `tv_diplomatic_victory_points` from monthly alliance-tier maintenance after Diplomatic Alliance membership; royal marriages, war wins, alliance founding, and alliance joining do not directly award DVP. Thresholds are 1 / 50 / 500 / 2,500 / 15,000. **Diplomatic Alliance IO** (`tv_diplomatic_alliance`): non-unique IO using the same character-leader-with-appointment pattern as the Arts Exhibition and Academy of Sciences IOs (`tv_diplomatic_alliance_leader_char` country variable; `leader_change_trigger_type = none`; leader titled Advocator). This IO has a custom `organization_panel` with HRE-style header (tier piechart at 220,60; cohesion piechart at 294,60; leader country flag), a parliament tab (`tv_alliance_assembly` parliament type, `uses_parliament_for_law_votes = yes`) for the 9-law system and normal parliament issues, and a custom Alliance Pillars tab. The tab reads the enacted `tv_alliance_pillar` policy directly and shows No Pillar while the law is empty. Its three mutually exclusive, independently implemented member-action cards are Sublime Empire (`tv_alliance_sublime_empire_subjugate_member`, retaining the 5-year tenure gate and `10 + relative_strength × 50` cohesion cost), Cultural Union (`tv_alliance_cultural_union_enforce_culture`, calling vanilla `enforce_culture_effect`), and Religious Congress (`tv_alliance_religious_congress_enforce_religion`, calling vanilla `enforce_religion_effect`). The IO has two IO-native variables: `tv_alliance_tier` (max 21, maintained as the sum of current reform-policy tier contributions and used as monthly DVP output: leader country gains full tier, non-leader members gain half tier) and `tv_alliance_cohesion` (max 100). Cohesion `monthly_change` always uses base +0.1, leader diplomatic reputation × 0.02, average non-leader member opinion of the leader × 0.002, internal peace +0.2, and appointed Advocator DIP × 0.002. It then applies exactly one enacted-pillar penalty: Sublime Empire uses `-0.2 ×` the strongest non-leader member's relative military strength; Cultural Union uses `-0.2 ×` the highest non-leader member culture's Cultural Tradition divided by leader-culture Cultural Influence; Religious Congress uses `-0.2 ×` the strongest non-leader member liturgical-language power divided by the leader's liturgical-language power. The leader country also receives a monthly `tv_alliance_strongest_member` scope variable for the strongest non-leader member in each led alliance by military strength; War Law AI uses that cache for common defense/offense votes, while leader-duty AI compares relative strength against the Advocator. Diplomatic Alliance invitations use country-interaction acceptance with current alliance cohesion mapped from 0-100 cohesion to +0-20 acceptance, alliance size × -5, an ally-of-Advocator bonus of +10, recipient location and diplomatic-reputation reluctance, same-religion bonus, and actor diplomatic-reputation bonus. The Advocator and all ordinary members receive implemented special statuses with parliament vote power; ordinary members use `tv_alliance_member_status`, which allows voting and member-status agenda/bribe demands.

Diplomatic Alliance invitation acceptance additionally includes the inviter's military strength relative to the recipient, mapped as `(relative strength - 0.5) × 50`.

Diplomatic Alliance laws note: the eight reform laws are seeded on creation to their baseline no-effect policies (`tv_adhoc_envoys_policy`, `tv_royal_couriers_policy`, `tv_non_piracy_policy`, `tv_cuius_regio_policy`, `tv_free_colonization_policy`, `tv_no_common_defense_policy`, `tv_no_common_offense_policy`, and `tv_no_leader_war_obligation_policy`), while the ninth `tv_alliance_pillar` law is deliberately omitted from the initial mapping and therefore starts with no policy. The pillar law sits alone in the `tv_alliance_pillar_laws` category and offers Sublime Empire, Cultural Union, and Religious Congress; these policies have no direct modifier/effect payload and use `on_activate` custom tooltips to describe their separately implemented mechanics. Only the alliance leader can initiate policy votes, but reforms still resolve through the IO parliament. During law votes the Diplomatic Alliance panel overrides the vanilla vote card area with an HRE-style three-column layout: supporting countries, countries that have not voted, and opposing countries, each showing vote strength and country rows; the supporting and opposing rows keep the IO parliament bribe buttons. The eight reform laws keep adjacent-only policy changes: the baseline policy counts as tier contribution 0, and level N can be selected only from current level N-1 or N+1. Pillar choices are not reform tiers and can be selected directly. The five legacy law groups are 4-level reforms with tier contribution 0/1/2/3; War Laws are 3-level reforms with tier contribution 0/1/2 and cover common defensive calls, common offensive calls, and the Advocator's personal war obligation. Conditional common war policies are locked until `tv_joint_denunciation_policy` or `tv_universal_council_policy` is active, then auto-call only when `scope:target` is heretic or heathen relative to the member calling under those laws; unconditional level-3 policies use direct always-join/call blocks. Legacy law AI vote weight scales by reform level: base 0/-100/-200/-300, opinion of the leader at 0/0.25/0.5/0.75 with tiered clamps, and leader diplomatic reputation x0/5/10/15. War-law AI uses relative military strength formulas against the monthly strongest-member cache for common defense/offense and against the Advocator for leader-duty votes. AI proposal/vote math may use `scope:recipient` because the documented policy AI scope gives recipient = IO, but generated blocks guard direct recipient reads with `exists = scope:recipient` for pre-evaluation safety; policy `allow` / `on_activate` / `on_deactivate` use the current IO root directly. Normal Diplomatic Alliance parliament issues are leader-selectable and grant all members mixed timed modifiers, while the associated `pa_tv_alliance_*` agendas let member-status delegates demand subsidies, archive access, or cohesion concessions through the parliament's native agenda/bribe flow.

The **Cultural Victory** path (文化胜利) is fully implemented with an Arts Exhibition layer: 5 triggers, 15 selectable reward modifiers, 5 events, and localization in English and Simplified Chinese. Score = `tv_cultural_influence_points` (CIP) accumulated via `on_work_of_art_created` (+3 CIP, scope `root.owner`) and `monthly_country_pulse` (+1 CIP per month); thresholds at 50 / 200 / 400 / 800 / 1600. M2–M5 also require `tv_arts_intl_influence` ≥ 25 / 50 / 75 / 100 respectively. **Arts Exhibition IO** (`tv_arts_exhibition`): non-unique IO; each country creates its own instance through the Cultural IO establishment flow via `create_international_organization` with `set_leader_country = prev`. `tv_arts_intl_influence` (International Influence) is an **IO type variable** (`variables = { }` block) with `min = 0 / max = 100 / start = 0`, automatic `monthly_change` decay of −0.05/month (shown in the IO panel's standard `ios_information_header` piechart with `IOVariableTooltip` monthly breakdown). All effect sites modify it via `every_international_organizations_member_of = { limit = { international_organization_type = international_organization_type:tv_arts_exhibition } change_variable = { ... } }`. M2–M5 milestone triggers use `any_international_organizations_member_of = { ... var:tv_arts_intl_influence >= N }` from country scope. **IO leader = a court artist** (`leader_type = character`, manually appointed via Appoint/Remove/Change Leader actions in `tv_io_leader_actions.txt`; leader character stored on leader_country as `tv_arts_exhibition_leader_char`; IO `leader = { leader_country ?= { var:tv_arts_exhibition_leader_char ?= { add_to_list = leaders } } }` with `leader_change_trigger_type = none`). The appointed leader's `artist_skill × 0.002` is added to `tv_arts_intl_influence.monthly_change` (via `leader_country.var:tv_arts_exhibition_leader_char.artist_skill` chained access), and busy artists cannot be appointed. The IO panel header shows the leader character automatically (standard vanilla character-leader IO header); no custom portrait slot is needed.

**International artist tours are artist-initiated.** On the January/April/July/October monthly country pulses, each living non-busy artist in a country that currently belongs to an Arts Exhibition IO independently receives a 1% proposal roll when at least one work they created is still owned by that country. A valid neighboring country and one of its owned locations are randomly locked before event `tv_arts_exhibition.10`; every qualifying work by that artist is captured in a pending artwork list. The country may allow the tour, fund it for `scaled_gold = 1.0` (adding the marker-only `tv_funded_artist_tour_modifier` for this tour), or forbid it, which retires/kills the artist while leaving the works at home. AI always allows valid proposals. Starting a tour transfers every still-valid locked artwork to the selected foreign location and host owner, moves the artist to the host, applies permanent `busy_modifier`, and assigns an equally weighted 1–10 year duration. If the traveler was Chief Artist, the appointment is cleared and the existing vacancy event fires. Multiple artists may tour in the same host country; there is no host-country occupancy marker or cap. Each active tour still reduces the home country's `num_possible_artists` cap by 1 through `tv_touring_penalty_N`, while `tv_dispatched_artists_count` and `tv_dispatched_artists_list` continue to drive the IO count display.

**Tour events and endings:** Standard touring events use a 3% roll per artist per month. The unremarkable outcome has been removed; after the roll succeeds, low/mid/high artist-skill bands respectively use weights 20/40/60 for great success (+0.5 International Influence), 60/40/20 for terrible (−0.1), 1/3/5 for artist hired (+1.0 permanently), and 19/17/15 for artistic inspiration (immediate +5 artistic skill). Each skill band's weights total 100. The five parallel monthly special events remain 1/300 per artist per month each with once-per-tour guards: 201 Cultural Diplomacy, 202 Exhibition Success, 203 Noble Patron, 204 Artist Exchange, and 205 Art Theft + Artist Death. Natural completion first returns every surviving tracked carried artwork to the home capital and restores home ownership, then returns the artist and selects the unchanged 90% plain return / 2% × five special return outcomes (.120–.124). Foreign-court hiring, overseas death through the character-death hook, and event .205 do not return carried artworks; surviving works remain owned by the host abroad, while .205 keeps its independent random `destroy_art` effect. Every completion, interruption, hiring, and death route clears `busy_modifier`, the funded marker, tour/pending artwork lists, timers, event flags, dispatch count/list state, and the capacity penalty.

**Current local exhibition mechanic:** The old one-click domestic exhibition has been replaced by a persistent local exhibition system. `tv_host_domestic_exhibition` requires at least 5 owned artworks, costs `scaled_gold = 3.5`, and uses a location select trigger. The chosen location receives the event-only `tv_local_arts_exhibition` building (nobles, employment 0.05, cultural category, heavy art-goods maintenance, `local_migration_attraction = 2`, `local_production_efficiency = -0.2`, `local_unrest = -0.2`), is stored as `tv_local_exhibition_primary_location`, and is added to the `tv_local_exhibition_locations` variable list. The Chief Artist present at start is stored as `tv_local_exhibition_host_artist` and receives `busy_modifier` until the exhibition ends or that character dies. Accepted foreign guest artists likewise receive `busy_modifier` until they return, the exhibition ends, or they die. The IO variable `tv_local_exhibition_enthusiasm` starts at 80, caps at 200, decays by 8/month while active, and ends the exhibition at 0; the country tracks active state, active location list/count, committed exhibits, duration, and rating, with no post-exhibition cooldown. The player expands the exhibition with `tv_local_exhibition_expand` (`scaled_gold = 1.0`, no cooldown): the next exhibit tranche is committed using the old escalating requirements (5, then 10, then 20), the player selects an owned location adjacent to any active exhibition location, the special building is constructed there, the location is added to `tv_local_exhibition_locations`, and +10 Enthusiasm is awarded. Closing rewards are per-location CIP multiplied by `tv_local_exhibition_location_count`: per-location CIP = 0.5 × duration in months + tier bonus, with tier bonuses at ratings 1/2/3/4 = -3 / 0 / +3 / +6. Prestige rewards are not multiplied: tier 1 loses 10 Prestige, tier 3 adds +10 Prestige, and tier 4 adds +15 Prestige. Tier 4 still grants +0.25 development and +0.1 prosperity to the primary location, while the 1-year `tv_local_exhibition_triumph_location_modifier` is applied to every location in the closing `tv_local_exhibition_last_locations` snapshot. The Arts Exhibition panel's custom tab shows owned artworks, committed exhibits, active venue count, months open, rating, and a cold-to-hot enthusiasm progress bar. Active exhibitions also unlock royal visit, radical art, royal art sale, artist recruitment, foreign guest artist invitation, centerpiece designation, and live creation contest. Monthly exchange events each have a 1% chance and trade gold, Prestige, estate satisfaction, stability, legitimacy, host development, or host prosperity for +10 enthusiasm, with a decline option for -5 enthusiasm.

**Arts Exhibition laws:** The IO uses the vanilla IO Laws tab with seven generated law groups under `tv_arts_exhibition_laws`: Artistic Patronage, Artistic Organization, Official Artistic Purpose, Architecture and Urban Aesthetics, Knowledge Transmission Media, State Ritual Aesthetics, and Relationship Between Art and Science. The former custom Censorship and Artistic Freedom group was removed because vanilla already provides the equivalent censorship law. All Arts Exhibition laws use `requires_vote = yes`, `has_parliament = no` hides the separate IO Parliament tab, and the IO starts with no initial policies. Each policy combines its specified country modifiers with a societal-value tendency where applicable.

**Arts exchange mechanic:** The Arts Exhibition IO shows the arts exchange information and action cards in the resolutions tab, leaving the overview tab for the touring-artist count and the vanilla Laws tab for legal policy votes. A country can start a year-long exchange with `tv_start_arts_exchange` (`scaled_gold = 2.0`) when it has an appointed Chief Artist and at least two living artists; there is no cooldown, but an active exchange blocks starting another. The country tracks `tv_arts_exchange_active`, elapsed/remaining month variables, and persistent participant lists (`tv_arts_exchange_participants`, `tv_arts_exchange_foreign_participants`), while each formally joined participant receives both the activity marker variables and permanent `busy_modifier`. When the exchange starts, the country immediately preselects up to three additional non-busy domestic artists, stores them in `tv_arts_exchange_auto_invite_1/2/3`, shows them in event `tv_arts_exhibition.400`, and applies them from the event option. During the exchange the player can manually invite more non-busy domestic artists (`scaled_gold = 1.0`) or foreign artists (`scaled_gold = 1.5`, country selection then character selection). Exchange completion, participant death, or participant pruning removes `busy_modifier` and the tracking state. Monthly exchange outcomes fire through events with a 50% monthly roll; events 401, 402, 403, 404, and 410 have doubled internal weights, while the overall monthly roll stays 50%. Event 410 is the no-progress dispute outcome and costs 10 Prestige. `tv_arts_exhibition.420` announces the end and clears all exchange state.

Artist activity selection uses the vanilla `busy_modifier` as the shared busy definition. Arts Exchange and Domestic Exhibition remain country-level mutually exclusive, and a Chief Artist busy with either of those or another marked activity cannot begin a new Chief-led activity or initiate an Arts Exhibition law change. IO law votes themselves do not apply `busy_modifier` and do not make the Chief Artist unavailable for other artistic activities.

**Arts influence clarification:** `tv_arts_intl_influence` decays by 0.1% of its current value per month and is still driven primarily by foreign artist tours and the appointed Chief Artist. Persistent local exhibitions award `tv_cultural_influence_points` at closure rather than directly adding International Influence.

The **Scientific Victory** path (科技胜利) is fully implemented with a unique Academy of Sciences building gate: 5 triggers, 15 selectable reward modifiers, 5 events, and localization in English and Simplified Chinese. Score = the unweighted number of researched Advances; because the engine `num_advance_researched` interface (script-facing `num_of_advances_researched`) is broken/stuck in the affected EU5 build, the dedicated `gen_tv_science_score_effects.py` generator temporarily writes a complete `has_advance` scanner to the independent `tv_science_score_effects.txt` file from every top-level definition in `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/advances/`. The situation performs one initial scan only for player-controlled countries, then `tv_science_victory_yearly_pulse` rebuilds only player-country scores once per year; AI countries do not run this expensive workaround. Thresholds remain 100 / 150 / 200 / 300 / 400 (provisional, calibrate after playtesting). **Unique mechanic:** Each milestone only fires after the corresponding **Academy of Sciences** building phase is manually constructed in the capital. The building chain (Phases I-V) is visible from game start; each phase requires the previous phase plus the corresponding score threshold (`tv_academy_of_sciences_level` variable). Construction conditions use `custom_tooltip` (score variable is not auto-parseable). **Academy of Sciences IO** (`tv_academy_of_sciences`): non-unique IO created by the Scientific IO establishment flow before route progress unlocks. Establishment sets `tv_academy_io_member = 1`, initializes research mechanism variables, and calls `create_international_organization`. **IO leader = an appointed non-artist character** (Chief Scientist), set manually via Appoint/Remove/Change Leader actions in `tv_io_leader_actions.txt`; leader character stored on leader_country as `tv_academy_leader_char`; IO `leader = { leader_country ?= { var:tv_academy_leader_char ?= { add_to_list = leaders } } }` with `leader_change_trigger_type = none`. The IO uses the vanilla IO Laws tab for `tv_science_factions` and `tv_academy_leadership`: `tv_academy_leader_gender_law` starts at `tv_academy_leader_gender_male_only_policy`, so Chief Scientist candidates must be male until `tv_academy_leader_gender_unrestricted_policy` is enacted; the other six Academy law groups start unenacted. All seven laws keep `requires_vote = yes` for IO policy votes, and the six scientific-faction laws can carry country_modifier gameplay effects after enactment; the Academy sets `has_parliament = no`, hiding the vanilla IO Parliament tab.

**Research Mechanism (Academy research-target shortcut):** Four sub-processes complete one full cycle, which auto-researches one chosen Academy target advance via `research_advance = advance_type:X` (these advances remain normally researchable via the vanilla research tab; the Research Mechanism is an extra unlock route, not a gate). Sub-process A (Interest Collection): monthly random event (`tv_research.1`, ~10% base chance) costs dynamic gold via `change_gold_effect = { scale = -2 }` and adds +1 to `tv_research_interest`; completes at 5. Sub-process B (Prerequisites): the current target list has no target-specific B prerequisites, so `tv_research_check_b_trigger` is `always = yes`; B remains visible in the staged UI and completes through the normal monthly flow. Sub-process C (Literacy Foundation): **IO variable** `tv_research_c_progress` accumulates `average_country_literacy × 0.05` per month; completes at 100 (~40 months at 50% literacy). When A/B/C are all complete, `tv_research.19` fires a one-time no-effect reminder that concentrated research can now begin. Sub-process D (Concentrated Research): **IO variable** `tv_research_d_progress` accumulates `effective ADM × 0.05` per month (base `leader_country.var:tv_academy_leader_char.adm` plus +20/+40/+60 from `tv_great_scientist_1/2/3`); completes at 100 based on effective ADM and then grants or upgrades the current Chief Scientist's Great Scientist trait. D halts when no leader appointed; while active it also applies `tv_concentrated_research_penalty` (-20% research speed), removed silently on completion or reset.

**Target selection:** Player clicks "Request Research Topic" -> random proposal from unresearched targets in the current age or earlier; if that pool is empty, the proposal falls back to the lowest future age with unresearched targets -> event `tv_research.10` for confirmation. Abandoning a proposed or active target costs 10 Prestige and has no cooldown; abandon options are hidden, and the abandon action is disallowed below 10 Prestige. **31 active Academy research targets:** 1=medieval_administration(A1), 2=scriptorium_advance(A1), 3=horse_riding_advance(A1), 4=bronze_working(A1), 5=iron_working(A1), 6=efficient_logistics(A1), 7=abacus_advance(A1), 8=renaissance_sculptures(A2), 9=crown_power_advance_renaissance(A2), 10=dock_advance(A2), 11=standardized_coins(A2), 12=gunpowder_advance(A2), 13=armory_advance(A2), 14=open_sea_exploration(A3), 15=blast_furnace(A3), 16=faceting(A3), 17=paper_workshop_advance(A3), 18=training_fields_advance(A3), 19=customs_house_advance(A3), 20=gov_reform_reformation_a(A4), 21=pharmacology_advance(A4), 22=letters_of_marque(A4), 23=slitting_mills(A4), 24=cannon_workshop_advance(A4), 25=barracks_advance(A4), 26=ostentatious_clothing(A5), 27=sanitation_advance(A5), 28=coke_blast_furnace(A5), 29=scientific_mapping_advance(A5), 30=artillery_vs_fort_age_5_absolutism(A5), 31=army_movement_speed_absolutism(A5). Each is listed in `data/locked_advances.yaml`; `tv_unlock_advance_<id>_effect` is generated by `scripts/gen_locked_advances.py` and called by `tv_research_concentrated_complete_effect` on cycle D completion. **Cycle phases:** 0=idle, 1=A/B/C running, 2=D concentrated research. State variables: `tv_research_phase`, `tv_research_target`, `tv_research_interest`, `tv_research_b_done`; display vars: `tv_rm_subprocess_a/b/c`, `tv_rm_subprocess_d_ready`.

**Philosophy Debate:** The Academy overview slot now presents debate history as a fixed world timeline rather than the old action-card minigame. The IO variable `tv_academy_philosophy_inspiration` is still the 0-100 Inspiration bar and gains `effective ADM × 0.2` per month while the Chief Scientist is idle, but it is displayed in the Academy header's right-side activity card alongside only the current Chief Scientist activity; the header does not show the debate topic. Debate nodes start in June 1313 and advance every 10 years; node indexes 4, 9, 14, ... are world debates after each group of four local debates. On Academy establishment, `tv_academy_debate_established_node` records the current node, `tv_academy_debate_last_notified_node` starts at that same node, and visible nodes with index <= the establishment node are shown as history. The overview always shows five nodes with the current node in the center, a fixed 50% background progress bar, node-type icons/tooltips for History/Local/World, and node years. A 1337 start-date Academy therefore shows 1313/1323/1333 as history, 1343 as local, and 1353 as world, with 1333 centered. When the calendar reaches a later node, the monthly Academy pulse updates the timeline and fires `tv_research.41` as the node-switch notification. The current issue card switches by node type: history shows a large roundtable with flavor text, local shows a large roundtable with the current issue or recess plus six local seats and a Crown chair when a debate is active, and world shows the live 50-seat world debate grid with country flags and stance tinting. Domestic local debate progress is the Academy IO variable `tv_academy_philosophy_debate_position`, defined on `tv_academy_of_sciences` with 0-100 bounds, start value 50, and localized format/change-format text. Debate initialization and current-state resets call `tv_academy_debate_set_local_progress_effect`; monthly seated-group progress, generated action hooks, and local random-event options call `tv_academy_debate_change_local_progress_effect`. That change effect performs the IO-scoped add and clamp and uses `tv_academy_debate_change_local_progress_text` effect localization so tooltips render consistent gain/loss lines automatically. Endpoint checks read `tv_academy_philosophy_debate_position` from the owned Academy IO rather than from a country variable. Reaching `>=100` fires the acceptance endpoint, grants the issue's institution progress to owned locations, researches the linked advance, advances the issue, and enters recess; reaching `<=0` fires the rejection endpoint, advances the issue, and enters recess with no reward. World debates use the separate always-active `tv_academy_world_debate_situation`: one Academy country contributes one seat, support strength drives monthly progress from the 50 midpoint, and resolution or timeout mirrors the world result back to eligible Academy countries. The old six-round state circles, action slots, monthly decay, and old reward/penalty paths are removed or disconnected. At 100 Inspiration on a local node with no active/pending debate, `tv_research.40` announces the completed conception and starts the local debate only if the current issue still exists and its institution has not already been embraced. Each country keeps its own issue sequence regardless of world-debate nodes; the next issue cannot begin before the current issue has ended. The issue order is fixed as Meritocracy, Renaissance, Banking System, Discovery of the New World, Printing Press Applications, Confessionalism, Global Trade, Factory Production, and Scientific Revolution. If the country embraces the institution tied to the current issue, the `on_institution_embraced` bridge immediately fires `tv_research.42`; the monthly pulse also catches issues already embraced before the Academy existed. That event enters recess, advances to the next issue, resets Inspiration, and grants no debate reward or penalty.

The header manages the Chief Scientist appointment/replacement/dismissal from the portrait area. The resolutions slot is renamed Concentrated Research and contains the Research Mechanism UI: target, Chief Scientist name (from appointed character), subprocess A/B/C/D status, current B requirements rendered with `RequirementsList` + `ShowTriggerConditions('tv_research_check_b_trigger', PlayerScope.Self)`, and research action buttons. The victory situation panel keeps Scientific Victory to the shared progress row after establishment; Academy operational details live in the IO panel. **Medicine Tab:** A separate Medicine tab in the IO panel lets the player treat a court character with a bad health trait. The player clicks "Treat Character" (`tv_treat_character` generic action) to pick a target filtered by `tv_has_health_condition_trigger` (blind, one_eyed, maimed, disfigured, scarred, hunchback, pockmarked_trait, smallpox_trait, bubonic_plague_trait, sickly). Once a patient is selected, `tv_med_target_char` (country variable on leader_country) holds the character reference; only one active patient at a time. Monthly processing (in `monthly_country_pulse` effect block): if the Chief Scientist is alive, cache effective ADM into `tv_med_disp_adm` and base `dip/mil` into `tv_med_disp_dip/mil` for display, then `tv_med_progress += effective ADM × 0.1`; when progress >= 100, `tv_med_complete_treatment_effect` fires (`tv_medicine.3` popup, all bad health traits removed, state reset). Patient natural death triggers `tv_medicine.4` popup and state reset. Sub-action `tv_med_discovery_pulse` fires `tv_medicine.1` (~1%/month, fixed approximation of dip×0.2%): three options - Conservative (95%: +5 progress / 5%: patient dies), Risky (80%: +10 progress / 20%: patient dies), Wait (no effect). Sub-action `tv_med_accident_pulse` fires `tv_medicine.2` (~5%/month, fixed): notification only. Tab displays: current patient name (or "None"), progress bar (max 100, visible when patient active), monthly gain rate, discovery chance, accident chance. State variables: `tv_med_target_char`, `tv_med_progress`, `tv_med_disp_adm`, `tv_med_disp_dip`, `tv_med_disp_mil` - all country variables on leader_country, initialized when the Academy is established and reset by `tv_med_cleanup_effect`.

Academy Medicine also supports **Meditation**. The player clicks `tv_start_meditation` in the Medicine tab to select a living character with at least one negative non-health trait from the ruler, cabinet, or artist trait sets (`tv_has_meditation_negative_trait_trigger`); negative health traits remain treatment-only. Active meditation stores `tv_meditation_target_char` and `tv_meditation_progress` on the country. The target receives `tv_meditating_character_modifier` while meditation is active, blocking military leadership and applying −50% character cabinet efficiency; if the selected target is the current ruler, the country also receives `tv_meditating_ruler_country_modifier` for −0.2 monthly legitimacy. Monthly progress uses the same rate as medical treatment (`effective ADM × 0.1`) through `tv_change_meditation_progress_effect`, whose effect localization reports positive and negative progress changes. Reaching 100 fires `tv_medicine.20`, removes all matching negative ruler/cabinet/artist traits from the target, removes the temporary meditation modifiers, and clears meditation state. The player may stop meditation at any time through `tv_stop_meditation`; stopping only clears the meditation target/progress and temporary meditation modifiers, without cleansing traits. While meditation is active, `tv_meditation_monthly_pulse` has a 10% monthly chance to fire exactly one event from the shared meditation event list: calm (+5), restlessness (−5), high-DIP guidance (+10 if Chief Scientist DIP > 80), low-DIP counterguidance (−10 if DIP < 30), or inner turmoil (50/50 +5/−5). When meditation is vacant, the Chief Scientist is alive and available, and at least one eligible character exists, `tv_meditation_yearly_pulse` makes a 50% yearly roll; success fires `tv_medicine.22`, randomly selects one eligible character, and starts meditation immediately on that character's own initiative. The Medicine tab shows a separate Meditation card with default guidance text, the selected character card, a progress bar, the monthly gain rate, and the fixed event chance.

Chief Scientist work is mutually exclusive: concentrated research, Academy law-change policy votes, philosophy debate, medical treatment, and meditation each block the other long-running tasks, with explicit busy-state tooltip text naming the active task.

Current concentrated research behavior: D progress and completion require a living Chief Scientist with at least 20 effective ADM. The player can pause D with `tv_pause_concentrated_research`, preserving `tv_research_d_progress` while freeing the Chief Scientist for laws or medicine. While D is active, `tv_research_d_pulse` uses one shared `random_events` list for `tv_research.100`-`.129`; each currently eligible event has 1% monthly weight, and eligibility is gated by effective ADM tiers <20, 20-49, 50-79, and >=80.

Great Scientist traits (`tv_great_scientist_1/2/3`) are Chief Scientist-only character traits in `src/in_game/common/traits/tv_great_scientist_traits.txt`. They add +20 / +40 / +60 effective ADM only while the character is appointed as the Academy Chief Scientist; they are read by concentrated research progress, D-event tiering, and Medicine monthly progress. Completing concentrated research grants the current Chief Scientist level 1 or upgrades level 1→2→3; level 3 stays capped.

The mod is additive-only and uses the `tv_` namespace prefix throughout.

## Core Features

Current correction for this list: Diplomatic Victory Points now come from monthly alliance-tier maintenance after Diplomatic Alliance membership. Royal marriages, war wins, alliance founding, and alliance joining do not directly award DVP; no M1 reward creates an IO directly.

1. **Victory Situation** — One generated situation panel (`tv_victory_situation`) displays a top row of six path-selection actions, then one selected path page at a time. If that path's `tv_<path>_victory_enabled` flag is absent, the page shows the three-step IO establishment card; once enabled, it shows that path's progress bar, five milestone nodes, five reward cards with three circular reward actions each, and that path's global top-five leaderboard. Page selection is stored on the player country as `tv_victory_selected_path`; unset defaults to Conquest.
2. **Milestone Events** — Popup country events per milestone. All 30 events implemented across 6 paths: Conquest (`tv.conquest.1`–`5`), Prosperity (`tv.prosperity.1`–`5`), Trade (`tv.trade.1`–`5`), Diplomatic (`tv.diplomatic.1`–`5`), Cultural (`tv.cultural.1`–`5`), and Scientific (`tv.science.1`–`5`). Event options call `tv_unlock_<path>_milestone_<n>` for unlock-only side effects and do not grant the milestone modifier.
3. **Selectable Permanent Rewards** — All 90 milestone reward-option modifiers are permanent static modifiers (`days = -1`); no time-limited buffs. Each unlocked milestone exposes three generated reward actions, each with its own `tv_<path>_m<n>_reward_<choice>_bonus` modifier. Selecting a reward stores `tv_<path>_m<n>_reward_choice`, silently removes all three reward modifiers for that milestone in `hidden_effect`, and visibly applies only the selected modifier.
4. **Diplomatic Victory Points** (`tv_diplomatic_victory_points`) — Initialized to 0 when needed by alliance establishment, alliance joining, milestone checks, or monthly maintenance, then accumulated through monthly Diplomatic Alliance tier maintenance. The alliance leader gains the full `tv_alliance_tier` each month, while each non-leader member gains half. Royal marriages, war wins, alliance founding, and alliance joining do not directly award DVP.
5. **Cultural Influence Points** (`tv_cultural_influence_points`) — Accumulated via `on_work_of_art_created` (+3 CIP to `root.owner`), `monthly_country_pulse` (+1 CIP/month), and persistent local exhibition rewards from the Arts Exhibition IO.
6. **Scientific Technology Score** (`tv_science_score`) — Temporary engine workaround isolated in generated `tv_science_score_effects.txt`: one initial and then yearly complete `has_advance` scan for player-controlled countries only, over Advance ids generated from the mirrored vanilla definitions; AI countries are excluded. Thresholds are 100 / 150 / 200 / 300 / 400. Remove the independent scanner and callback when the engine `num_advance_researched` / script-facing `num_of_advances_researched` count is confirmed fixed.
7. **Academy of Sciences Building Chain** — Five capital buildings (`tv_academy_of_sciences_1`–`5`) gate Scientific Victory milestones after the Scientific IO establishment flow unlocks route progress. Each requires the previous phase built and the corresponding score threshold; `on_built` immediately triggers `tv_check_science_milestones_effect`. Building category: `cultural_category`; visible in all capitals from game start. Establishing the Scientific IO creates the **Academy of Sciences IO** (`tv_academy_of_sciences`) and shows the generated establishment popup. Membership flag: `tv_academy_io_member` (country variable set to 1 on join). IO leader (Chief Scientist) = appointed non-artist character stored on leader_country as `tv_academy_leader_char`; managed via the Appoint/Remove/Change Leader actions in `tv_io_leader_actions.txt`. `leader_change_trigger_type = none`; `international_organization_chooses_new_leader` is deliberately NOT called — it triggers the vanilla leader-election process, violating the no-elections design. The Academy has a vanilla IO Laws tab but no Parliament tab. On establishment, only `tv_academy_leader_gender_law` is initialized, at `tv_academy_leader_gender_male_only_policy`; the other six law groups start unenacted. Enacting `tv_academy_leader_gender_unrestricted_policy` removes the gender filter from Chief Scientist candidate selection, and all Academy law changes keep `requires_vote = yes`.
8. **Research Mechanism (Academy Research-Target Shortcut)** — Full four-subprocess cycle that auto-researches one of 31 active Academy target advances via `research_advance` on cycle completion. The advances remain normally researchable via the vanilla tech tree — the Research Mechanism is an *extra* unlock path, not a gate. Sub-process A: monthly random interest events (~10%; 5 funded successes needed; event namespace `tv_research`; each success costs dynamic gold via `change_gold_effect = { scale = -2 }`). Sub-process B: passive prerequisite stage with no target-specific prerequisites in the current target list; `tv_research_check_b_trigger` is `always = yes`, while B remains visible and completes through the normal monthly flow. Sub-process C: IO variable `tv_research_c_progress` auto-accumulates via `monthly_change`; its current completion hook is legacy IO `monthly_effect` debt and must be migrated before validation can pass. Once A/B/C are all complete, `tv_research.19` pops once as a no-effect reminder that concentrated research can begin. Sub-process D: IO variable `tv_research_d_progress` auto-accumulates via the appointed leader's effective ADM (base ADM plus Great Scientist trait bonus), applies `tv_concentrated_research_penalty` (−20% research speed) while active, triggers unlock on completion, and grants/upgrades that Chief Scientist's Great Scientist trait. Three research generic actions: `tv_request_research_target`, `tv_abandon_research_target`, `tv_start_concentrated_research`; abandoning a proposed or active target costs 10 Prestige and has no cooldown, with abandon UI gated behind 10 Prestige. Lifecycle managed by scripted effects in `tv_research_subprocess_effects.txt`; conditions in `tv_research_subprocess_triggers.txt`; the Academy overview displays B's current conditions via `ShowTriggerConditions('tv_research_check_b_trigger', PlayerScope.Self)`. Philosophy debate uses the generated `tv_academy_debate` event namespace and `data/philosophy_debates.yaml`: local debates initialize at 50 progress with a fixed pro-issue Crown seat, then fill five non-Crown seats through monthly events with estate, variant, artist, foreign-power, and Great Scientist groups whose stances, defections, prices, special scopes, progress contributions, and result effects are generated from the YAML source. **31 active Academy research targets:** 1=medieval_administration(A1), 2=scriptorium(A1), 3=horse_riding(A1), 4=bronze_working(A1), 5=iron_working(A1), 6=efficient_logistics(A1), 7=abacus(A1), 8=renaissance_sculptures(A2), 9=crown_power_renaissance(A2), 10=dock(A2), 11=standardized_coins(A2), 12=gunpowder(A2), 13=armory(A2), 14=open_sea_exploration(A3), 15=blast_furnace(A3), 16=faceting(A3), 17=paper_workshop(A3), 18=training_fields(A3), 19=customs_house(A3), 20=the_prince(A4), 21=pharmacology(A4), 22=letters_of_marque(A4), 23=slitting_mills(A4), 24=cannon_workshop(A4), 25=barracks(A4), 26=ostentatious_clothing(A5), 27=sanitation(A5), 28=coke_blast_furnace(A5), 29=scientific_mapping(A5), 30=siege_cannons(A5), 31=magazines(A5). Each is listed in `data/locked_advances.yaml` with `vanilla_advance_id` and `age` fields; the `tv_unlock_advance_<id>_effect` (set_variable + `research_advance = advance_type:<vanilla_id>`) is generated by `scripts/gen_locked_advances.py`. Target proposal first randomizes among unresearched targets in the current age or earlier; if that pool is empty, it falls back to the lowest age among remaining unresearched target advances. The Concentrated Research tab's "Potential Projects" card needs the same age-gate/researched-status logic in GUI-readable form (GUI cannot evaluate scripted triggers), so `gen_locked_advances.py` also generates `tv_research_refresh_advance_status_effect` (one `tv_advance_<id>_researched` variable per advance, mirroring `has_advance`) into `tv_advance_unlock_effects.txt`, and `tv_research_subprocess_effects.txt` hand-defines `tv_research_refresh_open_age_ceiling_effect` (mirrors the four `tv_research_proposal_includes_age_N_frontier_trigger`s into a single `tv_research_open_age_ceiling` int, since the open-age set is always a contiguous prefix). Both are refreshed together via `tv_research_refresh_potential_projects_effect`, called from `tv_academy_of_sciences_create_effect` (join) and `tv_academy_of_sciences_monthly_pulse`.
9. **IO Leader Management** — Unified character-leader pattern across six TV character roles (Arts Exhibition, Diplomatic Alliance, Academy of Sciences, Governor's House Grand General, Engineering Department Great Engineer, Trade League Grand Merchant). Each IO uses `leader_type = character` + `leader_change_trigger_type = none` + `leader_change_method = none` + a `leader = { leader_country ?= { var:tv_<io>_leader_char ?= { add_to_list = leaders } } }` block, with Governor's House using `tv_conquest_general_char`, Engineering Department using `tv_great_engineer_char`, and Trade League using `tv_grand_merchant_char`. `tv_io_leader_actions.txt` now contains 30 generated actions: 12 establishment/build-HQ actions plus 18 Appoint/Remove/Change management actions. Appointing a chief sets `tv_io_chief_role` on the character, every selector rejects characters already carrying that marker, and remove/change/death cleanup clears it with the role modifier. Each management action sets or clears the country variable only — `international_organization_chooses_new_leader` is deliberately NOT called (it triggers the vanilla leader-election process, violating the no-elections design). All TV IOs have `unique = no`; never use `international_organization:type_name` syntax for them — use `every_international_organizations_member_of = { limit = { international_organization_type = international_organization_type:<io_type> } ... }` instead. Arts Exhibition filters candidates by `is_artist = yes`; Academy, Engineering Department, and Trade League exclude artists; Diplomatic Alliance accepts any non-ruler/heir/consort alive adult character; Grand General additionally excludes female characters and artists. Five main-mod CMF alerts remain in the shared main alert chain. The sixth, `tv_missing_engineering_department_chief_alert`, is standalone-owned: its Engineering Department monthly pulse synchronizes it, its dedicated `cmf_on_callback` handler records alert clicks, and its trigger/effects/scripted-GUI/hidden bridge open the Engineering Department panel; its labels live in the standalone EN/ZH localization. **Character title prefixes (Arts Exhibition + Academy + Governor's House + Engineering Department + Trade League):** Appointing/removing leaders also adds/removes zero-stat character modifiers (`tv_chief_artist_role_modifier` / `tv_chief_scientist_role_modifier` / `tv_grand_general_role_modifier` / `tv_great_engineer_role_modifier` / `tv_grand_merchant_role_modifier`). The shared generator writes root-specific full-copy `character_title.txt` outputs: the main TV root carries the TV + Engineering Department union for the manually-later TV winner case, the Engineering Department root carries only the Great Engineer title, and Court Positions carries no TV title insertions. Modifier definitions are split between `src/in_game/common/static_modifiers/tv_io_role_modifiers.txt` and `src_engineering_department/in_game/common/static_modifiers/tv_engineering_department_role_modifiers.txt`. The Diplomatic Alliance leader has no title modifier.
10. **Governor's House IO** (`tv_govhouse`): non-unique IO created by the Conquest IO establishment flow. **Governor system:** characters appointed by the player to govern EU5 regions. Governor bonuses scale dynamically with character attributes, and the exact local function modifier is selected by the three **Local Governor Functions** IO laws: ADM can provide max control, monthly development, or production efficiency; DIP can provide build cost, pop promotion, or population growth; MIL can provide unrest, levy size, or fort defense. Each regional governor also runs one autonomous yearly task: restore prosperity, integrate region, convert culture, convert religion, strengthen recruitment, or strengthen order. Data model: `tv_govhouse_region_list` is a country list of regions with owned locations, rebuilt only by lifecycle hooks; `tv_govhouse_region_display_list` is the GUI representative-location list; `tv_governor_list` stores appointed governor characters; `tv_govhouse_region_to_admin_location_map` is a stable country map from region to the system-designated Local Administration location, not a scan of existing buildings; selected locations carry `tv_govhouse_local_admin_owner` and `tv_govhouse_admin_region`; and `tv_govhouse_has_valid_governor` is the monthly random-event cache flag. Non-capital regions select an admin point only when the map is missing or the old point is no longer owned, preferring the highest-population city/megalopolis, then town, then rural settlement. Startup/load, location owner change, capital move, and Local Administration confirmation maintain the region/admin caches; monthly logic does not rebuild them. Manual appointment/change routes through `tv_governor_request_appointment_effect`: the capital region appoints directly, non-capital regions appoint only when the mapped admin location has `tv_govhouse_local_administration`, and missing non-capital regions save pending governor/region/location variables before firing `tv_govhouse.5` for the mapped location. Accepting instantly constructs the event-only Local Administration building at that designated location, refreshes the marker/map, and resumes appointment; declining clears the pending state. If the designated admin location is transferred away, `tv_govhouse.7` removes the building from the transferred location, clears the losing country's map and governor assignment for that region, and lets loser/winner refresh only the affected region cache so the winner never inherits the building. `tv_govhouse_local_administration` remains a one-level government-category support building available to all ranks for scripted construction only: `country_potential`, `allow`, automation construction, and demolition are all disabled; it uses paper 1 + tools 1 maintenance and cannot be manually built or destroyed. The overview-level `tv_auto_assign_governors` action repeatedly fills empty capital/prepared regions with the highest `total_abilities` eligible characters and can prompt the player to build the mapped Local Administration for one unprepared non-capital region; it does not scan for arbitrary regional buildings. All governor management actions require an alive Grand General. If the Grand General is dismissed or dies, existing governors remain appointed and continue through event/yearly/law-driven maintenance, but no new governor can be appointed/changed/removed until a successor is appointed. **Grand General:** character leader stored as the independent country variable `tv_conquest_general_char`; managed via generated Appoint/Remove/Change IO leader actions and displayed in the IO header. Newly appointed Grand Generals initialize `tv_gg_loyalty` on the character if absent (nobles 0%, burghers/clergy 10%, crown 30%, other estates 20%); the value is mirrored to `tv_gg_loyalty_value`, does not drift automatically, and lowers the Governor's House power-balance equilibrium by the same amount. A monthly 5% loyalty pulse can fire one of three events: -5 loyalty, +5 loyalty, or a reward demand costing `scale = 1` gold for +5 loyalty with refusal causing -5 loyalty. `tv_govhouse_authority` IO variable (0-100, monthly decay x 0.0005, grows by Grand General's mil x 0.05). The Governor's House overview tab renders the display-location list: each row shows a region icon/name, the autonomous task slot, right-aligned ADM/DIP/MIL function entries with formula tooltips, and the current governor as a standard `io_character_card`; Assign/Cancel/Change Governor buttons pass the row's region scope. The dedicated Grand General tab shows the current variable-backed character, active training progress below the character card, authority value, Appoint/Change/Remove actions, and the existing Train Grand General action.

Governor automation note: `tv_enable_governor_automation` / `tv_disable_governor_automation` toggle the country variable `tv_govhouse_governor_automation_enabled` from the overview `button_checkbox_auto` checkbutton beside `tv_auto_assign_governors`. When enabled, governor death, manual removal, location ownership changes, governor country moves, a governor becoming ruler, and marriage/royal-marriage cases where a governor becomes a consort each run one guarded `tv_govhouse_auto_assign_if_enabled_effect` pass; that helper delegates to `tv_governor_auto_assign_effect` only while a living Grand General is available, so the automation does not rely on monthly polling.

Governor assignment country note: new Local Governor assignments store `tv_governor_country` on the character so event-driven vacancy cleanup can find the original appointing country even if the character's current owner changes.

Governor autonomous task note: `tv_governor_select_yearly_task_effect` sets one active task per regional governor. The six tasks are restore prosperity, integrate region, convert culture, convert religion, strengthen recruitment, and strengthen order; integration is one of these autonomous tasks, not a separate always-on governor modifier. The task values scale from ADM/DIP/MIL, are displayed through `tv_govhouse_display_task_type/value`, and are refreshed into region-wide location modifiers by `tv_governor_apply_modifiers_effect`.

Regional Governor random events: `tv_govhouse_governor_events_monthly_pulse` runs through the generated `monthly_country_pulse` registry and only triggers if at least one living governor has an owned governed region. It uses a single monthly country-level roll with 180 no-event weight plus 20 event weights, giving a 10% total monthly chance. Events `tv_govhouse.100`-`.119` each pick one random eligible governor in `immediate`, save the governor, governed region, and a representative owned location as event scopes, then apply one-year region-wide location modifiers and/or immediate or one-year country effects. The 20-event set covers two-choice good events, one-option good events, two-choice tradeoff events, two-choice bad events, and one-option bad events.

Governor's House laws: the IO uses vanilla international-organization parliament law votes through `tv_govhouse_assembly`, and `tv_govhouse.gui` reuses the Diplomatic Alliance three-column parliament vote-card UI for support / not-voted / opposition rows. Only the locked `leader_country` / Supreme Overlord can initiate policy votes. The Supreme Overlord special status always contributes `special_status_power = 100`; each subject-chain member votes through `tv_govhouse_subject_status`, whose dynamic special-status power is based on its relative strength against the leader (`root.relative_strength(scope:recipient.leader_country) * 200`). The assembly keeps a leader-capital `parliament_seat` synchronized at creation and subject-chain refresh so non-HRE IO parliament law votes have a valid seat. Six generated core law groups (`tv_govhouse_expedition_goal_law`, `tv_govhouse_governor_appointment_law`, `tv_govhouse_local_autonomy_law`, `tv_govhouse_religious_mission_law`, `tv_govhouse_frontier_military_law`, `tv_govhouse_imperial_integration_law`) each have four effect-bearing country policies, and their generated `country_modifier` blocks are intentionally unfiltered so policy effects apply to every Governor's House member country. `tv_govhouse_grand_general_power_law` sits in the new `tv_govhouse_grand_general` category: Marching Orders gives newly appointed Grand Generals +10% initial loyalty, Garrison Orders makes governor task/function effects 20% stronger, and Supreme Command gives newly appointed Grand Generals -10% initial loyalty while doubling General Tactics and War Plan progress gain. Four generated Local Governor Function laws sit in `tv_govhouse_governor_functions`: the ADM/DIP/MIL function laws decide which scaled local modifiers governors apply, while `tv_govhouse_governor_duty_tendency_law` adds +0.30 task-selection weight to one of the six autonomous governor tasks. Every generated policy has a data-driven AI base vote bias from -60 to +60 in 20-point steps, favoring subject-friendly, traditional/conservative, and profit-seeking choices; when a policy is the current proposed law target (`ai_io_law_target`), the AI also adds opinion of the IO leader scaled from -200..+200 opinion to -100..+100 vote bias plus +5 vote bias per leader diplomatic reputation. All eleven laws keep `requires_vote = yes` and their generated `allow` block requires `uses_parliament_for_law_votes_trigger = yes`; the IO starts with no initial policies. There is no monthly default-law backfill.

Governor's House War Tent: requires an alive Grand General. The custom War Tent tab has three units: General Tactics, War Plan, and Main Actions. On war entry, `tv_govhouse_war_entry_effect` proposes one random tactic from the eleven-tactic pool and fires `tv_govhouse.40`; the player approves or rejects it. While continuously at war, the monthly pulse has a 5% chance to propose a replacement tactic, excluding the tactic already active. Approved tactics are applied as country-scoped static modifiers and are cleared when the country leaves all wars or loses its Grand General; this approximates general tactics because the army-wide combat modifiers did not apply reliably from the stored character path. Supreme Command doubles the applied tactic modifier size, including the Capital Raid location modifier. War plans are mutually exclusive: offensive plans select a target country and, while at war with that target, scale Siege Ability by progress x 0.2% and Discipline by progress x 0.1%; defensive plans need no target and, while in any defensive war, scale Fort Defense by progress x 0.2% and Military Tactics by progress x 0.005. On war entry, applicable offensive/defensive plan bonuses are activated through `tv_govhouse.41` / `tv_govhouse.42` events instead of being granted directly by the war-entry effect. Plan progress increases monthly by Grand General adm x 0.02 up to 100, doubled under Supreme Command; aborting/changing target resets it, and peace with an offensive plan target aborts that plan.

Governor's House header action note: `tv_govhouse.gui` adds a fixed-size semi-transparent Grand General Actions card to the IO header. It displays, in priority order, the appointed Grand General's commanded army link, wartime tactic planning, peacetime subject-annexation assistance, peacetime inspections, or the no-Grand-General fallback. The peacetime annexation row is driven by the country cache `tv_govhouse_annexing_subject_active`, refreshed by `tv_govhouse_update_general_action_display_effect` from the existing monthly War Tent path so the GUI does not scan subject diplomacy directly.

8. **Global Victory Leaderboard** — Monthly per-path ranking of the top 5 countries for each victory path. `tv_update_leaderboard_effect` writes situation variables `tv_<path>_rank_1_country` through `tv_<path>_rank_5_country`; the Victory Situation page for the selected path displays those countries with flag, country name, the selected path circle icon, that path's milestone count, and that path's score text.

Governor's House eligibility/death note: new governors and Grand Generals exclude female characters and artists at the selection/action-effect entry points. There is no monthly legacy-eligibility cleanup, to keep the IO monthly path focused on active governor bonus refresh. `tv_govhouse_governor_death` is registered under `on_character_death`, fires `tv_govhouse.1`, removes the dead governor from `tv_governor_list`, clears their region modifiers, and rebuilds the display lists immediately.

Grand General training note: `tv_train_grand_general` applies the one-year `tv_grand_general_training_modifier` country modifier defined in `src/in_game/common/static_modifiers/tv_govhouse_modifiers.txt` (`court_spending_efficiency = -0.10`), tracks `tv_gg_training_months` on the Grand General via monthly pulse, mirrors it to the country-side GUI display variable `tv_gg_training_progress`, resolves at 12 months through `tv_training_resolve_effect`, and routes to skill gain, trait-path upgrade, injury, or death events before clearing state.

Grand General death notification note: natural Grand General deaths trigger `tv_govhouse.2` from `tv_govhouse_char_death`; training-caused deaths set `tv_gg_training_death_notified` before `kill_character = this` so the already-shown training death event is not duplicated.

Governor's House balance-of-power backlash note: `tv_govhouse_power_balance` is an IO variable in the 0..1 range and is checked monthly by `tv_govhouse_negative_monthly_pulse` (registered through `data/pulse_registry.yaml`). `tv_govhouse_power_equilibrium` recalculates monthly from a -20% base, the Grand General's share of combined ruler/general abilities, `tv_govhouse_power_consolidation`, and a subtraction equal to the mirrored Grand General loyalty value; `tv_govhouse_power_balance` then moves toward that equilibrium by up to 0.6% per month. Below 25% no backlash events fire. At 25%/50%/75% the pulse uses a single `random_list` roll with a 300 total weight so at most one backlash event can appear per month, and each eligible event has weight 1/300. Events `tv_govhouse.10`-`.13` are tier 1, `.20`-`.25` are tier 2, and `.30`-`.34` are tier 3. Every backlash event has exactly two options: accept the negative effect, or pay an alternative resource and call `tv_govhouse_reduce_power_balance_effect` to lower the balance by 5 percentage points. Tier 2 adds rare art/artist losses; tier 3 upgrades the local satisfaction loss to an area-wide effect and adds a cabinet crisis. The Governor's House GUI shows right-aligned fixed-width 25/50/75/100% markers and four warning rows below the Balance of Power bar for the <25, 25-49, 50-74, and 75-100 segments.

Grand General replacement cost note: generated Remove/Change Grand General actions call `tv_govhouse_pay_grand_general_replacement_cost_effect` before resetting `tv_govhouse_power_consolidation`; the cost is Government Power equal to `tv_govhouse_power_consolidation * 20`, so a fully consolidated Grand General costs 20 government power to remove or replace.

## IO Architecture Design Decisions

The character-led TV IOs (`tv_arts_exhibition`, `tv_diplomatic_alliance`, `tv_academy_of_sciences`, `tv_govhouse`, `tv_engineering_department`, `tv_trade_league`) share invariants that must never be broken.

### 1. leader_country = founding country, locked at creation

`on_creation = { set_leader_country = scope:actor }` runs once when the IO is created. `leader_country` is not reassigned by normal gameplay afterward; the only exception is the manual recovery action `tv_reinitialize_mod`, exposed at the top of the Victory Situation panel, which recreates missing victory-path IOs where possible and reassigns joined TV IO instances back to the current country. It controls IO permissions, `is_leader_of_international_organization` trigger, and `leader_country.modifier:` chained access in `monthly_change` scripted values.

### 2. No elections — leader_change_trigger_type = none is mandatory

All TV character-led IO type definitions keep `leader_change_trigger_type = none` and `leader_change_method = none`. These prevent the engine from reassigning `leader_country` automatically. `international_organization_chooses_new_leader` is **globally banned** from all TV IO-related code — this effect triggers the vanilla election process. Any call to it violates the no-elections design and risks silently reassigning `leader_country` to a different country.

### 3. Great person characters are independent country variables, not the vanilla ruler

The character displayed in the IO header is stored as a country variable on the `leader_country`:
- Arts Exhibition: `tv_arts_exhibition_leader_char`
- Diplomatic Alliance: `tv_diplomatic_alliance_leader_char`
- Academy of Sciences: `tv_academy_leader_char`
- Governor's House: `tv_conquest_general_char`
- Engineering Department: `tv_great_engineer_char`
- Trade League: `tv_grand_merchant_char`

These are set/cleared exclusively by the generated generic actions in `tv_io_leader_actions.txt`. The vanilla `leader_country.GetGovernment.GetRuler` and `appointed_leader.*` are different concepts and must not be used in TV IO calculations.

### 4. IO variable monthly_change uses chained character attribute access

All IO variable `monthly_change` scripted values that depend on the appointed great person must use `leader_country.var:tv_xxx_leader_char.attribute` chained access — never `appointed_leader.attribute`. Examples:
- CORRECT: `value = leader_country.var:tv_academy_leader_char.adm`
- FORBIDDEN: `value = appointed_leader.adm`

### 5. IO monthly_effect blocks are forbidden

TV international_organization type definitions must not use `monthly_effect` blocks. IO monthly effects have severe performance costs even when hidden. Keep visible typed-variable monthly arithmetic in each variable's `monthly_change`; route maintenance, completion checks, and side-effect work through registered country monthly pulses or explicit lifecycle hooks.

### 6. Header display uses blockoverride

All character-led IO panels use `blockoverride` on their header template to display the appointed character (read from the country variable via `GetPlayer.MakeScope.GetVariable(...).GetCharacter`). Never use the vanilla `GetLeaderCountry.GetGovernment.GetRulerOrRegent` accessor chain.

### 7. All TV IOs have unique = no

Each country pursuing the relevant victory path creates its own independent IO instance. The `international_organization:type_name` scope link is **invalid** for non-unique types. Always use `every_international_organizations_member_of = { limit = { international_organization_type = international_organization_type:<io_type> } ... }`.
