The same pattern can happen while iterating character/location lists; variable-map reads belong on the scope that owns the map. |
| advisory | scope | variable_map | Using an inflated ordered_key_in_variable_map max value such as 10000 to process every current key | Use every_key_in_variable_map with a found/stop flag when all keys may need inspection, or set ordered_key_in_variable_map max only to an exact known upper bound that cannot exceed the current map size. | EU5 logs a Script system error when ordered_key_in_variable_map max is larger than the current key list, even though it reports that it capped max at the list size. Do not use a huge max as a harmless 'iterate all' shortcut. |
| advisory | gui | image | Using CSS-like GUI image fittype = contain | Use a vanilla-supported EU5 fittype such as centercrop, fill, start, or end, or omit fittype when no fit transform is needed. | EU5 GUI fittype names are not CSS object-fit names. The location-window dynamic test icon used contain and failed static GUI conversion at startup. |
| lint | localization | gui | Engineering Department support building localization keys used as square-bracket game concept links | Use plain localization substitution such as $tv_wonder_labor_camp$ for building names, or register a real game concept before using [key|E]. | These support buildings have normal localization keys, not game_concept definitions. In GUI-bound localized text, [tv_wonder_labor_camp|E] is parsed as a data-system function and logs Could not find data system function. |
| lint | localization | building | Using [GetBuilding('building_key').GetName] inside localization text | Use the localization helper [ShowBuildingTypeName('building_key')] for building-type display names. | EU5 localization exposes ShowBuildingTypeName/ShowBuildingTypeNameWithNoTooltip helpers for building types. GetBuilding('key').GetName is not a valid localization data function and causes GUI-bound localized text parsing to fail. |
| advisory | localization | generic_actions | Using datacontext-dependent Country.MakeScope or container-dependent SCOPE.sCountry('actor') reads inside generic action title/description localization | Generic action description loc may be prefetched with no Country datacontext and no script-scope Container. Preserve player-country dynamic features by using a context-independent global binding such as Player.MakeScope, or move non-player-scope dynamic lines to a GUI widget/tooltip with an explicit datacontext. Do not replace the dynamic feature with static text without user approval. | action_button descriptions may be localized once through a contextless prefetch path and again through the real action hover. Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...) and SCOPE.sCountry('actor').MakeScope.GetVariable(...) produced continuous PdxDataFetchLocalizedData errors even while the visible tooltip rendered the selected wonder correctly. |
| lint | gui | any | GetVariable('tv_governed_area').GetArea.GetName — navigating from a scope variable that stores an Area to call .GetName directly via .GetArea | GetVariable(...).GetArea is not a valid EU5 GUI accessor. To display an area name from a scope variable, store a location from the area as a separate character variable (e.g. tv_governed_area_capital), then use GetVariable('tv_governed_area_capital').GetLocation.GetArea.GetNameWithNoTooltip. Location.GetArea.GetName IS valid when the Location type is a proper GUI-layer typed object (e.g. from GetLocation on a typed view). | The accessor .GetArea exists on the Location GUI type but not on scope variable wrappers returned by GetVariable(). Chain: GetVariable('loc_var').GetLocation.GetArea.GetNameWithNoTooltip works because GetLocation returns a typed Location object. Pattern rule narrowed to only flag GetVariable(...).GetArea. to allow Location.GetArea. chains. |
| lint | event | character | kill_character = yes | Use kill_character = this when in character scope (e.g. inside var:name ?= { }), or kill_character = scope:char_ref from any scope. kill_character = yes is invalid — EU5 expects a character scope reference, not a boolean. | kill_character takes a character scope value, not a boolean. kill_character = yes causes PostValidate error: target scope type boolean, expected character. |
| lint | event | character | remove_trait = blind | remove_trait = trait:blind — always prefix the trait name with trait: for remove_trait and add_trait effects. Note: has_trait = blind (no prefix) is correct for trigger checks. | remove_trait effect requires trait: prefix (remove_trait = trait:blind). has_trait trigger does NOT use the prefix (has_trait = blind). Mixing them causes: remove_trait effect [ target: field not set ]. |
| advisory | localization | any | none_available_msg_key localization value missing @trigger_no! icon prefix | In the localization YAML, the VALUE of the none_available_msg_key string must begin with @trigger_no! — e.g. "@trigger_no! No characters available." The key name itself has no prefix requirement. | Engine logs: Key X doesn't exist or doesn't start with trigger_no icon. The @trigger_no! inline icon must be the first token in the localization value string. |
| advisory | generic_actions | character | Using column = { data = character_noble } or another selector attribute column that is not defined in common/attribute_columns | Use a defined attribute column such as character_info or character_artist, or add a real common/attribute_columns entry plus localization. Keep eligibility filtering in visible/enabled. | The selector column data token is an attribute-column id, not a free label for the filtered target type. An undefined column can surface as a startup localization fallback such as character_noble: "Character noble". |
| lint | script | any | Placing character trait definitions in common/character_traits/ (e.g. src/in_game/common/character_traits/my_traits.txt) | Place trait files in common/traits/ — the correct EU5 directory: src/in_game/common/traits/my_traits.txt | EU5 loads character traits from common/traits/ not common/character_traits/. Files in the wrong directory are silently ignored and all has_trait checks against those trait IDs produce 'Invalid database object' errors at PostValidate. |
| lint | script | location | construct_building = { ... cost_multiplier = N ... } without cost_multiplier_reason | When construct_building uses cost_multiplier, also provide cost_multiplier_reason = <loc key>. For event-funded free construction, vanilla commonly uses cost_multiplier_reason = "game_concept_event". | EU5 logs a script system error when construct_building has a cost multiplier but no reason key: 'No reason given for the cost multiplier in construct_building effect'. 2026-08-02: upgraded from advisory (documented but unchecked) to lint after this exact mistake recurred — the CMM skip_ceremony ritual-annex construction added 54 new construct_building calls (`cost_multiplier = 0 instant = yes`, one per generic wonder) in tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt without the reason key, even though the identical cost_multiplier = 0 free-construction pattern already existed correctly (with cost_multiplier_reason = "game_concept_event") in the adjacent tv_wonder_ceremony_effects.txt file in the SAME codebase. Added check_construct_building_cost_multiplier_reason, using the existing _find_matching_brace helper to extract each construct_building = { ... } block (handles both the single-line and multi-line forms both used in this codebase) and flag any block containing cost_multiplier but not cost_multiplier_reason. Unit-tested directly against synthetic single-line and multi-line bad/good snippets before trusting it, then verified 0 false positives against the full 288-file repo. |
| advisory | modifier | country | Using capital_country_modifier for a building that can be finalized or created outside the capital, expecting country-wide effects everywhere | Keep local effects in building modifier/raw_modifier; for noncapital national effects use Country Auto modifiers gated by owned buildings, or require the building to be in the capital. | capital_country_modifier is explicitly applied to the country only if the building is built in the capital. Engineering Department wonders may be sited outside the capital, so their final buildings carry national/base/ritual country effects only as display data; actual national effects come from building-gated Country Auto modifiers. |
| advisory | localization | any | text = "LOC_KEY" in a GUI file when LOC_KEY is not defined in main_menu/localization | Define the key in every supported localization file, correct the key to an existing one, or use raw_text for literal display strings. | GUI text fields are localized. A typo such as TV_RM_TARGET_STEAM_ENGINE when only TV_RM_TARGET_IRON_WORKING exists logs 'Unlocalized text' at load. |
| advisory | script | country | Defining a generic action without listing it in common/generic_action_ai_lists | Add every generic action id to at least one generic_action_ai_lists file. Use the list potential to restrict evaluation to countries that can use that feature; player-only actions may still set ai_will_do = { add = -100 }. | Unlisted generic actions are put into the global AI list and the engine logs a performance warning: 'Action X is not explicitly listed in an ai list!'. |
| advisory | scope | country | A monthly/yearly/on_action helper manually simulates a generic action by calling action/building eligibility helpers that evaluate code expecting literal scope:actor. | Put AI behavior on the generic action itself with ai_tick, ai_will_do, and a generic_action_ai_lists entry. If an execution-only pulse must call action-style helpers, create the exact event targets they require before evaluating them; a custom saved scope name is not a substitute for scope:actor. | The generated tv_io_establishment_ai_progress_effect saved the country as tv_io_establishment_ai_actor, then entered capital and called location_and_owner_can_build. That helper invokes can_build_building for the location and owner, causing the building allow block to read scope:actor. Because the monthly pulse did not define the literal actor event target, the engine logged Undefined event target 'actor' and unset scope errors every month. The current flow keeps monthly pulses to requirement/cache updates and lets AI use the generated build-HQ/establish-IO actions. |
| advisory | modifier | country | Defining a mod price key without a matching <price_key>_cost_modifier modifier type | For every mod-defined price key, add <price_key>_cost_modifier in main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions with category = country, and localize the price key plus MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME/DESC entries. | EU5 automatically looks for a cost modifier type named <price_key>_cost_modifier for each price. If it is absent, the engine logs 'Missing modifier type for price'. |
| advisory | modifier | country | Defining a custom goods key without the matching auto-requested modifier type family | For every custom goods ID, add ban_exports_of_<good>, ban_imports_of_<good>, local_<good>_output_modifier, global_<good>_output_modifier, can_extract_<good>, <good>_impacts_inflation, <good>_used_for_minting, and global_<good>_pop_demand in main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions, matching vanilla goods categories and boolean/percent flags. | EU5 automatically tries to use these modifier type names for every goods database entry. A custom goods key without the family logs 'Modifier type definition <name> must exist in DB'. |
| advisory | script | any | Defining a custom goods key with a vanilla goods color such as color = goods_masonry | Create a unique named color for the custom good in main_menu/common/named_colors, then reference it from the goods definition, e.g. color = goods_tv_wonder_materials. | EU5 logs 'Goods <custom_good> has same color as <vanilla_good>' when two goods resolve to the same named color/value. Custom goods should have their own goods_<id> color key. |
| lint | modifier | any | Defining a custom modifier type without a matching common/modifier_icons entry | For every mod-defined modifier type, add a same-key entry in main_menu/common/modifier_icons with at least positive = "gfx/interface/icons/modifier_types/<vanilla_or_custom_icon>.dds". Reuse a close vanilla icon when no custom asset is needed. | EU5 loads modifier icon mappings separately from modifier_type_definitions. A valid custom modifier type can still log 'Missing Icon for Modifier : <name>' at startup until common/modifier_icons maps the key to a DDS. 2026-08-02: upgraded from advisory to lint after scripts/validate.py's check_modifier_type_definition_loc_and_icon_coverage was added (WARNING severity, since a missing icon does not block loading, unlike missing MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME/DESC localization which is an ERROR in the same check) — see missing_localization_for_new_modifier_price_auto_modifier_definitions. |
| needs_parser | script | country | Defining a generic action without a PERFORM_<action_id>_ACTION message type or all matching SETUP/LOG/MAP localization keys | Add a PERFORM_<action_id>_ACTION block to scripts/gen_messagetypes.py TV_ENTRIES, rerun scripts/gen_messagetypes.py, and define SETUP/LOG/MAP localization keys for supported languages. | EU5 expects a message type and its SETUP/LOG/MAP localization trio for generic action notifications. Missing message types log 'Failed to find message type: PERFORM_<action_id>_ACTION'; missing members of the localization trio log the key with 'TODO : Write a desc'. scripts/validate.py cross-checks every generic action against the English and Simplified Chinese localization key sets. |
| lint | localization | any | ROOT.MakeScope.GetVariable('var_name') inside event localization | Use ROOT.GetVariable('var_name') in event localization. Reserve Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...) / Location.MakeScope.GetVariable(...) for GUI object contexts. | In event localization, ROOT is already a script scope. Adding MakeScope produces runtime data errors such as 'Could not find promote for MakeScope' and 'Failed converting statement'. |
| lint | localization | any | custom_description { text = MY_KEY } in common/scripted_effects where MY_KEY exists only in YAML or is absent from common/effect_localization | Register MY_KEY in src/in_game/common/effect_localization with the needed perspective mappings. The mapped localization keys still live in src/main_menu/localization. | EU5 validates scripted-effect custom_description text keys against common/effect_localization at load. YAML-only keys produce 'No effect loc KEY' and PostValidate errors even when the display text exists. |
| lint | localization | any | An effect_localization block maps a *_neg perspective to the same localization key as its positive counterpart | Map global_neg/first_neg/third_neg/global_past_neg/first_past_neg/third_past_neg to distinct negative localization keys, even for static custom_description text. | The engine logs 'Negative and positive version share loc for effect loc KEY' when a negative perspective reuses the positive loc id. For static effect text, generate separate *_negative_text localization keys; the translated text may be simple negative wording, but the loc ids must be distinct. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | Changing an IO type variable inside a scripted_effect with a bare change_variable, expecting callers to show the variable gain or loss | Wrap the IO-scope change_variable in custom_description = { text = <effect_loc_key> value = $value$ ... } and register <effect_loc_key> in common/effect_localization with global/first/third/past/negative perspectives. Add matching main_menu localization strings for those perspective keys. | Scripted effect calls can otherwise produce empty option/action tooltips even though the IO variable changes. Vanilla uses custom_description plus effect_localization for imperial, celestial, and papal authority so the tooltip shows the signed value while executing the real effect. |
| advisory | localization | any | Using [COUNTRY.GetName] in third/third_past effect localization strings for a custom_description shown from a generic action tooltip | Keep effect localization perspective strings self-contained, e.g. 'Gains $VALUE|+$ #Y $var$#!'; do not assume COUNTRY exists during action hover pre-evaluation. | Generic action hover pre-evaluation may select the third-person effect localization perspective without a COUNTRY promote target. [COUNTRY.GetName] then returns nullptr and logs data errors repeatedly while hovering. |
| advisory | script | location | Event-created artwork uses create_art with only artist/quality and no explicit type/key, expecting a reliable tooltip and artwork name | For event-created named artworks, provide an explicit work_of_art_type and key with matching main_menu localization. If the type depends on the artist, branch with visible if/else_if create_art blocks so the real effect tooltip renders. | tv_arts_exhibition.406 created a work in the capital but the option tooltip was empty and the resulting artwork had no name. Vanilla named artworks pair create_art with both type = work_of_art_type:<type> and key = <loc_key>. Visible if/else_if branches can render the real create_art tooltip while still selecting the appropriate art type by artist_type. |
| lint | script | international_organization | Defining a monthly_effect block on a TV international_organization type | Do not use IO monthly_effect blocks. Keep visible typed-variable monthly arithmetic in the variable's monthly_change, and move maintenance/completion side effects to registered country monthly pulses or explicit lifecycle hooks. | TV IO monthly_effect blocks have severe performance costs even when the body is hidden. IO variable monthly_change remains the correct place for visible variable breakdowns; side-effect maintenance should be routed through country pulses that first do cheap membership/leader checks. |
| lint | gui | international_organization | Trying to hide an IO parliament tab with blockoverride "organization_parliament_tab_visible" | Set has_parliament = no on the international_organization type. Keep law requires_vote behavior as a separate design decision. | The vanilla organization panel comments out the would-be parliament visible block and binds the tab directly to InternationalOrganizationsView.GetInternationalOrganization.HasParliament. There is no block to override. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | IO law votes routed through parliament without an implemented special status that has special_status_power | For uses_parliament_for_law_votes IOs, add a special status in common/international_organization_special_statuses, list it in special_statuses_implemented, give it special_status_power, define <status>_can_participate_in_parliament with category = internationalorganization, and enable that modifier in the parliament type. | The policy_vote resolution requires country_combined_special_status_power(scope:recipient) > 0 when uses_parliament_for_law_votes_trigger is active. Without a participating special status, the law debate can open but the IO parliament page has no voter group to show. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | A custom IO parliament special status relies only on auto_bestowal_trigger or same-tick on_joined state to appear | For custom IO statuses that drive parliament voting or UI power, grant/remove the status directly in the action or lifecycle effect that creates, invites, promotes, demotes, or removes the country. Do not add recurring monthly refreshes for special-status repair. | HRE free city actions explicitly call international_organization_add_special_status from the HRE scope; free_city has an empty auto_bestowal_trigger and does not need a monthly repair pass. Diplomatic Alliance members could wait through monthly pulses without showing tv_alliance_* special statuses, but starting a law vote refreshed visible status/power state. The minimal TV pattern is to grant the leader's advocator status at creation and grant each joining country member status in on_joined, without recurring special-status maintenance or extra parliament-seat repair. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | IO law votes routed through parliament for an organization that has neither a parliament_seat nor an owned location | For uses_parliament_for_law_votes IOs with no IO-owned locations, set the IO variable parliament_seat to a valid location such as the leader country's capital before any law vote can be proposed. | Vanilla call_parliament_for_law_change unconditionally calls set_parliament_location = scope:parliament_location. For non-HRE IOs it first uses parliament_seat, then a proposer-owned location that is owned by the IO, then a random IO-owned location. Diplomatic Alliance has no IO-owned locations, so law votes reached set_parliament_location with a null target until parliament_seat was set from the leader capital. |
| advisory | scope | international_organization | Calling an IO-scoped helper with IO-only triggers directly inside create_international_organization's effect block | Put IO-scoped initialization in the IO type's on_creation/on_joined hooks, or call it only after switching to a saved IO scope that is verified to exist. Keep create_international_organization blocks free of helpers that assume root is the new IO during pre-evaluation. | Generic-action tooltip/effect pre-evaluation can walk a create_international_organization block before the new IO is a stable root. An IO helper that starts with international_organization_type then logs 'Expected international_organization, but got country'. Diplomatic Alliance fixed this by relying on IO on_creation/on_joined and removing the direct helper call from the creation block. |
| advisory | modifier | international_organization | Defining a custom IO special status without the matching auto-requested parliament modifier type pair | For every custom IO special status key that can be implemented on an international organization, define <status>_can_participate_in_parliament as boolean and <status>_agenda_impact as percent in main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions with game_data category = internationalorganization. | EU5 automatically tries to resolve these modifier type names for custom IO special statuses; missing definitions produce startup DB assertions such as 'Modifier type definition <status>_can_participate_in_parliament must exist in DB'. |
| advisory | scope | international_organization | Using scope:recipient inside IO policy allow/on_activate/on_deactivate to read or mutate the IO | Use the current IO root directly in policy allow/on_activate/on_deactivate. Reserve scope:recipient for documented country-root policy AI math blocks such as wants_this_policy_bias and wants_propose_policy. | Generated Diplomatic Alliance laws wrapped IO variable checks and tier changes in scope:recipient in execution blocks. At runtime those blocks had no recipient event target, causing repeated 'Undefined event target recipient' and unset-scope errors. |
| needs_parser | scope | international_organization | Using direct `leader_country = { ... }` inside IO policy on_activate/on_deactivate effect chains | Keep IO variable writes on the current IO root, but enter the leader country with `leader_country ?= { ... }` for mirrored country state or side effects. | The law browser and policy tooltips can walk activation/deactivation effect chains before a leader country is available. Direct leader_country scope entry logs `Event target link 'leader_country' returned an invalid object`; the optional link safely no-ops when null. |
| needs_parser | scope | international_organization | Using scope:recipient inside IO policy AI math without first checking exists = scope:recipient | In wants_this_policy_bias, wants_propose_policy, wants_keep_policy, reasons_to_join, and diplomatic_capacity_cost, guard direct scope:recipient reads with exists = scope:recipient or use optional scope:recipient ?= blocks. | Although the law readme documents recipient = IO for policy AI math, the engine can pre-evaluate these maths without a recipient event target. Unguarded scope:recipient reads caused repeated 'Undefined event target recipient' and unset-scope errors in generated Diplomatic Alliance laws. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | An IO policy member modifier is meant to affect only the leader or another subset, but the modifier block has no potential_trigger filter. | Use an appropriate member filter. For visible leader-only effects that must stack with other law modifiers, keep country_modifier but guard the filter for law browsing: potential_trigger = { OR = { NOT = { exists = scope:recipient } is_leader_of_international_organization = scope:recipient } }. Use leader_modifier/non_leader_modifier only when replacement semantics are intended. Vanilla policy_vote automatically adds scope:vote.modifier_utility(scope:actor) as POLICY_MODIFIER_UTILITY, so every voting member evaluates unfiltered member modifiers as personal policy utility. | Governor's House subject-chain members voted on overlord administration country modifiers. Some subject tooltips showed extreme 'Policy Modifier Utility' support for particular laws. A recipient-guarded potential_trigger keeps the additive country modifiers and their automatic utility on the supreme overlord while still displaying during ordinary law browsing. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | Representing a visible additive leader-only IO law effect as country_modifier = { potential_trigger = { is_leader_of_international_organization = scope:recipient } ... } without a no-recipient display branch | Use country_modifier = { potential_trigger = { OR = { NOT = { exists = scope:recipient } is_leader_of_international_organization = scope:recipient } } ... } when the leader-only policy effect must remain an additive country modifier. The no-recipient branch is for ordinary law browsing; the leader check still applies when a vote/application recipient exists. | The normal law browser may evaluate modifier display without a populated scope:recipient, causing an unguarded filtered country_modifier package to collapse to 'No Effects'. leader_modifier has leader-country target semantics, but the law readme says it replaces previous leader_modifier blocks, so additive multi-law packages should stay country_modifier with a display-safe filter. |
| lint | scope | country | Comparing leader_country to this inside any_international_organizations_member_of in a generic_action_ai_list | The AI-list potential root is the evaluating country, while this becomes the IO inside the iterator. Compare with root instead: exists = leader_country; leader_country = root. | Inside the IO membership iterator, this is the international organization. leader_country = this compares a country to an international_organization and logs a type-mismatch error. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | Adding IO parliament issues for a special status without any valid parliament_agenda for that special status | Add at least one common/parliament_agendas entry with type = international_organization and special_status = <status>. Keep its potential/allow valid for the IO so the parliament button has a special-status agenda to resolve. | IO parliaments rely on special-status agendas as well as issues. Without a valid agenda for the participating special status, the UI can report that no special status wants to propose an issue even when parliament_issues exist. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | All idle IO parliament issues for a custom special status have non-positive wants_this_parliament_issue_bias in ordinary founding conditions | Give at least one normal idle parliament_issue for the participating special status a positive base wants_this_parliament_issue_bias after normal modifiers, while keeping potential/allow/selectable_for valid. | The vanilla call_organization_parliament issue chooser reports that no special status has issues to bring when it cannot surface any issue. Diplomatic Alliance issues with base -25 and only opinion-based bonuses could leave a new or neutral alliance with no positively desired idle issue, even though the issue definitions otherwise passed potential/allow/selectable_for. |
| advisory | script | international_organization | A single-member IO relies on a generic member special status to propose IO parliament issues | Do not rely on vanilla call_organization_parliament for single-member support-token meetings. The mechanic needs a different design before custom support meetings are enabled. | The vanilla call_organization_parliament picker builds its issue list through every_possible_parliament_issue and reports 'no special status wants to propose an issue' when it cannot surface one. propose_parliament_issue then initializes support from the issue special status and marks the proposer as voting yes. In a founder-only IO, self-backed support issues are brittle even with a leader status. |
| lint | scope | country | Reusable country milestone trigger uses root inside a nested market iterator | Use prev inside nested iterators so the target remains the currently checked country when the trigger is called from situation->every_country. | GUI ShowTriggerConditions(PlayerScope.Self) can pass because root is the player country, while the monthly situation checker calls the same trigger from a situation root. In any_market_with_merchants, prev points back to the iterated country. |
| advisory | gui | any | Putting text_single with multiline text inside an hbox elastic column whose width is unconstrained (for example size = { -1 H }) | Give the text area an explicit width or maximum width. For paragraph-like content, wrap it in a fixed-width widget/card and use text_multi with max_width and autoresize, as vanilla IO GUI does. | In Jomini GUI, multiline text can contribute its natural text width back into hbox layout. A right-side size = { -1 H } text column in the Engineering Department proposal row expanded to 446px and pushed its parent vbox from 470px to 548.3px even though the outer card bounds were correct. |
| lint | script | any | Using display/alias-like goods names such as candles or luxury_cloth in production method upkeep | Use real goods database IDs: beeswax for candle upkeep, fine_cloth for luxury cloth upkeep. | Production method input keys must be exact goods IDs. Invalid goods tokens inside common/production_methods can load as 'Unexpected token' errors, as seen with candles and luxury_cloth. |
| needs_parser | script | any | Production method base input/output values produce profit above 30% | Compute recipe value from goods default_market_price and keep base profit below 30%, usually by slightly increasing inputs or reducing output/default output price. | EU5 logs a startup error when a production_method's base profit exceeds 30%, e.g. tv_wonder_material_dispatch_point_production at 33.33%. A recipe worth 5 output needs input value greater than 3.8462 to be just under 30% profit. |
| lint | script | building | Using building levels multiplied by nominal employment size to determine how many workers a building actually employs | For actual employment, scope into the building object with ordered_buildings_in_location/random_buildings_in_location and read building_employed_amount. If downstream variables are stored as people rather than pop-size units, multiply the value by 1000. `location_building_level(building_type:X)` remains valid when the intended value is nominal completed building levels, such as the Engineering Department's current worksite scale. | location_building_level reports completed levels/capacity, not current staffing. For labor-sensitive mechanics such as Engineering Department construction, use completed levels only for nominal scale, then compare against total_effective_building_levels or building_employed_amount for labor-sensitive effective staffing. |
| advisory | script | building | Custom generic-action building buttons rely only on can_build_building and do not repeat the building max-level / in-construction cap check | Add an explicit trigger shared by the action allow, select_trigger visible/enabled, and final effect guard. For per-country building caps, use total_building_levels_including_construction:<building> so queued levels count. For one-per-location selectors, also filter existing buildings and any_buildings_in_location with building_levels_under_construction >= 1. | Building max_levels can stop normal construction but a custom IO/generic-action button can still spend its scripted price unless its action and effect repeat the cap logic. Re-check inside the effect because generic action UI pre-evaluation and stale target lists can otherwise charge the player for a no-op. |
| advisory | script | building | Using has_building = building_type:X as the gate for a mechanic that should unlock only after X is completed | Use has_building_with_at_least_one_level = X for completed-building gates. Keep separate under-construction checks such as building_levels_under_construction >= 1 when the mechanic needs to block duplicate queues. | The IO establishment headquarters cache used has_building on the capital, so starting construction made the step-3 appoint-chief action available before the headquarters finished. The completed gate must require an actual built level. |
| lint | script | building | Passing a typed building_type:<id> value into location_and_owner_can_build | Pass the bare building id, e.g. location_and_owner_can_build = { building_type = tv_conquest_headquarters }. The scripted trigger adds building_type: internally. | location_and_owner_can_build expands its parameter as building_type:$building_type$ for value, can_build_building, and owner can_build_building checks. Supplying building_type:<id> as the parameter produces building_type:building_type:<id> and logs 'More than one colon in event target link'. |
| needs_parser | script | building | A while loop checks location_building_level and then tries to drive construct_building instant = yes or change_building_level_in_location until the level catches up | Do not wait for location_building_level to change inside the same effect. Use fixed if / else_if branches or a bounded script-value delta so the effect runs once and never depends on a 0-day build task updating synchronously. | Wonder selection/finalization previously used while loops that assumed instant construction updated the location building level during the same effect. That can deadlock or spin forever because the engine only creates a queued task. Treat instant construction as asynchronous for script control flow. |
| advisory | script | building | Using building on_built as the only completion signal for a mechanic that also upgrades existing building levels | Use on_construction_ended to directly mark the active auxiliary-building ritual finished and run the normal ritual completion effect. Do not depend on on_built for upgrades, and do not require location_building_level to have updated inside the construction-ended hook. | Engineering Department style-2 ritual annex completion relied on on_built. Fresh annex construction completed, but expansion reused the same annex building as a higher level, so the ritual needed construction-ended direct completion. |
| advisory | scope | international_organization | Calling a country-scoped scripted_trigger from an IO variable monthly_change when that trigger depends on root, prev, caller-specific saved scopes, or country variables | Write the monthly_change condition in IO scope, or create a separate IO-scoped trigger. Use leader_country ?= { ... } for country state, compare IO variables directly against leader_country.var:X from the IO scope, and inline ownership/state checks whose prev/root chain must point at the leader country. | monthly_change evaluates from the international organization/variable context. A nested leader_country block does not make root inside a called scripted_trigger become the country, and caller-sensitive prev/root chains can differ from the same trigger's country-pulse behavior. In Engineering Department survey progress, reusing tv_wonder_survey_active_trigger inside the IO variable monthly_change silently prevented the visible IO progress variable from gaining monthly progress; inlining the active-survey conditions under leader_country preserved monthly_change as the tooltip-visible source. |
| lint | script | any | Filtering random_list branches with modifier = { factor = 0 ... } | Use trigger = { ... } inside each weighted random_list branch. Vanilla random_list branch eligibility uses trigger blocks; use NOT = { var:current ?= N } there when a reroll must exclude the current result. | Engineering Department estate-demand rerolls used modifier factor zero in random_list branches. A player could bribe a demand into the artwork-loss branch and repeated same-day rerolls kept resolving to that same visible demand. Replacing branch modifiers with trigger filters and excluding the current demand makes the random pool behave like vanilla and prevents visible no-op rerolls. |
| advisory | script | any | Using common/music_player_tracks as if it can load raw audio files or define country/event/war trigger conditions for automatic music playback | Register only Wwise event keys there, with optional composer/performer/soloist metadata and matching music_player localization. Use verified GUI/audio hooks such as Audio_PlayEvent(..., 'music_manager') or hardcoded on_actions for conditional music behavior; do not invent trigger fields inside music_player_tracks. | The official type note says new songs are added in Wwise and registered by event key. Vanilla entries contain no trigger/chance/category fields beyond metadata, and the in-game music player plays registered track objects via GUI bindings. EU5 does expose Audio_PlayEvent to send a Wwise event to music_manager from GUI, and on_war_declared exists as a script hook, but current references do not expose EU4-style songs.txt conditional rules or a normal effect that directly plays a named music-player track. |
| advisory | script | location | Applying vanilla promote_religion's full target condition to a regional priority heuristic without checking the mechanic's design intent | Use the full vanilla OR when mirroring cabinet-action province eligibility. For Governor's House autonomous conversion priority, use only the share of owned locations whose dominant_religion differs from the governing country's religion. | Vanilla promote_religion can still work on minority non-state-religion pops when local_religious_unity is below 100%. The Governor's House design intentionally ignores that cleanup case so regional governors only prioritize locations where the non-state religion is still dominant. |
| advisory | gui | international_organization | Filtering InternationalOrganizationsView.GetMemberItems by comparing MemberTypeItem.GetName to a special-status display helper | Use real MemberTypeItem predicates such as MemberTypeItem.IsAllMembers for the outer group, then apply country-row filters inside MemberTypeItem.GetCountries. | The display-name comparison against ShowSpecialStatusName('tv_alliance_member_status') hid every Diplomatic Alliance member row. MemberTypeItem.GetName is not a reliable selector for custom special-status groups in this panel. |
| advisory | gui | international_organization | Checking an IO member-row country with IsIOLeaderCountry(Country.Self), or comparing Country.Self directly to GetLeaderCountry without .Self | Compare matching GUI objects, e.g. Not(ObjectsEqual(Country.Self, InternationalOrganizationsView.GetInternationalOrganization.GetLeaderCountry.Self)). | IsIOLeaderCountry(Country.Self) produced FetchData failed in the IO member row context. The original ObjectsEqual without .Self did not hide the leader because it compared different object shapes. |
| advisory | gui | generic_actions | Using enabled = "[UIAction.IsEnabled]" directly inside a custom-panel button without a verified UIAction datacontext | Use an explicit GUI predicate, or keep the conventional action_button/action_button_diamond template only when the widget defines a real left_action and has been verified after a full panel/game reload. | A direct enabled = [UIAction.IsEnabled] produced 'No function for call' / empty FetchData errors in this custom panel. Later apparent errors from normal action-button templates were traced to stale GUI hot reload state, not the template itself. |
| advisory | gui | generic_actions | Passing an IO member-row country action parameter as [Country.Self] | Pass country targets that generic actions read as scope:target through [Country.MakeScope], and place the parameter on the action button so both tooltip/allow evaluation and the click action receive the same scope. Reserve Country.Self for GUI object comparisons such as ObjectsEqual. | The Diplomatic Alliance row button passed [Country.Self] inside left_action, leaving scope:target absent for the generic action. The guarded effect then no-oped silently: no cohesion was spent and no subject was created. |
| advisory | modifier | character | Putting country_cabinet_efficiency inside a character-scoped modifier to lower one cabinet member's efficiency | Use character_cabinet_efficiency for character modifiers. Reserve country_cabinet_efficiency for country modifiers. | country_cabinet_efficiency is a country modifier type and has no effect on a character modifier; the character-scoped equivalent is character_cabinet_efficiency. |
| advisory | gui | texture | Using texture = "[Concatenate('gfx/...', key, '.dds')]" to dynamically select a raw DDS path | Route dynamic images through a GUI function that returns a texture handle, such as GetConceptTexture(...). For arbitrary mod DDS assets, register image-only game concepts like tv_wonder_display_image_<id>, add matching game_concept_* and game_concept_*_desc localization, and build that concept id from a numeric slot id. | In the location-window test, GetConceptTexture(Concatenate(...)) rendered, but nested, suffix-only, and flat Concatenate expressions that returned raw DDS path strings did not render and produced no useful error log. Static texture paths are parsed by the GUI loader, while runtime texture expressions need a texture-like return value rather than a CString path. |
| advisory | gui | modifier | Calling ShowModifierEffect with wonder display modifier ids built by GUI Concatenate(...) without generated static modifier definitions and unreachable script references | Generate matching static display modifiers plus an unreachable always = no script block that references every possible display id through add_country_modifier/add_location_modifier. Use those mirrors for GUI ShowModifierEffect display only; do not route completed wonder grants or scripted-effect tooltip previews through them. | Building modifier blocks and GUI static mirrors carry display-only country/local effects, while country auto modifiers provide the actual national effects from owned final buildings. Scripted effect previews are a separate parser path: add_country_modifier validates against static/country modifiers, not common/auto_modifiers; the current wonder flow avoids modifier-list previews for auto-driven country effects. |
| advisory | gui | trigger | Calling ShowTriggerConditionsForScope with a trigger name built by GUI Concatenate(...) from a numeric display id, but only defining the underlying scripted_trigger under its string wonder key; or aliasing straight to a real gameplay trigger that carries extra guard conditions the display context already guarantees | Generate a thin alias scripted_trigger named with the numeric display id (e.g. tv_wonder_display_<id>_base_site_rules_trigger) so the Concatenate-built name resolves. Unlike ShowModifierEffect, scripted_trigger/scripted_effect names are not a separate database lookup, so no unreachable always = no reference block is needed for these aliases. Do not simply delegate the alias to the real gameplay trigger (tv_wonder_location_meets_<key>_base_site_rules_trigger = yes) if that trigger carries a guard condition (such as unique wonders' this = location:<site>) that is always true in the display's own context — a location tooltip already IS that location, so showing that condition as a row is redundant noise. Instead, re-embed just the site-rule condition list (the same trigger_conditions(...)/site_trigger_lines_for_wonder(...) call the real trigger uses) directly in the display alias, omitting the always-true guard. | tv_wonder_location_meets_<key>_base_site_rules_trigger is already the location-scoped (unwrapped) form of the Engineering Department's any_owned_location-wrapped site rule check, so a GUI block that already runs in LocationView.GetLocation.MakeScope.Self can reuse its condition list directly via ShowTriggerConditionsForScope(Concatenate('tv_wonder_display_', Concatenate(idString, '_base_site_rules_trigger')), LOCATION_SCOPE) without any_owned_location. For unique wonders specifically, keep the this = location:<site> guard only on the real gameplay trigger (still needed so the wonder can only be queued/built at its designated site); the display alias should call trigger_conditions(wonder, mechanics, 1) directly rather than delegating, so the redundant 'is <site>' row does not render. |
| advisory | gui | sizing | Using TooltipTextBlock for a fallback/empty-state line next to a sibling TooltipRequirementsList or text_single that has blockoverride row_size / field_text_format sizing, expecting both to render at the same height | TooltipTextBlock (vanilla type in main_menu_cooltip_types.gui) wraps its text in a fixed vbox { margin = { @tooltip_inner_margin @tooltip_inner_margin } } (10px each side) around a text_multi_template textbox, and exposes no blockoverride for font size or row height. blockoverride row_size / field_text_format on a sibling TooltipRequirementsList does not reach it. When a mutually-exclusive fallback line must match a sized row's height (e.g. an effect list vs. its own "no effect" line), replace TooltipTextBlock with a plain text_single using the same fontsize and an explicit size, not the templated tooltip type. | Observed in src/in_game/gui/location_window.gui: the ritual TooltipRequirementsList was resized to 13pt/22px rows to match the country/local modifier columns, but the mutually-exclusive ritual_no_effect_visible TooltipTextBlock stayed visibly taller until it was swapped for a text_single with fontsize = 13 and size = { -1 22 }. |
| advisory | modifier | country | Using add_country_modifier with a modifier id defined only in common/auto_modifiers | Keep Country Auto modifiers in common/auto_modifiers for actual automatic effects. For tooltip previews or scripted effect lists, emit real non-modifier effect lines or omit the modifier-list preview. Do not pass auto modifier ids to add_country_modifier, and do not restore finalization-time static grants/removals for building-gated wonder effects. | Country Auto modifiers and static country modifiers are separate databases. A Country Auto modifier may have category = country, but add_country_modifier still cannot use its id. |
| advisory | localization | modifier | Emitting a common/auto_modifiers id without matching AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> localization | Generate AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> for every emitted auto modifier, including hidden hide_effects companions such as *_unscaled. | Hidden Country Auto modifiers still load as modifier database entries and the localization system requests their AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME keys during startup. |
| lint | localization | modifier | Emitting a common/static_modifiers id without matching STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> localization in every supported main-menu language | Generate or add STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> for every static modifier id, including generated display-only families used by ShowModifierEffect. | Static modifiers load as modifier database entries and the localization system requests their STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME keys during startup or GUI display. scripts/validate.py checks static modifiers in both in_game and main_menu common/static_modifiers against all supported main-menu localization languages. |
| advisory | modifier | any | Using EU5 1.3 *_efficiency cost-style modifier keys while the project/reference data target EU5 1.2 | Use the EU5 1.2 *_cost modifier key. When rolling back from 1.3, invert the value sign: +x% efficiency becomes -x% cost, while -x% efficiency becomes +x% cost. | The current reference files define 1.2 cost-style keys and do not define the migrated 1.3 efficiency keys. EU5 1.3 replaced several of these with good-colored percent efficiency variants; changing direction without sign inversion reverses the intended gameplay effect. |
| lint | modifier | country | Writing subject_loyalty as a fractional percent-like value such as 0.10 | Write subject_loyalty as loyalty points, e.g. 10 for +10 loyalty. Vanilla advances use values such as subject_loyalty = 5 even though the modifier type displays decimals. | The modifier definition has decimals=2 but no percent=yes, and vanilla advances grant whole loyalty points. A 0.x value is effectively a tiny fraction of a loyalty point, not a 10% or +10 loyalty bonus. |
| advisory | performance | international_organization | Generating one market every_export/every_import route scan per good when the mechanic needs all goods from matching routes | Scan each route once, save the route good with `traded_goods = { save_scope_as = ... }`, and use that goods scope as a variable_map key or list item. Keep per-good static branches only for effects that require a static database id, such as `add_temporary_demand type = demand:*`. | Trade route scopes can expose the current good as a scope. The Trade Chain bargaining-demand accumulator now stores demand by slot maps keyed by goods scope, while final temporary-demand application still dispatches to generated static demand ids because no dynamic `type = demand:<scope>` pattern is verified. |
| advisory | scope | country | Reading `scope:recipient` or an unset actor variable directly inside country interaction accept scoring or a target-mutating effect before the select_trigger has chosen a country | Guard recipient-dependent reads and mutations with `exists = scope:recipient`. Initialize actor-owned variables at the feature creation lifecycle point, and gate nullable value reads with `has_variable` before using `scope:actor.var:<name>` in `accept`. | Country interaction accept/effect paths can be evaluated from panel buttons before the selector populates recipient. Direct reads then report wrong-type `none`, unset variables, or unset `var` scopes while the player is only looking at the country picker. |
| advisory | scope | international_organization | Creating custom monthly variables, cooldowns, or date stamps only to know how long a country has belonged to an international organization | Use the native country-scoped `years_in_international_organization = { international_organization = <io scope> value >= N }` trigger, or the script value form `country.years_in_international_organization(io)` for scoring. | EU5 already tracks country membership tenure per IO instance. In IO member iterators, `root` or `prev` can point the tenure check back to the current IO, avoiding extra monthly state and cleanup work. |
| advisory | event | events | Defining hidden country events without a `title = ...` field | Give every event, including hidden tick/executor events, a localized title key. The title can be technical if the event is never shown. | EU5 validates the event database schema before considering whether an event is hidden. Hidden monthly tick or executor events still need a title field and matching localization to avoid startup errors. |
| needs_parser | scope | generic_actions | Using `save_scope_as` inside a select_trigger `visible`/`enabled` block, or inside an `effect`'s `if`/`limit` trigger body, to stash a scope for a later comparison in that same trigger context | Use `save_temporary_scope_as` for any scope save performed inside a trigger-evaluated block (select_trigger visible/enabled, allow, potential, or any `limit = { ... }` body). Reserve `save_scope_as` for saves performed directly inside an effect body (siblings of `limit`, not inside it), where the scope needs to persist to sibling/later effect lines. | The engine's trigger parser does not register `save_scope_as` as a valid trigger type at all; only `save_temporary_scope_as` is valid inside trigger evaluation. `save_scope_as` is effect-only. The Trade Chain generator (`scripts/in_game/common/generic_actions/gen_tv_trade_league_actions.py`) used `save_scope_as` inside `chain_previous_save`/`chain_nearest_previous_limits` (limit-block distance comparisons) and `chain_selector_filter` (select_trigger visible/enabled), producing hundreds of duplicate 'Unknown trigger type: save_scope_as' errors on load. Fixed by switching those three helpers to `save_temporary_scope_as`; the one genuine effect-body save (`scope:target = { save_scope_as = tv_trade_chain_candidate_market }` in the action's `effect` block) was left unchanged. |
| advisory | scope | on_action | Gating an `on_pre_winning_war`/`on_ending_war` on_action's effect only on `has_variable`/ownership/site-control checks, treating 'this war-ending on_action fired' as equivalent to 'root won a war' | Add `scope:winner = { this = root }` to the trigger when the gated effect is meant to require an actual win, not just war conclusion. `root` fires for both the winner and loser of the war; `scope:winner`/`scope:loser`/`scope:war` are all available scopes in both on_actions. | Confirmed live instance: `scripts_engineering_department/unique_wonder_ritual_content/alhambra.py:build_on_action_body` set `tv_wonder_alhambra_war_validation = 1` in both `tv_wonder_unique_alhambra_ritual_on_pre_winning_war` and `_on_ending_war` whenever root held Granada, with no winner/loser distinction — a lost war validated the ritual's 'won or ended' war-gate exactly like a won one. Fixed 2026-07 by adding `scope:winner = { this = root }` to both on_actions' triggers. |
| advisory | workflow | any | Trusting a generated wonder ritual/mechanics file at face value after `scripts_engineering_department/unique_wonder_ritual_content/<key>.py` row-set or variable-naming conventions change, instead of re-running every generator registered against it | When error.log reports `Variable 'tv_wonder_<unique_key>_<...>_status' is used but is never set` (or a matching `_favorable_count`/`_narrowed`/`_started`/`_completed`/`_ritual_phase`/`_ritual_row_set_index` variant) for one of the bespoke unique wonders (Dome of the Rock, Bank of Saint George, St. Peter's Basilica), diff the flagged names against the wonder's own content module (`entity_status_var`/`favorable_count_var`/`narrowed_var`/`_stage_var` in `scripts_engineering_department/unique_wonder_ritual_content/_entity_ritual.py` and `<key>.py`) before assuming a runtime bug. Re-run `gen_tv_wonder_ritual_effects.py` (owns `tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt`, `data/generated_files.yaml`); it regenerates `tv_wonder_mechanics_clear_selected_ritual_runtime_effect` from each wonder's live `runtime_variables` list in `data/unique_wonders.yaml` and drops any stale entries left over from a prior naming convention. Do not hand-patch the generated file. | Dome of the Rock, Bank of Saint George, and St. Peter's Basilica were reworked 2026-07 from the shared `_entity_ritual` row-set naming (`sanctuary_access_groups`, `custody_duties`, `sacred_official_candidates`, `apostolic_service_duties`, `charter_options`, `public_credit_pledges`, plus `ritual_row_set_index`/`ritual_phase`) to bespoke short-form naming (`access`, `duty`, `role`, `assigned_role`, `ritual_stage`), and `data/unique_wonders.yaml`'s `ritual.runtime_variables` lists were updated to match — but `src/in_game/common/scripted_effects/tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt` (generated by `gen_tv_wonder_ritual_effects.py`) had not been regenerated since, so its `tv_wonder_mechanics_clear_selected_ritual_runtime_effect` cleanup effect still emitted `remove_variable` calls for the old long-form names. Those names are never set (false 'used but never set' warnings) and, worse, the *real* current-name status/favorable_count/narrowed/started/completed/ritual_stage variables were never cleaned up on ritual reset, leaking state across a ritual restart. Fixed by re-running the generator with no data changes; always re-run generators after editing a content module's naming helpers even when no runtime error has been observed yet. |
| lint | scope | any | Reading `scope:<name>` where `<name>` does not exactly match the string passed to any `save_scope_as = <name>` earlier in the same effect chain (e.g. reading `scope:tv_trade_candidate_market_location` when only `save_scope_as = tv_trade_candidate_market` was ever executed) | Grep the generator for every `save_scope_as = <name>` it emits and use that exact name in every later `scope:<name>` reference, including references made from a nested scope such as `scope:tv_trade_monopoly_leader = { ... }`. Saved scopes are exact-string keyed; a plausible near-miss name (an extra `_location` suffix, a singular/plural mismatch) silently resolves to nothing rather than erroring at generation time. | assign_candidate_to_io_rank runs inside scope:tv_trade_monopoly_leader = { ... }, itself nested inside the every_market_in_world iterator that saved the candidate as tv_trade_candidate_market. The `_location` suffix was never saved anywhere (no STATIC_MARKET_FIELDS entry and no other save_scope_as call produces it), so the representative-market assignment for the Trade League detail page silently wrote an unresolved scope into tv_trade_{prefix}_market_{rank}_{good} instead of the real candidate market. Fixed by correcting the reference to scope:tv_trade_candidate_market and regenerating tv_trade_league_effects.txt. |
| advisory | workflow | any | Treating a `jomini_effect.cpp` 'Variable X is set but is never used' / 'used but is never set' warning as an unfixable false positive and leaving it undocumented once the read/write site is confirmed to be a GUI-only `GetVariable` read, a `variable_map`/`global_variable_map` name that is only ever touched by `add_to_*_variable_map`/`clear_variable_map` (never a literal `set_variable`), or a vanilla-consumed variable name (e.g. `parliament_seat`) | Once source-reading confirms the variable genuinely has a legitimate consumer the engine's static usage tracker cannot see, register it in `data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml` (a literal name in `extra_suppressions`, or a new structured family function in `scripts/in_game/common/on_action/gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py` for anything with more than a couple of instances, e.g. per-advance or per-slot families read straight from the owning data YAML) and regenerate `tv_cmf_suppressions.txt`. Do not hand-add `cmf_suppress` lines to that generated file. |  |
| advisory | workflow | any | Writing a new unique-wonder ritual whose narrative shape is 'choice -> deterministic branch marking a fixed subset of tracked entities at risk -> one retry/incident event offering pay-a-cost-to-fully-resolve vs. accept-narrower-for-free -> threshold-scaled reward', even after removing `random_list` dice and renaming every variable | Design each wonder's state representation, time structure, and decision shape independently from its own `data/unique_wonder_ritual_specs.yaml` design_ir/mechanic_signature, not from a template shared with sibling wonders. When authoring more than one wonder ritual in the same batch, assign each to an isolated agent/context with no visibility into sibling implementations or into scripts_engineering_department/unique_wonder_ritual_content/_entity_ritual.py, so the natural 'copy the pattern already in this file' shortcut is unavailable. Run scripts_engineering_department/audit_unique_wonder_ritual_mechanic_similarity.py after the batch and treat any pair scoring combined_ratio >= 0.15 as a real finding to investigate, not just a number to beat. | Confirmed twice on the same 3-4 wonders: first when Alhambra/Dome of the Rock/Bank of Saint George/St. Peter's Basilica all routed through the shared _entity_ritual.py dice engine (combined_ratio up to ~0.7+), then again 2026-07 when Dome/Bank/St Peter's each got an independent bespoke rewrite that removed the dice but still converged on the same higher-level template (combined_ratio 0.48-0.71). Resolved 2026-07 by a second rewrite of all four wonders (including Alhambra, whose dice-driven _entity_ritual usage was itself a second instance of template reuse), each authored by an isolated subagent with no visibility into siblings: effects-file combined_ratio among Dome/Bank/St Peter's dropped to 0.18-0.32. |
| advisory | workflow | any | Reading scripts_engineering_department/audit_unique_wonder_ritual_mechanic_similarity.py's combined_ratio (effects+triggers together) as a direct proxy for mechanic-shape similarity when every wonder being compared has a very short triggers file (2-3 blocks total) | When a wonder ritual's triggers file is short, most of its content is the mandatory, near-identical site_control_trigger (owns = location:X) and active_trigger (has_variable = tv_wonder_locked / var:tv_wonder_locked ?= <id> / has_variable = tv_wonder_ritual_in_progress) every ritual in the mod must define. On short files this boilerplate can push combined_ratio over the 0.15 gate even when the effects files (the actual mechanic) are genuinely dissimilar. Read the printed effects_ratio and triggers_ratio components separately, not just combined_ratio; judge homogenization primarily from effects_ratio plus a direct read of the mechanic. Adding one genuine, wonder-specific, differently-shaped extra trigger per wonder (vary the shape across wonders too -- a flat comparison for one, an OR-of-AND compound for another -- reusing the same shape across two wonders' 'extra' triggers just recreates the same problem one level up) and wiring it into a real triggered_desc reduces but does not eliminate this artifact on very short files. | Observed while resolving wonder_ritual_choice_branch_retry_reward_template_trap: after the four-agent rewrite, Dome of the Rock <-> St. Peter's Basilica triggers_ratio swung from 0.99 (both had a redundant eligibility_trigger wrapping active_trigger+site_control_trigger) to 0.30 (after removing eligibility_trigger and adding an OR-of-AND compound trigger to Dome) to 0.909 for a different pair, Bank of Saint George <-> St. Peter's Basilica, purely because their two 'extra' triggers were accidentally given the identical AND-of-4-conditions shape. combined_ratio for that pair barely moved (0.300 -> 0.302) despite the swing, confirming the metric is noisy on short files and should not be chased below 0.15 in isolation from the effects-file ratio. |
| advisory | workflow | any | Re-deriving from scratch whether a variable-map name, GUI-only-read variable, or other dynamic-reference identifier flagged in error.log as used-but-never-set/set-but-never-used is a false positive, instead of checking data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml first. | This project has a pre-existing, already-adopted mechanism (data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml + scripts/in_game/common/on_action/gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py) for the whole class of variables that are read/written only through generation-time-static dynamic references (variable_map names, GUI GetVariable/GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap-only reads) the native scanner cannot trace. Check this file before spending time re-deriving why a variable 'looks unused'. But a suppression entry existing is not proof the variable is genuinely a false positive — see [[cmf_suppress_status_is_not_proof_a_variable_reference_is_real]] for the follow-up correction after one suppressed entry (tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id) turned out to be a real duplicate/dead map, not a dynamic-reference case, and five more (the wonder progress-tracking variables) turned out to have zero setters at all. | 2026-07-09: error.log flagged tv_advance_<id>_researched (31 advances, GUI-only read), tv_academy_debate_node_<1-5>_year/index/type (15 vars, GUI-only read), tv_research_open_age_ceiling (GUI-only read), tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id and tv_trade_good_global_consumption/production (global_variable_map names, assumed never literally set_variable'd), tv_trade_chain_demand_slot_1..20 (variable_map names, same reason), and parliament_seat (vanilla-consumed IO parliament-seat location, confirmed against reference_game_files) as used-but-never-set/set-but-never-used. All were added to data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml via new add_locked_advance_researched_suppressions/add_academy_debate_suppressions family functions plus TRADE_EXTRA_VARIABLES/trade-chain-slot-loop extensions and two extra_suppressions singletons, then tv_cmf_suppressions.txt was regenerated, without individually verifying each had a real reader. 2026-07-15: deeper investigation found tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id was actually a dead duplicate of tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_wonder_id (same generator loop, same value, zero readers anywhere) and was deleted rather than re-suppressed — this entry was previously (and incorrectly) recorded as a 'confirmed legitimate dynamic-reference case.' This note previously overwrote mechanic_similarity_audit_short_triggers_file_dominates_combined_ratio's own notes via a duplicate YAML key in that entry (YAML mappings silently keep only the last value for a repeated key) — split into its own entry 2026-07-15. |
| advisory | localization | any | Only using #R/#G color tags (or inventing a custom GUI/widget solution) for localization text emphasis, tabular tooltip alignment, or numeric breakdowns, without checking whether an existing `#tag` already covers the need | Check the Text Format Tag Catalog in docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md section 6.3 before designing new emphasis/layout for event or tooltip text. Confirmed tags: colors #R #G #Y #W #V #L #P #N #X #D #F (plus lowercase #r #g #y); style #bold #italic #weak #high #T #subtle_name; tabular #col #col_t #col_m; and special #TOOLTIP:$BREAKDOWN_TAG$, #indent_newline:N, #trigger_pass/#trigger_fail. All must be closed with #! and (per [[loc_color_tag_adjacent_text]]) separated from adjacent content by a space. | There is no single canonical reference file for the full #tag list in this repo's mirror — the underlying textformatting.gui asset is gfx-adjacent and pruned by scripts/sync_reference.py. The catalog was reconstructed by frequency-counting #[A-Za-z_0-9]+ tokens across all main_menu English localization files and reading surrounding usage context to infer each tag's semantics. |
| needs_parser | on_action | country | A monthly_country_pulse-registered callback (or any per-country on_monthly-style hook) starting straight into a global/expensive scope iteration (every_character, every_country, every_international_organizations_member_of, every_owned_location, nested every_key_in_variable_map, etc.) with no cheap has_variable/has_variable_list membership check first. Because the callback runs for EVERY country every month, an ungated O(k) scan inside it becomes O(countries x k) system-wide, and if k itself scales with world population/character count the callback alone is O(countries x world_size). | Before any nontrivial scope walk inside a per-country monthly callback, gate it behind the cheapest possible existence/membership check for the state the walk actually needs (has_variable_list on a country-scoped list variable is strictly better than a global every_character/every_country scan filtered by a limit, since the list is already bounded to what this country owns). Example fix: tv_leaderboard_monthly_pulse's touring-artist roll used an ungated every_character = { limit = { is_artist=yes has_variable=tv_on_exhibition } } scanning every living character in the world every country every month; replaced with if = { limit = { has_variable_list = tv_dispatched_artists_list } } every_in_list = { variable = tv_dispatched_artists_list ... } since dispatching countries already maintain that list. | Engineering Department, Governor's House, and Academy of Sciences monthly pulses already follow the correct has_variable-gate-first pattern and are good reference examples. This rule is needs_parser (not lint) because detecting it requires knowing whether a cheap gate trigger precedes the expensive iterator within the same callback body, which needs brace-aware scope tracking; a future scripts/validate.py checker could walk pulse_registry.yaml callbacks and flag every_character/every_country/every_international_organizations_member_of/every_owned_location appearing before any has_variable/has_variable_list check in the same effect body.
Second technique, used for tv_io_establishment_mark_basic_requirements_effect's conquest branch: an existence/membership gate does not help when the country IS a legitimate participant and the value must still progress toward completion (e.g. tv_update_conquest_score_effect's every_subject_or_below scan feeds a threshold every not-yet-established country needs to keep evaluating). There, split by is_ai instead of gating on existence: keep the scan on the existing monthly_country_pulse callback for is_ai = no, and add a sibling callback registered under yearly_country_pulse (see pulse_registry.yaml) that runs the same scan for is_ai = yes once a year. This mirrors the already-accepted human-monthly/AI-yearly split in towards_victory_situations.txt's tv_victory_situation.on_monthly. The scan itself is bounded by the ticking country's own subject count (not world size), so this is a lower-severity instance of the pattern than the world-character scan in tv_leaderboard_monthly_pulse -- worth fixing for consistency and to stop it running forever for AI, but not an O(n^2) hazard on its own.
Third technique, used for tv_trade_league_country_monthly_pulse_effect and tv_diplomatic_alliance_country_monthly_pulse_effect: both scanned every_international_organizations_member_of across ALL instances of the IO type every country every month, relying on an inner limit (leader_country ?= scope:<pulse_country>) to discard every entry except the one where the ticking country happens to be that instance's leader -- meaning only actual IO leaders ever did real work, but every non-leader country still paid the full scan cost walking every league/alliance's membership list. A plain has_variable_list membership gate is too coarse here (it would still admit every non-leader member, not just leaders). Use is_leader_of_international_organization_of_type = { type = tv_<io> } instead -- a single O(1) trigger check, already used elsewhere in this codebase (data/io_establishment.yaml ai_creation_requirement_body, src/in_game/common/generic_actions/tv_io_leader_actions.txt) -- to skip the scan entirely for every country that isn't currently leading any instance of that IO type.
Fourth case, tv_academy_world_debate_monthly_effect (scripts/philosophy_debate_codegen.py): this one already had the right gate (if = { limit = { has_variable = tv_academy_world_debate_active } ... }) but the tv_academy_world_debate_mirror_all_countries_effect = yes call (an every_country walk over every academy-owning country) was emitted at the WRONG indent level, one level shallower than the if body, so it ran every month unconditionally regardless of whether a debate was active. Not a missing gate, a misplaced statement outside an otherwise-correct one -- when a codegen helper builds nested blocks by explicit indent level (emit(lines, level, text) style, as opposed to raw multi-line string templates), double-check the indent level of every appended line against the block it is meant to close into, especially the last statement before a block's closing brace. The mirror is still safe to run on the exact month the debate resolves, since resolve_effect only clears active partway through the same if body and EU5 effect blocks execute sequentially rather than re-checking the outer limit -- so moving the call to be the last line inside the if (not after it) preserves the one clearing pass on resolution while skipping all the months no debate is running. |
| needs_parser | workflow | any | Changing a unique wonder's `base_key` in data/unique_wonders.yaml (e.g. to fix a failing site-rule check from scripts_engineering_department/audit_unique_wonder_site_requirements.py) without also updating that wonder's `mechanic_key` field to the same value | scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/_core.py's load_unique_wonders() hard-requires mechanic_key == base_key for every unique wonder entry and raises ValueError(f"Unique wonder {key} must keep mechanic_key = {base_key}") otherwise, failing every downstream generator (gen_tv_wonder_module_buildings.py, gen_tv_wonder_finalization_effects.py, gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_*.py, gen_location_window.py, etc.) that calls load_unique_wonders(). Before regenerating after a base_key change, check that the generic wonder entries for the old and new base_key in data/wonders.yaml share compatible size/category/pop_type/final_buildings level count (they need not be byte-identical, just structurally compatible), then update both base_key and mechanic_key together to the new value. | Confirmed both great_wall and star_fortress_city in data/wonders.yaml are size: small, category: military_category, pop_type: soldiers, with the same maintenance key and a 3-level final_buildings progression, so the reassignment was mechanically safe once mechanic_key was updated to match. This does not generalize automatically -- always diff the two base wonder entries in data/wonders.yaml before reassigning, since a mismatch in final_buildings level count or category would need the unique wonder's own overrides (final_buildings, initial_level) reconciled too. |
| advisory | gui | any | Assuming EU5's Europedia (Encyclopedia) sidebar supports adding a new named page/category through data -- e.g. a `page =`/`category =` field on a `game_concept` block, or a `common/encyclopedia_pages/`-style registry folder -- and searching for one before implementing a custom browsable tab | There is no such registration point anywhere in reference_game_files or reference_official_defines: `Encyclopedia.AccessPages`/`EncyclopediaPage` is a read-only, engine-populated collection with getters only (GetTitle, Self, AccessSelf) and no AddPage/RegisterPage function.
