Card list is generated by scripts_engineering_department/in_game/gui/gen_tv_encyclopedia_wonders_cards_gui.py and spliced by merge_tv_encyclopedia_wonders_cards_gui.py via a `# BEGIN/END GENERATED TV_ENCYCLOPEDIA_CARDS` marker pair, reusing every existing game_concept_<id>/_desc pair from tv_game_concepts_l_*.yml and tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_l_*.yml rather than authoring new prose. |
| advisory | gui | any | Adding a static card-list vbox under a custom Europedia scrollbox without an expanding visible root/scrollbox/card-list root, a minimum content height, and expanding filter buttons | For the custom tab use `using = layoutpolicy_expanding` on its visible root and scrollbox; give the generated vbox directly inside `scrollbox_content` both `using = layoutpolicy_expanding` and `set_parent_dimension_to_minimum = height`; set `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` on every filter button. Preserve this in gen_tv_encyclopedia_wonders_cards_gui.py so regeneration cannot drop the contract. | The first custom Towards Victory tab omitted the expanding/minimum-height contract from its static card-list root and did not expand its filter buttons. The parser accepted the GUI but layout received no stable content extent, producing an upper-left collapse at runtime. |
| advisory | event | country | Writing `value = leader_country.estate_power(estate_type:crown_estate)` (or any other parenthesis-argument event target link, e.g. `estate_power(...)`, chained after a scope and used directly as the RHS of `value =`, a comparison, `add =`, `subtract =`, etc.) without wrapping the whole scope+call expression in double quotes | Wrap the entire chained expression in double quotes: `value = "leader_country.estate_power(estate_type:crown_estate)"`. Every vanilla usage of a function-call-style event target link (estate_power(...), estate(...), etc.) used as a script value or trigger operand is quoted, e.g. reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/disasters/coup_attempt.txt:13 `power > "root.estate_power(estate_type:crown_estate)"` and reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/attribute_columns/57_parliament_issues.txt:40 `value = "scope:actor.estate_power(root.estate_type)"`. | Runtime symptom is a cluster of 3 errors per occurrence: jomini_eventtarget.cpp 'No data specified for an event target link that requires data. Link estate_power', jomini_scriptvalue.h 'Cannot read [leader_country.estate_power] as a script value', and pdx_persistent_reader.cpp 'Unexpected token: (' immediately followed by 'Unexpected token: )' and 'Unexpected token: =' on the next two lines -- the parser splits on the bare `(` because unquoted script-value RHS syntax does not support inline function-call arguments; quoting makes the whole expression parse as one compound math-value string instead. Found across 47 identical occurrences in a single generated file (tv_academy_of_sciences.txt), all from the same 3 codegen template lines. |
| advisory | event | country | Adding an EU5 effect name to a generic 'reward type -> effect' vocabulary table (e.g. STYLE_3_REWARD_EFFECTS in scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/_core.py) with scope: country_scalar (a bare number as the whole statement, e.g. `add_bureaucracy = 12`) without checking reference_official_defines/docs/effects.log's 'Supported Targets' line for that effect | Before adding any add_X effect to a numeric reward vocabulary table, grep reference_official_defines/docs/effects.log for '## add_X' and confirm it has no 'Supported Targets' line (or one that only names a scope, not a typed target). Effects whose doc entry lists a Supported Targets type (e.g. add_bureaucracy: 'Supported Targets: bureaucracy_type') take a typed reference argument, not a scalar, and will silently no-op (PostValidate returns false, no runtime crash) if assigned a bare number. | This failure mode is dangerous because it does not throw a parse error or crash -- error.log only shows 'PostValidate of effect add_bureaucracy returned false at <file>:<line>', which reads like a minor validation notice rather than 'this reward never applies to the player'. All 12 occurrences were replaced per-entry with an already-supported country_scalar reward type (stability/prestige/honor/government_power) chosen to avoid duplicating a type already present in the same ceremony stage_N_reward group, then scripts_engineering_department/in_game/common/scripted_effects/gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_effects.py, gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_events.py, and both gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_l_*.py localization generators were rerun. |
| advisory | localization | global | A generator that emits one static_modifier (and therefore one auto-derived STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> localization key) per integer value across a large numeric range -- e.g. scripts/in_game/common/static_modifiers/gen_tv_trade_chain_display_modifiers.py emitting tv_trade_chain_strength_display_0 .. tv_trade_chain_strength_display_20000, 20001 modifiers/keys from a single strength_display_cap: 20000 in data/trade_chain.yaml | Prefer bucketing a continuously-scaling display value into a bounded number of tiers (e.g. every 50 or 100 points) rather than one generated key per exact integer, both to keep modifier/localization key counts sane and to reduce the chance of a 32-bit loc key hash colliding with an unrelated vanilla or mod key. Not yet applied to tv_trade_chain_display_modifiers.py as of 2026-07-11 -- left as-is per explicit project owner decision; if new hash collisions appear whose 'other' key traces back to this generator, bucketing becomes the recommended fix. | This is a known-accepted risk, not a fix-in-progress. Do not add ad hoc mitigation (renaming just the colliding keys, etc.) without revisiting the project owner's decision -- the actual fix, if pursued, is reducing key volume at the generator level (bucketing), not patching individual collisions as they're discovered. |
| needs_parser | gui | any | Instantiating tv_engineering_department_card_common (or any similarly-templated *_card_common type with a `common_header` icon+text block) with `blockoverride "header_size" { size = { -1 0 } }` to zero out the header row, but not also `blockoverride "common_header" {}` -- e.g. gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py's first draft overrode header_size/header_decor_templates/common_header_back_decor only. | Forcing the header widget's declared size to 0 does not suppress its child content: the default `common_header_icon`/`common_header_text_full` block (an icon plus a literal placeholder title) still gets created and renders, overflowing the zero-height parent and appearing as a leaked duplicate icon+title above the card's real content. Add `blockoverride "common_header" {}` alongside header_size/header_decor_templates, matching the verified in-repo precedent at tv_engineering_department.gui:7547-7549 and :7640-7642. | Found 2026-07-11 fixing the Unique Wonder Ceremony card fragment: all 9 cards (the ready card + 8 stage cards) leaked the default 'Card title header' placeholder icon+text once above the whole card group. common_header_back_decor was already empty by default in the type, so overriding it again was a no-op that didn't address the real leak. |
| advisory | gui | any | Stacking multiple *_card_common instances directly in a vbox (or wrapping each in a plain `widget = { ... }`) without giving each instance an explicit `layoutpolicy_vertical = fixed` plus a numeric `minimumsize`/`maximumsize` height -- e.g. gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py's ready/stage cards had no vertical policy or size at all, only `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` on an outer wrapper widget. | A plain `widget` (unlike `hbox`/`vbox`) does not auto-size vertically from its child content in this codebase's GUI convention; every other stacked card usage in tv_engineering_department.gui (7467-7472, 7495-7499, 7542-7545, 7635-7638, 1646-1650) sets `layoutpolicy_vertical = fixed` with an explicit `minimumsize`/`maximumsize` height directly on the card_common instantiation itself (no extra wrapper widget). Without it, every card collapses to zero height and the vbox stacks them all at the same position, reading as cards 'piled on top of each other' instead of listed in sequence. | Found 2026-07-11 alongside card_common_untitled_card_missing_common_header_blockoverride in the same ceremony-card fragment; the two bugs compounded visually (the leaked header content from the missing common_header override was what was actually visible piled up at the top of the collapsed card stack). |
| advisory | gui | any | Wrapping a `text_multi` in an extra `widget = { layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding text_multi = { layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed max_width = N autoresize = yes ... } } }` inside a card_common bottom-content row -- e.g. gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py's stage cards, observed rendering the text column at ~375px against a nominal 380 max_width and pulling the whole fixed-500-wide card wider than its declared minimumsize/maximumsize. | Do not sandwich text_multi inside an extra `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` generic `widget`. Place text_multi directly as the hbox child instead, with `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding`, `max_width`, and `autoresize = yes` set on the text_multi itself -- matching the verified working precedent at tv_engineering_department.gui:8422-8428 (the Pharos stage-1 text row). Also keep max_width comfortably below the row's real budget (card width minus card_common_bottom_margin minus the icon column minus hbox spacing gaps), not flush against it -- this project settled on a shared `TEXT_MAX_WIDTH = 340` constant in wonder_ceremony_lib-adjacent gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py for a 500px card with a 60px icon column. | Found 2026-07-11, reported by the user as the ceremony stage cards' right-side text visibly overflowing the card at ~375px. This is the same failure class already documented in docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md (an expanding child pulling its natural width back into the row and blowing out a bounded parent, historically observed at 548.3px against a 470px-bounded card) -- confirms the anti-pattern recurs even when max_width nominally fits the arithmetic budget, because the expanding wrapper widget does not respect the ancestor's declared size clamp at all. See docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 11 and rule 22. |
| advisory | gui | any | A card_common instance declares auto/flexible height at the outermost level (`maximumsize = { W -1 }`, no explicit layoutpolicy_vertical needed there) and the first nested content wrapper correctly uses `layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking` + `size = { W -1 }` + `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` on its vbox, but a DEEPER nested wrapper widget inside that same content reverts to a hardcoded fixed height (e.g. `widget = { size = { 462 330 } vbox = { ... } }` with no shrinking policy) -- found in tv_engineering_department.gui's outer Ceremony card (TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_CARD_TITLE), whose inner `widget = { size = { 462 330 } ... }` wrapped the Pharos/Hagia ritual-status area and the newly generated 1-9 ceremony stage cards. | The auto-height chain (`layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking` + `size = { W -1 }` on the wrapper widget, `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` + `layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking` on its vbox) must be applied at EVERY nesting level between the outermost flexible card_common and the actual variable-height content, not just the first level. A single fixed-height wrapper anywhere in that chain caps the measured content at its hardcoded number regardless of how much content is actually rendered inside it, so variable-count child lists (like a growing stack of ceremony stage cards) silently overflow past the outer card's computed bounds instead of stretching it taller. | Reported by the user: the ceremony stage cards (now correctly stacked in sequence per card_common_list_missing_fixed_vertical_size_overlaps_in_vbox) still didn't stretch the outer Ceremony card taller, because that card's ritual-status content sat inside a hardcoded 330px-tall widget dating from when this area only ever held the much shorter Pharos/Hagia step text. The generated ceremony cards fragment itself needed no change; only the hand-maintained wrapper one level up in tv_engineering_department.gui did. |
| advisory | gui | any | An hbox/vbox sets an explicit `size = { W H }` to bound a mixed fixed-column + expanding-column layout (e.g. a fixed icon widget plus an expanding text_multi) but does not itself declare `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` -- e.g. gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py's per-card `hbox = { size = {432 104} spacing = 8 widget {...64px icon...} text_multi {...} }`. | When a container combines an explicit `size` hint with expanding children, also set `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` (and `layoutpolicy_vertical` if relevant) on the container itself, matching this file's own established precedent (Pharos stage rows at tv_engineering_department.gui:8401-8402; the Hagia active-ritual row at :9372-9375). Without it, the declared `size` is not honored -- the container instead sizes itself to the sum of its children's intrinsic minimums, so a fixed-width icon column plus a text_multi with no minimumsize (0 intrinsic width until stretch is applied) collapses the whole row to roughly just the icon's width, leaving the text column effectively 0px wide and unselectable. | Reported by the user via in-game inspection: the per-card content hbox rendered at ~80px wide (icon column + spacing only) instead of its declared 432px, and the flavor text_multi was unselectable (effectively 0 width). A prior guess that the missing property was `ignoreinvisible = yes` on the same hbox was reverted by the user as not addressing the actual observed symptom; the real gap was the container's own layoutpolicy_horizontal. This rule is specific to hbox/vbox as multi-child flex containers -- for the DIFFERENT (but easy to conflate) failure where a vbox uses `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes`, see `set_parent_size_to_minimum_vbox_needs_leaf_minimumsize_for_width` instead; adding layoutpolicy_horizontal to the surrounding widget/vbox in that case has no effect and was reverted by the user as such. |
| advisory | gui | any | A `vbox` with `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` (the standard auto-height chain pattern, paired with `layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking`) contains a bare `text_multi` leaf with only `max_width`/`autoresize = yes`/`layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` and no `minimumsize` of its own -- e.g. `tv_engineering_department.gui`'s `TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_ACTION_ONGOING`/`TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_FINALIZED_TEXT` text_multi blocks, direct children of a vbox at `:8132`/`:8224` with `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes`. | `set_parent_size_to_minimum = yes` computes the vbox's own size from the MINIMUM size each child reports -- not from any `layoutpolicy_horizontal`/`size` set on the vbox itself or its parent `widget` (those were tried first, confirmed to have zero effect, and reverted). A bare `text_multi` with no `minimumsize` reports 0 intrinsic width, so it silently zeroes out the whole chain: the vbox (and any ancestor `widget` that in turn auto-fits to the vbox) collapses to margin-only width, and the text renders unselectable at effectively 0 width, with no error in the log. Give the leaf `text_multi` an explicit `minimumsize = { W -1 }` matching its `max_width` -- matching the vanilla `minimumsize = { W -1 }` + `autoresize` idiom (e.g. `reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/advances_lateralview.gui:331-332`). The already-working Pharos/Hagia text rows in this same file never hit this because they are fully self-contained fixed-size boxes (`size = { W H }`, both dimensions literal) that never depend on `set_parent_size_to_minimum` in the first place. | Two prior guesses were tested live by the user and confirmed to NOT fix this: (1) adding `ignoreinvisible = yes` to a sibling hbox -- irrelevant, wrong container; (2) adding/removing `layoutpolicy_horizontal`/`layoutpolicy_vertical` and explicit `size` on the ancestor `widget`/`vbox` (:8128, :8220) -- the user's own live measurements showed the vbox width stuck at exactly its own doubled horizontal margin (8 = 4+4) regardless of those properties, and removing the widget's `layoutpolicy_vertical = shrinking` entirely broke the widget's OWN width too (470 -> 8). Those measurements were the key evidence that pointed to `set_parent_size_to_minimum` computing from child minimums rather than from ancestor policy. `docs/knowledge/risk_cards/gui.md` rule 14 and the tooltip-row paragraph in `docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md` already documented giving the ROW/CONTAINER a `minimumsize` under `set_parent_size_to_minimum`, but neither stated that the LEAF content widget itself also needs one -- that gap is why this took three attempts to diagnose instead of one doc lookup. A follow-up project-wide regex/brace scan for other instances of this pattern produced far more false positives than real hits, because it cannot detect that an intervening fully-literal-sized `widget` (explicit numeric width AND height) breaks the dependency chain and makes everything inside it safe regardless of ancestors further up (e.g. `tv_engineering_department.gui:8263`/`:8298`-style ritual-requirement rows, and `tv_trade_league.gui:593`, both false-flagged). Only 2 additional real instances were confirmed by hand (`:8140`, `:8211`, same vbox as the original two). A reliable project-wide audit needs a `needs_parser` `validate.py` check that tracks literal-size-wrapper breaks, not a naive ancestor-substring scan. |
| advisory | gui | any | A Python .gui generator function builds a repeated block (e.g. one card in a list of N cards) across many `lines.append(...)` calls and closes each inner sub-block (blockoverride, hbox, vbox) but omits the final closing brace for the outermost block the function opened, e.g. `append_card()` in gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cards_gui.py opened `tv_engineering_department_card_common = {` but its last two appended lines only closed the inner `hbox` and `blockoverride "common_bottom_content"`, never the card instantiation itself. | Verify total `{`/`}` counts for one generated repeated unit balance to zero, or -- for a list of siblings meant to render at the same nesting depth -- confirm every repeated instantiation opens at a constant depth (not a strictly increasing one). Since .gui block scope is pure brace balance, a missing closing brace does not raise a Python error; it silently nests the next generated unit one level inside the previous one. With N repeated units each missing one closing brace, unit k ends up nested k-1 levels deep inside unit k-1, cascading every subsequent unit's content into the first unit's innermost still-open block (here, the base type's header hbox at tv_engineering_department.gui:59) instead of stacking as N independent siblings. | Reported by the user as the 8 ceremony stage cards rendering as 'horizontally arranged, extremely tall' instead of vertically stacked wide cards. Confirmed by an awk brace-depth trace: each `tv_engineering_department_card_common = {` opened at depth 1, 2, 3, ... 8 instead of a constant depth 1, and total `{`/`}` count for one card span was 45/44 (imbalance of exactly 1). Every other layout property (fixed 462x144 size, shrinking-height wrapper chain, header collapse, hbox-per-card content) matched prior documented fixes and was not the cause -- the bug was purely a missing brace in the generator, invisible to plain-text reading unless brace counts are checked explicitly. |
| advisory | scripted_effects | wonders | tv_wonder_initialize_existing_unique_wonders_effect (generate_location_display_effects() in gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.py, output src_engineering_department/in_game/common/scripted_effects/tv_wonder_location_display_effects.txt) instant-constructs the final building for an initial_level>0 unique wonder and registers it as a priority-expansion candidate, but never wrote tv_wonder_surveyed / tv_wonder_survey_scale_competence / tv_wonder_survey_logistics_competence / tv_wonder_survey_organization_competence / tv_wonder_survey_scale_tier on that location for the wonder's id. | tv_wonder_surveyed is a shared gate used in three places: (1) this init effect's own re-run guard (it fires on both on_game_start and on_game_load, so it must skip re-registering the priority candidate once done), (2) tv_wonder_mechanics_copy_completed_survey_from_location_effect's guard for restoring saved competence into the country's active project, and (3) tv_wonder_selected_survey_already_cached_trigger, which tv_wonder_mechanics_start_survey_effect checks to decide whether reassigning the survey site to this location should restore cached values or start a fresh survey from 0. Because the existing-wonder priority-resume path (tv_wonder_prepare_priority_project_effect) never runs a real survey, tv_wonder_surveyed for that wonder id is never set by the normal survey-completion effect (tv_wonder_mechanics_complete_survey_effect, gated on tv_wonder_survey_active which the resume path never sets) -- so reassigning the survey site to that location always takes the 'fresh survey' branch and resets competence to 0/0/0, even though the resume-proposal fallback in tv_wonder_prepare_priority_project_effect had shown 85/85/85 (100 fixed-tier scale seed after this fix) moments earlier. Fix: seed tv_wonder_surveyed=1 plus the four competence/tier maps (scale=100, logistics=85, organization=85, scale_tier=6) for that wonder id, in the SAME already-not-yet-surveyed guarded block that calls append_register_existing_unique_priority_candidate (append_existing_unique_wonders_initialization_effect in gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.py). Seeding it there, atomically with the one-time candidate registration, satisfies all three consumers at once: registration only ever happens once (across every future game_load), and both the priority-resume copy and the reassign-survey-site cached branch find real saved data instead of falling through to a country-scope default or a from-scratch survey. | User recalled the original design intent was to seed the location as 'already surveyed' at game start, but it was never implemented; the reason turned out not to be a genuine architectural conflict (the same tv_wonder_surveyed map's three consumers are all compatible once seeded atomically with registration), just a missed step in the original generator. Also a reminder that gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.py holds multiple independent generate_*() entry points (generate(), generate_proposal_effects(), generate_survey_effects(), generate_ritual_effects(), generate_location_display_effects()) feeding DIFFERENT output files via separate thin wrapper scripts (gen_tv_wonder_proposal_effects.py, gen_tv_wonder_survey_effects.py, gen_tv_wonder_ritual_effects.py, gen_tv_wonder_location_display_effects.py) -- editing a function in this file and regenerating via its own OUT_FILE/main() can produce a byte-identical no-op diff if that function is only reachable from a sibling generate_*() used by a different wrapper script; always trace which generate_*() a target function feeds before assuming `python gen_<file>.py` regenerates it. |

| advisory | gui | any | Filling a `-1 <fixed_height>` (expanding-width, fixed-height) `background = { texture = ... }` widget with a wide non-tileable illustration (e.g. the 2048x1152 victory reward-tree banners) -- e.g. gen_tv_victory_situation_gui.py's first draft on append_tree_background used `spriteType = stretched`, then a second draft added `fittype = centercrop`, and both still visibly distorted/misbehaved because the widget's runtime width is not fixed and does not match the texture's aspect ratio. | For a non-tileable illustration with a specific aspect ratio, do not let the container's width float (`-1`/expanding) while only the height is pinned -- any stretch or crop fit mode still has to guess how to reconcile an arbitrary runtime width against the fixed texture aspect ratio. Instead give the widget an explicit fixed size on both axes computed from the source image's real aspect ratio (e.g. `width = 508`, `height = round(508 * source_height / source_width)`), with `layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` and `layoutpolicy_vertical = fixed`, so `spriteType = Stretched` maps the texture 1:1 onto matching proportions with no distortion and no cropping. | Found 2026-07-12 wiring the six victory-path reward-tree background DDS assets into tv_victory_situation.gui. | advisory | gui | any | A `TooltipRequirementsList = { ... maximumsize = { N -1 } ... }` block with N narrower than the template's default minimumsize (400px, from `@tooltip_inner_wide = @tooltip_title_wide(420) - @tooltip_inner_margin(10)*2`) and no `blockoverride "tooltip_minimumsize"` to match -- e.g. tv_engineering_department.gui's three wonder-debate demand-row TooltipRequirementsList instances (nobles/burghers/clergy), each declaring only `maximumsize = { 230 -1 }`. | Whenever `maximumsize` on a `TooltipRequirementsList` is set narrower than the inherited 400px default, also add `blockoverride "tooltip_minimumsize" { minimumsize = { N -1 } }` with the same N. Without it, the wider inherited minimumsize wins over the narrower maximumsize and the widget renders near its ~400px minimum regardless, which -- inside a non-clipping `layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` ancestor chain -- bubbles up through every expanding container and stretches an outer fixed-`maximumsize` card wider than declared. Match the working precedent already used by every other TooltipRequirementsList instance in the same file (e.g. the 'suitability location conditions' blocks pairing `maximumsize = { 218 -1 }` with `blockoverride "tooltip_minimumsize" { minimumsize = { 218 -1 } }`). | Reported by the user as the Engineering Department wonder-debate Card (declared maximumsize = { 500 -1 }) visibly widening only while the debate stage (tv_wonder_stage == 1) was active, then reverting to 500px once the debate ended -- exactly matching the demand rows' visibility gate. | advisory | gui | any | Emitting one static, near-duplicate GUI `widget` block per data-table row (e.g. one `visible = mechanic_id == N` branch per wonder) purely to display a (label, value) pair that is otherwise homogeneous across rows -- e.g. the pre-2026-07-13 Engineering Department 'suitability location conditions' block, 54 near-identical widgets (~4000 generated lines) differing only in loc key and a flat bonus number per wonder. | GUI expressions can build a dynamic composite key at runtime with `MakeScopeFlag(Concatenate('<system_prefix>_', Concatenate(<id_string>, '_<slot>')))` and read a value baked into a global variable map with `GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap('<map_name>', <composite_key>).GetValue`, matching a `flag:<system_prefix>_<id>_<slot>` key written at generation time via `add_to_global_variable_map`. Give the flag a named prefix (e.g. `tv_wonder_suitability_`), not a bare `<id>_<slot>` number pair -- a bare-number flag key is indistinguishable from a concatenation bug when it shows up in error.log's benign 'flag is set but never used' notice (see the sibling advisory note on this same entry). Combine with `text = "[Localize(Concatenate('PREFIX_', ToString_int32(FixedPointToInt(<id_value>))))]"` to resolve a dynamic loc key from a small numeric enum id. This collapses N per-row static branches into one shared widget plus two small generation-time-static lookup tables (condition/label id, numeric weight), keyed `flag:<system_prefix>_<entity_id>_<slot>`. Only applies when the *display* is homogeneous (same widget shape, different data); do not use this to flatten genuinely heterogeneous per-target logic (e.g. per-wonder survey trigger scripts checking different location conditions), which the Structural Fidelity Rule still requires to stay as explicit branches. If the user wants the per-key 'flag is set but never used' log noise eliminated rather than accepted: `has_flag` is NOT a valid EU5 trigger at all -- Jomini has no EU4-style boolean flag system, and referencing `has_flag = <key>` anywhere logs 'Unknown trigger type: has_flag' on load (this was a bug in the original fix attempt, not a working scanner-satisfier). Since a `flag:<name>` variable-map key is a string-typed map key, not a scope flag, the real reference is `is_key_in_global_variable_map = { name = <map_name> target = flag:<key> }` against the map that keys off it (e.g. `tv_wonder_suitability_condition_type`), placed inside a scripted trigger/effect that is itself reachable from a real on_action/event/decision entry point (e.g. called once from an existing on_game_start/on_game_load hook) -- an unreachable/never-called scripted trigger or effect does NOT satisfy the scanner even though it textually contains the reference; the engine's own message says so explicitly ('Use in unused scripted triggers and effects also does not count'). See tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger for the working pattern: one generated trigger ORs `is_key_in_global_variable_map` across every baked key (always true since every key is baked at init, harmless), called once via a no-op `if` (`remove_variable` on a name that's never set) from tv_wonder_initialize_index_on_game_start/_on_game_load. | Implemented 2026-07-13 for the Engineering Department suitability panel: two new global variable maps (tv_wonder_suitability_condition_type, tv_wonder_suitability_weight) keyed flag:tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot 1..5>, baked once from suitability_knowledge_for_wonder() at generation time, replacing the 54-wonder GUI loop with one wonder-agnostic widget. Net effect: tv_engineering_department.gui dropped from 9041 to 5168 lines. validate.py cannot execute Concatenate/MakeScopeFlag/GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap (no Clausewitz GUI expression interpreter in the pipeline), so any future change to this pattern still needs manual in-game verification per wonder/reveal-tier combination. In-game confirmed 2026-07-13: on load, error.log/jomini_effect.cpp:1162 logs one 'Flag "<key>" is set but is never used' line per baked flag key (270 = 54 mechanics x 5 slots). This is EXPECTED and benign -- the engine's flag-usage scanner only tracks references from scripted triggers/effects, and its own message already documents that localization use doesn't count; GUI-file reads (GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap in .gui) are the same kind of blind spot. A first attempt to silence it by adding a `has_flag` reference inside a scripted trigger that was never called from anywhere still logged all 270 warnings, confirming the engine message's own caveat that unused/unreachable scripted triggers and effects also don't count. The user then asked for the warnings to actually be eliminated rather than accepted. Fix (same day): added `tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger` (generated, one `OR` of `has_flag = tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot>` per baked key) and called it once from the existing, already-reachable `tv_wonder_initialize_index_on_game_start`/`_on_game_load` hidden_effect via a no-op `if` (`remove_variable` on a variable name that is never set elsewhere, so the branch has zero side effects whether or not the always-false OR matches). Making the *containing* trigger reachable, not just the flag literal present in a text file, is what satisfies the scanner. `SUITABILITY_KNOWLEDGE_ROW_SLOTS` and the `tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot>` key format were centralized into `scripts/wonder_mechanics/suitability_conditions.py` (`suitability_row_key()`) so both generators stay in sync. Do not add a per-flag repair anywhere else, and do not move this reference into a hot/monthly path -- it only needs to run once per game start/load.
Correction (2026-07-13, same-day follow-up): the `has_flag` reference itself was invalid EU5 syntax -- error.log/pdx_persistent_reader.cpp:289 logged 'Unknown trigger type: has_flag' once per line (270 occurrences) on parse, before the flag-usage scanner even ran. `has_flag` does not exist in Jomini; there is no EU4-style boolean flag system. Since `flag:<key>` here is a string-typed `global_variable_map` key namespace, not a scope flag, the reference must instead be `is_key_in_global_variable_map = { name = tv_wonder_suitability_condition_type target = flag:tv_wonder_suitability_<mechanic_id>_<slot> }` (vanilla precedent: reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/country_interactions/hre.txt:897). `append_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger` in the generator was updated to emit this instead; the OR now evaluates true for every entry (all keys are baked at init) rather than always-false, which remains harmless since this trigger is a static reference, not real gameplay logic. |

Runtime logs `Unknown anchor 'topleft'` at load time and the position falls back to default, not the intended pixel offset. | Found 2026-07-13 wiring absolutely-positioned Victory Path Tree node buttons into tv_victory_situation.gui — `parentanchor = topleft` loaded without a hard error but logged 'Unknown anchor' for all 104 node widgets and silently used a fallback anchor instead of the intended top-left corner. | advisory | gui | any | Wrapping an already-working `hbox { layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding ... expand = {} ... }` row in a NEW parent `vbox` (e.g. to gate both the row and its alternate/placeholder `text_single` behind one shared `visible` condition) without also giving that new wrapper `vbox` its own `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding`. | A `vbox`/`hbox` container's own horizontal sizing defaults to shrinking-to-content unless a `layoutpolicy_horizontal` is explicitly declared; it does NOT automatically inherit or forward its ancestor's fixed width to its children. When the immediate parent enforces a fixed column width (e.g. `layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` + matching `minimumsize`/`maximumsize`, as in `tv_engineering_suitability_location_conditions_column`'s 218px sibling column), every intermediate wrapper between that fixed-width ancestor and an `expanding` row must also declare `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding`, or the chain breaks at that wrapper: the `expanding` row inside only gets the wrapper's shrunk-to-content width to expand into, so an `expand = {}` widget inside it renders at 0px and the row never reaches the intended max width. Give the new wrapper `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` at the point it's introduced. | Regressed 2026-07-13 by commit 84141ceb ('Refactor suitability conditions localization and logic'), which introduced `suitability_dynamic_row()`'s per-row wrapper `vbox` (grouping the reveal-gated `hbox` with its 'hidden' `text_single` fallback behind one `visible` check) to replace the old flat alternating hbox/text_single siblings directly under the fixed-218px column vbox. The user reported in-game that an `expand = {}` widget inside the row `hbox` rendered at 0 width and the row never reached its expected max width; comparing against the pre-refactor GUI (same commit range as this same-day suitability GUI collapse, see `gui_dynamic_composite_key_variable_map_lookup`) showed the old `hbox` was a direct fixed-column child, so its own `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` correctly picked up the 218px fixed width -- the new wrapper vbox sat between them without a horizontal policy of its own. Fixed by adding `layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding` to the wrapper vbox in `suitability_dynamic_row()`, then regenerating via `gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py` + `merge_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py` (this GUI section is fragment-generated then merged into the hand-maintained file, not a straight generated-file overwrite). |
| advisory | gui | any | RETRACTED 2026-07-13 — DO NOT build a custom pending-variable + shared overlay GUI to make a generic-action button 'ask for confirmation'. A prior version of this rule claimed the engine has no mod-facing confirmation mechanism and prescribed exactly that workaround; it was wrong. The actual bad pattern is the workaround itself: staging a click into a pending-id country variable, rendering a shared scrim+dialog overlay gated on that variable, and dispatching Confirm/Cancel through a big if/else_if chain — all to reproduce something the engine already does natively. | Set `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold = yes` directly on the `generic_action` definition (see `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/generic_actions/readme.txt:76`: 'optional, to always show confirmation dialog. Useful for tweaking dangerous IO actions like declaring wars without redoing UI layout'). The engine shows its own native confirmation dialog before running the action's `effect`, reusing the SAME `title`/`description` loc keys the action's `action_button` already sets for its tooltip — no extra loc keys, no pending variable, no custom overlay widget tree needed. `main_menu/gui/confirm_window.gui`'s `ConfirmWindow` singleton (the thing the original search was looking for and correctly ruled out as not parametrizable) is unrelated to this — `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold` is a different, per-generic_action-level field, not a GUI window. | Implemented 2026-07-13 for the Victory Path Tree's click-to-unlock flow, then corrected same-day. First pass searched reference_game_files for a reusable confirmation window, found only the hardcoded `ConfirmWindow` singleton (no mod-facing hook for a custom on-confirm effect), and WRONGLY concluded no native mechanism existed at all — missing the separate, un-searched-for `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold` field on `generic_action` itself. Built the pending-variable (`tv_tree_pending_id`) + shared overlay (`append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay`) workaround instead, which then needed two more anti_patterns entries (`gui_sibling_overlay_outside_datacontext_scope_loses_context`, `blockoverride_content_indirectly_inside_vbox_via_base_template`) just to debug the overlay's own GUI structure bugs. The user flagged that EU5 natively supports click-and-confirm and asked for the entire workaround to be deleted; verified via the generic_actions readme and two vanilla examples, then removed `PENDING_VAR`/`CONFIRM_ACTION`/`CANCEL_ACTION`/`node_uid`/`PATH_ORDER` from `victory_tree_node_codegen.py`, the whole `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay` function and its call from `gen_tv_victory_situation_gui.py`, and the two now-dead action ids from `gen_messagetypes.py`. Lesson: when a reference search comes back negative for the FIRST mechanism you think of (a modal window), check the readme/field list of the RELEVANT script object (here, `generic_actions/readme.txt`) for a purpose-built field before concluding 'no native support' and reaching for a custom GUI workaround. |
| advisory | gui | any | Copy-pasting the same ~30-line header button+tooltip block (icon, action_tooltip, tooltipwidget shell) into every instance of a shared card type instead of baking it into the type's default, and/or leaving a fully-overridden shared-type instance (every visible block already blockoverride'd) instantiating the type anyway. | When a shared `type X = hbox { block "A" {...} block "B" {...} }` gets the same non-trivial content repeated across ≥3 instances (this project's own ≥3-repeated-structural-unit threshold), move the repeated content into the type's own default for that block and expose ONE narrow placeholder block at the point per-instance content differs -- but that placeholder MUST sit directly in the outer type's own body, not nested inside a further `blockoverride` of a DIFFERENT type's instantiation (e.g. a `tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = { blockoverride "tooltip_content" { ... } } }` embedded in the outer type). A per-instance `blockoverride` on the OUTER type could not reach a custom block declared two levels deep inside that nested type's own blockoverride -- confirmed broken in-game even though it validated and parsed fine (validate.py is a text linter, not the real block-resolution engine). Every OTHER working block-override in this codebase is either declared directly within the same type's body (no nested-type crossing), or is a block belonging to the nested type itself, overridden directly by the caller at the instantiation site (e.g. `ConceptTooltipType`'s own `title_icon`/`title_text`/`tooltip_content`, per `SimpleConceptTooltipType`/right_panel.gui precedent) -- not a custom block invented inside that nested override. Fix: expose one placeholder that wraps the ENTIRE nested type instantiation (e.g. `block "header_help_tooltip" { tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = {...default...} } }`), so each instance's override supplies the whole `tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = { blockoverride ... } }` block directly -- more duplication than the two-placeholder version, but every override target is a proven-safe pattern. Conversely, before instantiating a shared type for a new generated/repeated component, check whether every block the type would provide is already going to be blockoverride'd (header fully blanked, background fully replaced, bottom content fully replaced) -- if so, the type contributes nothing but its outer layout skeleton and one non-overridden default property (here `card_common_bottom_margin`'s `margin = { 15 10 }`), which is cheap to reproduce directly without the type indirection. | Applied 2026-07-13: a Europedia-opening header help button (icon + action_tooltip + ConceptTooltipType tooltip) was first added by hand to 2 of 11 named tv_engineering_department_card_common instances, then baked into the base type's common_header_extra_left/right defaults once the plan was to apply it to all 11. First attempt used two custom placeholder blocks (`header_help_title_text`, `header_help_tooltip_rows`) nested inside the base type's own `blockoverride "title_text"`/`blockoverride "tooltip_content"` of a `ConceptTooltipType` instantiation -- this parsed cleanly and passed validate.py, but the user reported the Construction and Organization&Logistics cards' tooltip content had actually disappeared in-game. Re-architected to a single `block "header_help_tooltip" {}` wrapping the entire `tooltipwidget = { ConceptTooltipType = {...} }` as a direct child of `button_regular` (same-type nesting, no crossing), with each of the 6 named cards now overriding that one block with its full tooltipwidget content -- verified via brace-balance + validate.py after the restructure; still pending an actual in-game confirmation. Separately, the panel's 8 auto-generated Unique Wonder Ceremony stage cards already overrode every block the type renders (header_size/header_decor_templates/common_header all blanked, card_bg fully replaced, common_bottom_content fully replaced), so they were converted to a hand-built vbox in the generator, reproducing only the type's real remaining contribution -- the card_common_bottom_margin default `margin = { 15 10 }` that the cards' hardcoded 432px content width (`462 - 15*2`) already depended on. |
| advisory | gui | country | Appending a shared overlay `widget` (e.g. a confirmation popup meant to float above all tab content) as a sibling AFTER a `vbox`/`hbox` closes, when that vbox is the one carrying `datacontext = "[...]"`, instead of nesting the overlay inside the vbox or giving the overlay its own datacontext. `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay()` was called after `emit(lines, 2, "}")` closed the datacontext-carrying vbox, so the overlay widget and all its descendants (visible checks, text_single/text_multi labels) had no Country data context at all. | `datacontext` set on a widget/vbox/hbox only applies to that block's own descendants; it is NOT inherited by later siblings once the block closes, even if the sibling is still inside the same enclosing `blockoverride`. Any `Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...)` (or other typed accessor) used inside a sibling overlay placed outside the datacontext-carrying container must either be moved inside that container, or receive its own `datacontext = "[SituationView.GetPlayer]"` (or equivalent) directly on its own outermost widget. The runtime does not error at load time; every `FetchData` call inside the affected subtree silently fails with `pdx_data_callstack.cpp: No context supplied (Use SetDataContext), wanted context of type 'Country'`, which reads as the whole overlay's dynamic content just not evaluating (visible conditions default false, values blank) rather than a crash. | Found 2026-07-13 debugging the Victory Path Tree confirm/cancel overlay (see `generic_action_confirmation_needs_pending_variable_overlay_not_engine_window`): `append_panel()` calls `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay(lines, 2)` right after closing the tab-content vbox that carries `datacontext = "[SituationView.GetPlayer]"`, making the overlay a sibling of that vbox inside `blockoverride "situation_panel_main_content"`, not a descendant of it. Every `visible`/`text` expression inside the overlay referenced `Country.MakeScope.GetVariable(...)` assuming it would inherit the parent's Country context; instead every one of them failed FetchData silently. Applies to any future shared overlay/popup appended as a top-level sibling after a datacontext-scoped container closes -- always give it its own explicit `datacontext` if it needs typed-scope accessors. SUPERSEDED 2026-07-13 (same day): the overlay this bug lived in was deleted entirely once `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold = yes` (see `generic_action_confirmation_needs_pending_variable_overlay_not_engine_window`) was found to make it unnecessary -- kept here only as a general datacontext-scoping lesson for any future shared overlay/popup, not as a reference to still-existing code. |
| advisory | gui | any | Appending a `parentanchor`/percentage-size widget as a direct child of a `blockoverride "<name>" { ... }` body, on the assumption that because the widget appears as a plain sibling in the mod's own .gui file (not literally nested inside a local `vbox = { }`/`hbox = { }`), the hbox/vbox restriction does not apply. `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay(lines, 2)` was emitted as a direct sibling inside `blockoverride "situation_panel_main_content" { ... }` with `parentanchor = top|left` and `size = { 100% 100% }` directly on it. | A `blockoverride "X"` substitutes content at the exact point where the BASE type declares `block "X" {}` — read that declaration in `reference_game_files` before assuming the substitution point's parent container. `situation_panel_main_content` is declared inside `scrollwidget = { vbox = { ... block "situation_panel_main_content" {} ... } }` in `reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/panels/situation/common.gui`, so every direct child emitted inside the local `blockoverride` block is actually a vbox child at runtime, even though the mod's own file shows it as a sibling of other top-level widgets. Do NOT fix this by wrapping the ENTIRE blockoverride body (including the real scrollable content) in one outer `widget` — a plain `widget` does not propagate its children's natural/intrinsic height to its own parent the way a `vbox` does, so wrapping the tabs+pages vbox inside that outer widget hides its true content height from the enclosing `scrollarea`'s `autoresizeviewport` and disables scrolling entirely (confirmed by the user in-game). Only wrap the OFFENDING widget itself, and keep the real scrollable content vbox as a direct, unwrapped sibling in the blockoverride exactly as before. For a widget that must also default to zero layout footprint while hidden (e.g. a conditional overlay), the wrapper must be a `vbox`/`hbox` with `ignoreinvisible = yes` set on itself — not a plain `widget`, which does not support `ignoreinvisible` at all (see rule `gui_ignoreinvisible_on_plain_widget`) — then nest a plain `widget` inside that ignoreinvisible-capable vbox, and only put `parentanchor`/percentage `size` on a THIRD widget nested one level deeper still (a child of that plain widget, not of the vbox). | Found 2026-07-13 from docs/error_log/error.log: 'Unsupported property: parentanchor on widgets that are a child of a hbox/vbox' / 'size with a percentage ... consider using layout policies' at tv_victory_situation.gui:20148-20149, origin 20145 — the confirm-overlay widget added right after the tabs vbox closed. The existing lint validator `check_gui_parentanchor_under_hbox_vbox` (see [[parentanchor_under_hbox_vbox]]) only scans the LOCAL file's brace structure and cannot see that a `blockoverride` substitution point is itself inside a vbox in a different (vanilla) file, so this class of violation is not currently lint-detectable — verify blockoverride parentage against the base template (see gui.md risk card point 5) whenever adding parentanchor/percent-size widgets as direct blockoverride children. First fix attempt wrapped the WHOLE blockoverride body (tabs/pages vbox + overlay) in one outer `widget = { layoutpolicy_horizontal = expanding  layoutpolicy_vertical = expanding }`; this passed `validate.py` and cleared the log errors, but the user reported it broke the situation panel's scrollarea entirely. Re-fixed by reverting `append_panel` to its original unwrapped vbox, and instead restructuring only `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay` into `vbox { ignoreinvisible = yes ... } > widget { datacontext ... } > widget { parentanchor = top|left  size = { 100% 100% } ... }` — three levels, not the two originally used, because the outer level needs to be an `ignoreinvisible`-capable vbox (see `gui_ignoreinvisible_on_plain_widget`) to keep contributing zero height to the scrollarea while the popup is hidden (the default and by far most common state). SUPERSEDED 2026-07-13 (same day): `append_tree_pending_confirm_overlay` itself was deleted once `force_click_and_confirm_or_hold = yes` (see `generic_action_confirmation_needs_pending_variable_overlay_not_engine_window`) made the whole overlay unnecessary. The structural fact about `situation_panel_main_content` being nested in vbox in vanilla `common.gui`, and the wrap-only-the-offender-not-the-whole-block fix pattern, remain generically true for ANY future content injected into that same blockoverride — kept for that reason, not because the deleted overlay still exists. |
| lint | gui | any | Calling `.MakeScope.Custom('key')` in a GUI expression or localization value, e.g. `[InternationalOrganizationsView.GetPlayer.MakeScope.Custom('tv_academy_debate_current_node_line')]`. | `.Custom('key')` (customizable_localization) requires a scope of the TYPE declared by that key's `type = ...` (e.g. `type = country` requires a real Country-typed scope), but `.MakeScope` converts a typed scope into the generic `Scope` object used for `GetVariable`/`var:` access, which does not expose `Custom`. Call `.Custom(...)` directly on the typed scope before any `.MakeScope` conversion — e.g. `InternationalOrganizationsView.GetPlayer.Custom('key')`, not `InternationalOrganizationsView.GetPlayer.MakeScope.Custom('key')`. `GetVariable` and `Custom` want different scope representations of the same underlying object; do not chain them off the same `.MakeScope` call. | Found 2026-07-13 from docs/error_log/error.log: 'Could not find data system function Custom in InternationalOrganizationsView.GetPlayer.MakeScope.Custom(...)' at tv_research_l_english.yml TV_ACADEMY_DEBATE_CURRENT_NODE_LINE and TV_ACADEMY_PHILOSOPHY_CURRENT_NAME. Fixed by dropping `.MakeScope` since `InternationalOrganizationsView.GetPlayer` already yields a Country-typed scope suitable for `.Custom(...)` directly. |
| advisory | event | any | Trying to make an event `option.name` read differently per-condition by wrapping it in a `first_valid`/`triggered_desc` block, the same way `title`/`desc` support dynamic per-condition dispatch. | There is no vanilla or mod precedent anywhere for `option.name = { first_valid = { ... } }` (confirmed by an exhaustive grep of reference_game_files/game/in_game/events and every reference mod) — `option.name` is always a single flat localization key. To make that fixed key's displayed text vary per scope/condition, keep `option.name` as a flat key and instead give that key's `.yml` VALUE a Customizable Localization call: `some_key:0 "[ROOT.Custom('some_block')]"`, where `some_block` is a new `type = country` (or matching scope) block in `in_game/common/customizable_localization/` with one `text = { trigger = {...} localization_key = ... }` entry per case plus a `fallback = yes` entry. This keeps the event's option structure completely unchanged and reuses an already-verified engine mechanism instead of an unverified one. | Found 2026-07 while reworking the Unique Wonder Ceremony's two per-stage event options to read as wonder-specific flavor text (previously one generic 'Pay the price.'/'Not yet.' pair shared by all 121 wonders x 8 stages). The event's `tv_wonder_ceremony_events.txt` needed zero changes; only the loc generators changed, from a static string per flat key to `[ROOT.Custom(...)]`. See docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 26. |
| advisory | modifier | location | Assuming every new location-scoped static modifier definition must be prefixed `TRY_REPLACE:`, per CLAUDE.md's/Towards_Victory_Design.md's general wording about location-scoped statics. | `TRY_REPLACE:<name> = { ... }` is a merge-conflict-resolution prefix used specifically to override an ALREADY-EXISTING modifier name (vanilla or another mod's) — confirmed against reference-mod usage (e.g. `TRY_REPLACE:province_starving`, a real vanilla modifier id). A brand-new `tv_`-namespaced modifier name has no such collision and needs no wrapper — every existing wonder-domain location-modifier generator in this codebase (e.g. gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_ritual_auxiliary_location_modifiers.py, gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_cost_local_modifiers.py) already omits `TRY_REPLACE` and works correctly. Only reach for `TRY_REPLACE` when the name you are defining already exists elsewhere. | Found 2026-07 while adding the Unique Wonder Ceremony's location-scoped temporary cost modifiers. CLAUDE.md's 'Critical EU5 Gotchas' section and docs/design/Towards_Victory_Design.md both state TRY_REPLACE as a blanket rule for location-scoped static modifiers, which overstates the actual, narrower requirement — flagged for a future wording pass rather than silently worked around. See docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 26. |
| lint | effect | any | Using EU4/CK2-style `set_global_flag = X` / `has_global_flag = X` / `remove_global_flag = X` in EU5 script, on the assumption every Paradox/Jomini game has this exact global-flag mechanism. | EU5 has no `global_flag` effect/trigger at all — confirmed by an exhaustive grep of `reference_official_defines/docs/effects.log` and `triggers.log` (zero matches for `flag` in either) and of every file under `reference_game_files/`. The real EU5/Jomini equivalent is a bare global variable: `set_global_variable = X` (shorthand for `set_global_variable = { name = X value = yes }`) and `has_global_variable = X`, both `Supported Scopes: none` (usable from anywhere). Confirmed extensively in vanilla (e.g. `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/on_action/_hardcoded.txt:564` `set_global_variable = byzantine_empire_fallen`) and in community mods (`reference_mods/3613232232/in_game/common/on_action/pp_game_start.txt`'s `pp_mod_welcome_situation_completed`/`pp_mod_welcome_situation_pending` pattern, which this mod's own standalone-mod-bootstrap event copied). | Found 2026-07 while implementing the standalone Engineering Department mod's game-start/game-load bootstrap (data/pulse_registry.yaml + src_engineering_department/in_game/common/on_action/tv_engineering_department_standalone_on_action.txt), which needed a save-wide 'has this mod's intro event already run' marker not tied to any one country. The doc entries for set_global_variable/has_global_variable themselves quote the generic set_variable/has_variable usage examples verbatim (a documentation copy-paste artifact, not a syntax difference) — trust the real vanilla usage sites over the doc's literal example text. |
| advisory | effect | any | Assuming an existing `cmf_suppress = { v = X }` entry (via `data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml` + `gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py`) means variable `X` is a handled/accepted case and its 'used but never set' or 'set but never used' line in `error.log` can be safely ignored without checking whether `X` actually has a real setter/reader anywhere. | `cmf_suppress`'s own definition (`reference_mods/3692202776/in_game/common/scripted_effects/cmf_utility_effects.txt`) is an `always = no`-guarded dead branch that references `$v$` in every set/get form (`set_variable`, `exists = var:$v$`, `has_variable_map`, etc.), explicitly to make the engine's static usage scanner see both a 'set' and a 'used' occurrence — per its own comment this targets exactly the two `jomini_effect.cpp` warning strings this project cares about, not a separate CMF-only display. Whether it reliably works for every reference shape (plain scope variable vs. a bare `global_variable_map` name) through this project's own extra outer `always = no` wrapper (`gen_tv_cmf_suppressions.py`'s `tv_cmf_warning_suppressions_monthly_pulse`) is NOT independently confirmed — do not present 'cmf_suppress does/doesn't silence the native log' as a settled fact either way without an actual in-game error.log re-check after the suppression entry is added and the mod redeployed. What IS certain regardless of that mechanism question: a suppression entry is never a substitute for checking the real reference graph. Grep the variable across both mod roots for every `set_variable`/`change_variable`/`add_to_variable_map` (a real setter) and every `var:`/GUI `GetVariable`/`GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap` read (a real consumer) before deciding whether to keep code, delete it, or just suppress the log line. If there is truly zero setter anywhere, delete the dead `remove_variable` calls and the code they clean up rather than leaving them for a suppression entry to paper over. If a `global_variable_map` genuinely has zero readers anywhere including GUI and isn't planned future scaffolding, delete it instead of suppressing it. | 2026-07-15: triaged 9 error.log lines after the Engineering Department mod split. tv_wonder_{foundation,body,function,decoration}_progress and tv_wonder_construction_paused had zero setters anywhere (superseded by tv_wonder_construction_progress + tv_wonder_active_part) despite being cmf_suppress-listed since this suppression file's first commit — the suppression entry masked genuinely dead code rather than a real false positive; deleted the dead remove_variable calls and their suppression entries. tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_display_id (gen_tv_wonder_index_effects.py's FINAL_BUILDING_DISPLAY_ID_MAP) turned out to be a dead duplicate of the already-consumed tv_wonder_final_building_type_to_wonder_id (same generator loop, same wonder_id value, zero readers) rather than a genuine dynamic-reference case — deleted rather than kept suppressed. tv_wonder_suitability_weight (read only via GUI GetVariableFromGlobalVariableMap) and tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_never_set (an intentional trade-off documented on tv_wonder_suitability_flag_static_reference_trigger, trading one variable warning for avoiding dozens of unused-flag warnings) are both genuine cases where the code itself is correct and only the log line is cosmetic noise — added both to data/cmf_warning_suppressions.yaml for consistency with every other GUI-only-read/deliberate-workaround entry in that file, on the working assumption (unverified in this session, no way to launch the game) that cmf_suppress's mechanism is sound for the ~2200 other entries already relying on it and these two are not structurally different from those. |
| advisory | localization | any | Treating a winner-takes-all 'full vanilla copy with mod entries inserted' file (e.g. `character_title.txt`, `messagetypes.txt`) as an additive registry, or assuming dependency metadata alone makes the intended superset copy load last. | Files in this category are NOT name-keyed registries where independent per-file entries accumulate: the winning whole-file definition must contain every entry needed by the enabled mods it overrides. Standalone roots may carry only their own insertions when the playset manually sorts a later superset root after them; for TV+Engineering this means Engineering Department carries only Engineering entries, while the main TV copy carries the TV + Engineering Department union and must be sorted after Engineering Department. Validate the vanilla prefix and each root's expected singleton coverage. | Found 2026-07 while splitting the Engineering Department subsystem into `src_engineering_department/`; an initial standalone-subset/main-superset strategy assumed the main dependency would load later. Runtime logs on 2026-07-28 showed the Engineering Department singleton winning instead: all 81 main-only `PERFORM_`, `WE_PERFORM_`, and `ACTION_*_PERFORMED_ON_US` message types failed lookup despite appearing in the main copy. The other shared multi-IO files are genuine name-keyed registries and split cleanly. Updated 2026-08-01 after the user chose to manually sort TV after Engineering Department: `gen_character_title.py`, `gen_messagetypes.py`, and `check_vanilla_copy_integrity()` now enforce root-specific standalone subsets plus the main TV+Engineering superset instead of byte equality. |
| advisory | gui | any | A raw comparison against a conditionally-set variable (e.g. `var:tv_wonder_level > var:tv_wonder_project_base_level` where the RHS is only ever `set_variable`'d under one specific branch) inside a `custom_tooltip` block, when that trigger is also used as an effect's own `if.limit` (not just a generic_action's `allow`). | Follow the Milestone Trigger Tooltip Pattern already documented in CLAUDE.md: guard every raw variable comparison inside a `custom_tooltip` with `has_variable = X` in the same AND block before comparing `var:X`, exactly like the sibling branch in the same trigger already did (`var:tv_wonder_level ?= { this >= 1 }` is the safe form; the broken branch instead used a bare `var:tv_wonder_level > var:tv_wonder_project_base_level`). This is not just cosmetic: when the same trigger is reused as an effect's `if.limit` (not only a generic_action's `allow`), the tooltip-preview pass that renders the button's effect chain walks the whole body in order — if it errors out evaluating this `limit`'s custom_tooltip mid-pass, it can corrupt or blank rendering for a completely unrelated LATER effect in the same preview (observed: `destroy_building_forcefully = "building(building_type:tv_wonder_labor_camp|scope:tv_wonder_labor_camp_owner)"`, several lines further down in the same `if` body, showed a garbled/placeholder building name instead of the Labor Camp). Fix the root unguarded comparison rather than chasing the seemingly unrelated downstream symptom. | 2026-07-15: user reported the 'must have increased at least one level since starting/resuming construction' gate on the Finish Construction button was both ineffective and caused continuous pre-evaluation error spam while sitting in the construction stage, plus the button's own effect tooltip showed a corrupted 'X will be destroyed' line implying a missing building reference. Root cause: tv_wonder_project_base_level is only set via tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect when var:tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2 at site-selection time, so for any wonder not in that specific resume/upgrade mode the variable never exists at all, yet the trigger's first AND-branch compared against it unconditionally. Fixed by adding `has_variable = tv_wonder_project_base_level` to that branch, matching the safe idiom the trigger's own second branch already used one line below. The elsewhere-unguarded twin `var:tv_wonder_level > var:tv_wonder_project_base_level` inside tv_wonder_set_current_project_priority_candidate_mode_effect (same file, ~line 2164) was left alone: it is a plain effect `if.limit`, not a custom_tooltip, so ordinary AND short-circuiting protects it at runtime and no player-facing tooltip walks it. |
| advisory | effect | any | Restoring/backfilling a set of source counters (e.g. tv_wonder_foundation_units/body_units/function_units/decoration_units, read back from location_building_level for an existing pre-built building) without also re-running the effect that recomputes the derived cache variable (tv_wonder_level) computed from those counters, then immediately snapshotting that stale/still-unset derived variable elsewhere (tv_wonder_project_base_level = var:tv_wonder_level). | Whenever an effect restores/backfills the SOURCE counters a derived variable is computed from, immediately re-run the dedicated recompute effect (here, tv_wonder_update_wonder_level_effect) before anything downstream reads the derived variable. tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect called tv_wonder_restore_locked_wonder_final_building_state_effect and tv_wonder_sync_units_from_buildings_effect (which correctly restore tv_wonder_*_units from the location's existing building levels) but never called tv_wonder_update_wonder_level_effect before capturing tv_wonder_project_base_level = var:tv_wonder_level a few lines later — so for a wonder resuming construction on a site with pre-existing progress (an existing-at-game-start unique wonder like Jerusalem's Dome of the Rock, initial_level > 0, or any wonder whose site already has partial levels), tv_wonder_level was still 0/unset at the exact moment of the snapshot, so the set_variable using that non-existent source silently produced no tv_wonder_project_base_level at all. Added the missing tv_wonder_update_wonder_level_effect call between the restore/sync calls and the snapshot. The debug/repair tool tv_wonder_reinitialize_building_state_core_effect already had the correct order (sync then update_wonder_level) — that established precedent is what confirmed the fix, and it was also missing the equivalent project_mode==2 + missing-base_level repair, which was added there too so players already stuck mid-playthrough (base_level already silently missing) can recover via the tv_reinitialize_mod debug action instead of needing a fresh project. | 2026-07-15: user reported the 'must gain a level since resuming' Finish Construction gate stayed permanently blocked while expanding the pre-existing Dome of the Rock at Jerusalem, after the has_variable guard fix (see [[custom_tooltip_unguarded_comparison_corrupts_later_effect_tooltip_in_same_pass]]) correctly stopped the pre-evaluation crash but exposed that tv_wonder_project_base_level was never actually being set for this exact scenario. Traced through tv_wonder_initialize_existing_unique_wonders_effect (seeds only the survey/final-building-level maps at game start, not the country's own _units/level variables) -> the priority-project/proposal pipeline (correctly sets tv_wonder_project_mode=2 and tv_wonder_resume_project=1) -> tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect (correctly restores _units from the site's actual building levels via two dedicated restore effects, but never recomputed tv_wonder_level from them before the snapshot line two calls later). Confirmed data/unique_wonders.yaml id 103's only final_buildings entry (style 1 = tv_wonder_unique_dome_of_the_rock_inaugurated, initial_level 1) is exactly what the restore effects look up, so the restore itself works fine — only the missing recompute call was the gap. |
| lint | localization | any | Calling a Customizable Localization block directly off a bare `ROOT` in event/effect loc text: `[ROOT.Custom('some_block')]`. | `docs/technical/EU5_Modding_Knowledge_Base.md`'s own documented Customizable Localization example already shows the correct form: `[ROOT.GetCountry.Custom('ruler_residence')]` — `Custom` must be chained off a properly-typed scope link (`GetCountry`, `GetCharacter`, etc. matching the block's own `type = country`/`character`/...), not directly off the bare `ROOT` keyword, even in an already-country-scoped event/effect. The engine cannot resolve `Custom` as a function on the untyped root object: `pdx_data_factory.cpp: Could not find data system function 'Custom' in 'ROOT.Custom(...)'`, followed by `pdx_data_localize.cpp: Data error in loc string '<key>'`. Insert the matching `Get<Type>` link between `ROOT` and `.Custom(...)`. | 2026-07-15: user reported the Unique Wonder Ceremony's 8-stage pay/decline option text (tv_wonder_ceremony_events.txt, event tv_engineering_department.9300+ options .a/.b) errored on every stage; error.log confirmed 'Could not find data system function Custom in ROOT.Custom(...)'. The KB section this exact pattern was designed from (see risk_cards/wonders.md rule 26, added when the 8-stage ceremony's per-wonder pay/decline text was migrated to Customizable Localization) already documents the correct `ROOT.GetCountry.Custom(...)` form via its own worked example — the ceremony generator (gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_l_english.py/gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_l_simp_chinese.py) simply didn't chain the `.GetCountry` link when it was written. Grepping the whole repo for the same bare `[ROOT.Custom(` pattern found the identical bug already latent (never yet triggered in this playthrough) in scripts/philosophy_debate_codegen.py's tooltip_change_text, feeding the Academy Philosophy Debate's seated/vacated custom_description text (tv_academy_debate_group_seated_text and 7 sibling keys in tv_academy_philosophy_debate_l_english.yml/_simp_chinese.yml) — fixed both generators and regenerated all four affected localization files. Promoted to detectability: lint since the bare-ROOT pattern is a simple, unambiguous regex with no legitimate use (Custom always requires a typed link). |
| advisory | gui | any | destroy_building/destroy_building_forcefully targeting `building(building_type:X|scope:Y)` where `scope:Y` was captured earlier in the SAME effect via `save_scope_as = Y`, instead of an implicit link (`owner`, `root`, `scope:actor`, `prev`) that is naturally available without a prior save. | Every vanilla destroy_building/destroy_building_forcefully call resolves its `building(building_type:X|<link>)` second argument through an implicit, framework-provided link (`owner`, `root`, `prev`, `scope:actor`, `scope:target`, `scope:recipient`) — never through a custom name captured by a preceding `save_scope_as` inside the same effect (confirmed by grepping every vanilla usage in reference_game_files, zero counter-examples). The engine's runtime executor happily resolves a custom `scope:Y` reference and destroys the correct building, so this pattern looks correct in testing — but the effect-tooltip PREVIEW pass (used to render "here's what will happen" for a player-clickable generic_action) does not replay `save_scope_as` as a real side effect before trying to resolve the destroy target for display, so it fails to promote the engine's internal TARGET_BUILDING placeholder and the tooltip renders garbled/placeholder text instead of the real building name — while the actual click-through destruction still works perfectly. If the destroy call already executes from inside the target location's own scope (e.g. `var:tv_wonder_site ?= { ... }`), just use the location's own `owner` link directly and delete the now-unnecessary `save_scope_as`. | 2026-07-15: user reported that after the finish-construction gate fix landed, the button's effect tooltip still showed a garbled "X will be destroyed" line for the Labor Camp, but confirmed the actual click-through correctly destroyed the building every time — the split between broken preview and correct runtime execution was the key clue that ruled out a logic bug and pointed at a preview-only scope resolution gap. tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect did `save_scope_as = tv_wonder_labor_camp_owner` at country scope, then entered `var:tv_wonder_site ?= { ... }` (the location) and referenced `scope:tv_wonder_labor_camp_owner` in the destroy_building_forcefully target — replaced with the location's own `owner` link (matching vanilla's catholic.txt seat_of_cardinal precedent exactly, called from the same location-scoped position) and deleted the save_scope_as entirely. NOT yet fixed: the same save_scope_as-then-scope:X_owner pattern is used ~926 times across tv_wonder_module_effects.txt (tv_wonder_module_owner) for the generic/module wonder building-consolidation system — that whole family is only ever called from tv_wonder_reinitialize_building_state_core_effect (the tv_reinitialize_mod debug action) and every_owned_location, not from a normal player-facing construction button, so the same tooltip-preview corruption is plausible but unconfirmed there and was left alone rather than blindly refactoring 926 call sites without a reported symptom — check this file first if a similar garbled destroy-building tooltip is ever reported near the reinitialize debug action or module-building consolidation. 2026-07-16 follow-up: the `owner`-link fix alone did NOT clear the error — `docs/error_log/error.log` kept logging `Promote 'TARGET_BUILDING' returned nullptr` / `Data error in loc string 'THIRD_DESTROY_BUILDING_EFFECT'` because `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect` was still called directly from `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect`, which is itself the generic_action `tv_wonder_finish_construction`'s own `effect =` body — the exact chain the button's hover/tooltip preview pre-evaluates. See `[[destroy_building_forcefully_in_button_effect_chain_fails_tooltip_preview_even_with_owner_link]]` for the real fix (move the call out of the button's effect chain entirely). |
| advisory | gui | any | Calling a scripted_effect that contains `destroy_building`/`destroy_building_forcefully` directly (even through nested scripted_effect calls) from inside a player-clickable generic_action's own `effect = { ... }` body — the block the button's hover/tooltip preview pre-evaluates — even after the target is resolved through a correct implicit link (`owner`, `root`, etc., see [[destroy_building_target_via_custom_saved_scope_breaks_tooltip_preview]]). | The implicit-link fix only cures the case where the destroy target itself was mis-resolved (a custom `save_scope_as`). It does not cure the separate case where the destroy call sits directly inside the button's own tooltip-pre-evaluated effect chain: `tv_wonder_finish_construction` (generic_actions/tv_engineering_department_actions.txt) has `effect = { scope:actor = { tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect = yes } }`, and `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` used to call `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes` inline before scheduling `trigger_event_non_silently = { id = tv_engineering_department.202 days = 1 }`. Because the destroy call lived inside the directly-evaluated chain, the button's preview pass still tried (and failed) to promote TARGET_BUILDING for the THIRD_DESTROY_BUILDING_EFFECT loc string, even with the owner-link fix already applied. Fixed by removing `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes` from `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` entirely and adding it to event `tv_engineering_department.202`'s `option` effect instead — the event already fires asynchronously via the existing `trigger_event_non_silently ... days = 1` call, so the destroy now runs after the button's own preview pass has already rendered, from within an already-scoped event option rather than a hover-evaluated generic_action effect chain. This generalizes rule 9 in `docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md` (keep `hidden_effect` light, defer heavy chains to a triggered event's `immediate`) to also cover a generic_action's own non-hidden `effect =` body: any destroy_building call anywhere in that chain is pre-evaluated for the button tooltip, so it must be deferred to a separately-triggered event rather than run inline. | 2026-07-16: error.log kept showing the TARGET_BUILDING/THIRD_DESTROY_BUILDING_EFFECT error after the owner-link fix (commit 431810e7) had already landed, proving the two bugs were distinct and the first fix alone was insufficient. Moved `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes` out of `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` and into `tv_engineering_department.202`'s option effect. All three callers of `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` (the player button, an on_action auto-completion path, and an events.txt call) still schedule event 202 identically, so the demolition still happens on every completion path — it is now deferred until the event's option resolves instead of running synchronously inside the completion effect. |
| advisory | gui | any | Gating a GUI visibility toggle on a proxy variable's mere existence (e.g. `tv_wonder_active_part`, 'which part is currently targeted') when the intended condition is a different, related-but-distinct fact (e.g. 'is real monthly progress > 0 actually accumulating right now'). | `tv_wonder_active_part` gets assigned by `tv_wonder_monthly_construction_effect` purely from `tv_wonder_organization_logistics_unlocked_trigger` (debate/lock/site-selected/not-complete) plus 'some part is still below max' — it says nothing about whether the labor camp is actually staffed (`tv_wonder_has_organized_labor_trigger` / `total_effective_building_levels:tv_wonder_labor_camp > 0`) or whether `tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress` is actually nonzero this month. A wonder can have `tv_wonder_active_part` set while producing zero real progress (e.g. an unstaffed Labor Camp), so a GUI toggle built on `active_part.IsSet` alone silently drifts from the intended 'is there a genuinely working site' condition. Add an explicit check on the real underlying variable (`GreaterThan_CFixedPoint(GetVariable('tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress').GetValue, '(CFixedPoint)0.0')`) alongside the proxy, rather than trusting the proxy to always track it. | 2026-07-15: user reported that the 'when monthly progress is 0, show no available work site instead of a remaining-time estimate' behavior had stopped working. Traced to the construction card's two visibility conditions (added 2026-07-13, commit 469ab549) having always been gated purely on `tv_wonder_active_part.IsSet` — a proxy that was never actually tied to real progress, since the monthly auto-advance branch that assigns `tv_wonder_active_part` does not check `tv_wonder_has_organized_labor_trigger` the way the four manual tv_wonder_begin_foundation/body/function/decoration actions already do. Added `GreaterThan_CFixedPoint(... tv_wonder_monthly_construction_progress ..., 0)` to both visibility conditions (AND'd with the existing active_part check) so the GUI now reflects genuine 0-progress stalls (e.g. an unstaffed Labor Camp) instead of only 'no part selected at all'. Left tv_wonder_monthly_construction_effect's script-side auto-advance logic untouched since the underlying construction math already self-corrects (0 progress never crosses a completion threshold) — this was purely a display-layer gap. |
