| lint | script | any | Authoring `ceremony.stages[i].cost` in `data/unique_wonders.yaml` with 2 entries (e.g. one `country_reward`/`local_reward`/`character_reward` resource cost plus one `country_modifier`/`local_modifier` debuff) instead of exactly 1. | The Unique Wonder Ceremony's 8-stage design (docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 20) requires **exactly one** authored cost per stage — odd steps (1/3/5/7) at ×1 magnitude, even steps (2/4/6/8) at ×2 magnitude via `ceremony_cost_stage_multiplier` — with steps 2/4/8 additionally carrying the one-time/local-building/national reward channels. `_validate_ceremony_stage_cost` in `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/_core.py` previously allowed `1-2` entries, which let 120 of 121 wonders (740 of 968 stages) drift to 2 costs per stage during initial authoring; the generator (`wonder_ceremony_lib.py:ceremony_cost_effect_lines`) faithfully emitted every authored entry, so players saw 2 cost lines in the pay-option tooltip instead of 1. Fixed by tightening the validator to `len(items) != 1` (hard error) and re-authoring all 120 wonders via a per-wonder judgment pass (matching each stage's narrative flavor to the single cost that fits best) through `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/merge_ceremony_cost_options.py`. When authoring or reviewing new `ceremony.stages[i].cost` entries, always author (and expect) exactly 1 `{catalog, type}` per stage — the validator now rejects 0, 2, or more. | 2026-07-15: user reported '很多步骤有两个代价' (many steps have two costs) against the original 1-cost-per-step / 2-4-8-reward-channel / odd-1x-even-2x design. Confirmed 740/968 ceremony stages had 2 cost entries. User directed a per-stage judgment pass (pick whichever cost fits that stage's own title/desc narrative) rather than a mechanical positional rule, since the 2-cost pairs had no consistent catalog ordering. Fanned out 10 parallel agents (one per ~12-wonder batch) to decide per stage, merged via merge_ceremony_cost_options.py, then tightened the schema validator so this can't silently reappear. |
| advisory | script | any | A raw hard comparison between two variables (`var:X <= var:Y`, `var:X > var:Y`, etc.) placed after a `has_variable = X` / `has_variable = Y` guard earlier in the SAME `AND` block, instead of checking whether the comparison's dynamism is even needed on that code path before patching its RHS syntax. | `has_variable` guards preceding a raw two-variable comparison inside the same `AND` do NOT prevent the engine's tooltip/effect-preview pre-evaluation pass from independently evaluating that comparison line and logging 'Event target link var returned an unset scope' / 'Invalid left side during comparison var' / 'Invalid right side during comparison var' / 'Failed to fetch variable ... due to not being set' — that preview pass evaluates each condition line independently rather than truly short-circuiting the AND at the first false guard. Two syntax-only fixes were tried on `tv_wonder_has_selected_ceremony_trigger`'s `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_style <= var:tv_wonder_locked_style_count` line and both failed: wrapping as `var:X ?= { this <= var:Y }` (still invalid — inside the `?=` block, scope has changed to the *value* of X, so a bare `var:Y` RHS can't resolve there), then `var:X ?= { this <= root.var:Y }` (syntactically valid per `reference_game_files/game/in_game/events/disaster/castilian_civil_war.txt:2446`, but STILL failed in practice). The real root cause was structural, not syntactic, per risk_cards/wonders.md rule 10: `tv_wonder_locked_style_count` is a *derived cache variable* written by `tv_wonder_index_cache_locked_wonder_attributes_effect` and read back by this same trigger later in the *same pre-evaluated chain* (`tv_wonder_choose_ceremony_style_effect`'s non-hidden `effect=` -> `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect` -> writes the cache -> calls this trigger to read it) — during tooltip/allow preview the write hasn't actually committed yet when the read happens, so no RHS syntax fixes it. The actual resolution was simpler still: the comparison was solving a non-problem. `tv_wonder_has_selected_ceremony_trigger` (via `tv_wonder_can_choose_ceremony_style_trigger`'s `NOT = { tv_wonder_unique_locked_trigger = yes }` guard) is reachable only for *generic* wonders, which always have exactly 3 fixed ceremony styles (`tv_wonder_choose_ceremony_style_1/2/3` are the only actions that ever set this variable) — unique wonders use a completely separate ritual system (`tv_wonder_selected_ritual_*` variables) and never reach this trigger. The dynamic `tv_wonder_locked_style_count` (driven by `ceremony_styles()`/`final_buildings` in `scripts_engineering_department/wonder_mechanics/_core.py`, genuinely variable per *unique* wonder) was introduced into this *generic*-wonder-only trigger by mistake in commit 66b5edf5 ('implement wonder global variable map', 2026-06-04), replacing the original literal `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_style ?= { this <= 3 }` (from the trigger's introducing commit d394ac0a) that had never had this bug. Restored the literal `<= 3` form and dropped the now-unneeded `has_variable = tv_wonder_ceremony_style` / `has_variable = tv_wonder_locked_style_count` guards. General lesson: two consecutive failed syntax-only fixes on the same two-variable comparison is a strong signal to check the git history / design intent for whether the dynamism is even reachable on that path, rather than trying a third RHS variant. | 2026-07-16: user reported errors 'kept happening' (持续报错) while constructing a generic wonder, then after a first `?=`-wrap syntax fix, reported it errored again with a new message ('Invalid right side during comparison var'), then after a second `root.var:` syntax fix, said the fix was still ineffective ("修复无效，我已回退") and asked to look at how the trigger was originally written instead of continuing to patch the comparison syntax. `git log -S"tv_wonder_has_selected_ceremony_trigger"`/`-S"tv_wonder_locked_style_count"` found the trigger's original form (commit d394ac0a) used a literal `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_style ?= { this <= 3 }` with zero history of this error, and pinpointed commit 66b5edf5 as the exact change that introduced the two-variable comparison and thus the bug. The user then clarified the actual design: all generic wonders have exactly 3 ceremony styles; unique wonders never reach this trigger (separate ritual system) — so the 'dynamic style count' the two prior fix attempts were trying to make safe was never a real requirement on this path, it was a copy-paste-era mistake from the global-variable-map refactor. Restored the literal `<= 3` original form (deleting the now-pointless `has_variable` guards) and validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report passed. Key process lesson for future sessions: two consecutive failed syntax-only fixes on the same comparison line is a strong signal to check the git history / design intent for whether the dynamism is even needed, rather than trying a third RHS variant. |
| advisory | script | any | Rendering a `country_reward` gold cost via bare `add_gold = <N>` (a flat literal amount) when the design intent is an economy-scaled loss. | `data/cost_reward_units.yaml`'s `country_reward` catalog originally had an id `gold` (value: 1) that `wonder_ceremony_lib.py:CEREMONY_COST_COUNTRY_REWARD_EFFECTS` mapped straight to bare `add_gold = <value>`. The user clarified the intended semantics was always 'lose gold at scale=1' — i.e. the vanilla `change_gold_effect = { scale = <N> }` scripted effect (which computes the actual gold delta from the country's `capital_wealth`/`country_economical_base`), matching the sibling `scaled_gold` catalog entry already used everywhere else in the Wonder Ritual system (Pharos/Hagia/tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt/tv_academy_philosophy_debate_effects.txt all use `change_gold_effect = { scale = ±1..±5 }`, never bare `add_gold` for a scaled cost). Renamed the catalog id from `gold` to `scaled_gold` (same base value=1, so no `ceremony_cost_computed_value` magnitude changed) and added an explicit `scaled_gold` branch in `ceremony_cost_effect_lines()` that renders `change_gold_effect = { scale = <value> }` instead of going through the flat-scalar `CEREMONY_COST_COUNTRY_REWARD_EFFECTS` map. Do not add a new flat-literal `gold` cost id back to this catalog — any gold-flavored ceremony cost must render through `change_gold_effect`, consistent with `docs/knowledge/anti_patterns.yaml` rule `flat_gold_cost_when_dynamic_event_cost_intended`'s general guidance that dynamic/repeatable costs should scale with the economy, not use a flat literal. | 2026-07-15: user flagged this immediately after the exactly-1-cost-per-stage fix landed: '所有的gold代价直接写了数值，其实原始数据的含义是失去scale=1的gold'. Confirmed via the sibling scaled_gold usage across Pharos/Hagia/philosophy-debate effects that change_gold_effect with small integer scale (±1 to ±5) is the established convention for gold costs tied to economy scale, and that add_gold's value-block form has no scale sub-key at all (scale is a change_gold_effect macro parameter, not part of add_gold's syntax). Renamed the catalog id and added the dedicated render branch rather than inventing new add_gold syntax. |
| advisory | events | any | Calling an effect that runs `create_international_organization` (with nested `add_country_to_international_organization` / `set_leader_country`) directly inside a normal (non-`immediate`) event `option = { ... }` effect chain, especially when it is the option's only/first effect. | Event option effect chains are pre-evaluated to render the option's tooltip before the player confirms. `add_country_to_international_organization` and `set_leader_country` each render their own tooltip line (`THIRD_ADD_TO_INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION_EFFECT`, `SET_LEADER_EFFECT`) by promoting an `INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION` scope — but during preview the option's own `create_international_organization` block has not actually run yet, so that promote returns nullptr and the engine logs `Promote 'INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION' returned nullptr` for both `GetName` and `GetLeaderTitle`, with a `Data error in loc string` for each of the two effect tooltip keys. Move the whole IO-creation call (and any join/leader-assignment nested inside the same `create_international_organization` block) to the event's `immediate` block instead, so the IO is real, committed state by the time the option renders its tooltip. `immediate` runs unconditionally and is not tooltip-previewed the way option effects are. Keep the option itself for player-facing acknowledgment/hidden bookkeeping (e.g. clearing a pending flag) only. | 2026-07-16: user reported that the Engineering Department submod's game-start onboarding event should move IO creation to `immediate`, because the current country's join/become-leader would fail to find the target IO during pre-evaluation. error.log confirmed two nullptr promotes for `INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION.GetName`/`GetLeaderTitle` against loc strings `THIRD_ADD_TO_INTERNATIONAL_ORGANIZATION_EFFECT`/`SET_LEADER_EFFECT`. The event (`tv_engineering_department_standalone.1`, single option, no prior `immediate` block) called `tv_engineering_department_create_effect = yes` directly as the option's first effect; that scripted effect's `else` branch creates the IO and nests `add_country_to_international_organization = prev` / `set_leader_country = prev` inside the same `create_international_organization = { ... }` block, matching rule 19's existing guidance to keep IO-scoped helpers out of the creation block root — but rule 19 did not cover the fact that the creation call itself, when left in a visible option rather than `immediate`, still triggers a preview-time nullptr for the vanilla join/leader tooltip text. Fixed by moving `tv_engineering_department_create_effect = yes` into the event's `immediate` block and leaving the option with only its existing `hidden_effect` (pending-flag cleanup). validate.py --changed --fix passed with 0 errors/warnings. |
| advisory | gui | any | A GUI generator rewrite that adds a new bespoke UI path (here: the unique-wonder ceremony framework's Pharos/Hagia bespoke confirm buttons) deletes the generic-case code block it's replacing without checking whether the generic case still needs its own equivalent. | Every player-facing generic_action in this mod's Engineering Department panel (including `tv_wonder_confirm_ceremony`/`_scaled_gold`/`_prestige`) is reachable ONLY through an explicitly hand-authored widget in this generator — these are not vanilla-surfaced generic actions with an automatic fallback UI. Commit 5a56683e ('add unique ceremony framework and update UI elements', 2026-07-12) rewrote `scripts_engineering_department/in_game/gui/panels/organization/gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py`'s `TV_WONDER_MECHANICS_HOLD_BUTTONS` section to add the two bespoke Pharos/Hagia `hold_button()` calls (wonder id 101/102 specifically), and in the same edit deleted the pre-existing generic-wonder block (`not_special_unique_locked_expr()`/`selected_ritual_cost_visible()` + three `hold_button()` calls for `tv_wonder_confirm_ceremony`/`_scaled_gold`/`_prestige` gated to 'any locked wonder that is not Pharos/Hagia') without adding a replacement. The underlying generic_actions, their `allow=` trigger chains, and the cache-effect pipeline that populates `tv_wonder_selected_ritual_*` variables were all still fully correct and functional — the regression was pure GUI wiring: no widget anywhere called `left_action = { action_name = "tv_wonder_confirm_ceremony..." }` for a non-unique wonder, so the button silently vanished for every generic wonder after choosing a ceremony style, while Pharos/Hagia (the only two wonders exercised by the accompanying test) kept working — masking the regression from `scripts_engineering_department/test_wonder_mechanics_rules.py`, which only asserted the Pharos/Hagia buttons exist and (over-broadly) that the shared button-must NOT appear at all, never asserting a generic-wonder path still worked. Fixed by restoring the generic hold-button block, reusing the already-present `generic_wonder_locked_expr()` helper (a cleaner equivalent to the deleted `not_special_unique_locked_expr()`, added by a later commit for the ceremony-style-choice buttons) instead of re-adding a redundant helper, and narrowing the test's blanket 'must not be emitted' assertions to 'must be emitted, gated on `tv_wonder_locked_is_unique`' so Pharos/Hagia and generic wonders can't double-render buttons. General lesson: when a generator rewrite adds a bespoke special-case path, explicitly verify (or write a test asserting) that the generic/default case this special-case is carved out of still has its own working path — do not assume deleting the special case's old home also means the generic case has nowhere else to live. | 2026-07-16: after the ceremony-style two-variable comparison bug was fixed (see [[preceding_has_variable_does_not_guard_a_later_raw_two_variable_comparison]]), user reported the bottom execute/confirm button for a generic wonder's ceremony no longer appeared after choosing a style ('这个问题是解决了，为什么底部用于执行的按钮又不显示了'), again not knowing which commit broke it. Dispatched a dedicated read-only investigation (Explore agent) to trace the full allow=/cache chain end to end rather than guessing; it confirmed the generic_action/effect/trigger/cache pipeline was internally consistent (regenerated the relevant generators in-memory and byte-matched the committed .txt output) and isolated the actual break to `git log -S` on the GUI generator, finding commit 5a56683e deleted the generic-wonder hold-button block. Restored it (reusing `generic_wonder_locked_expr()` rather than the original deleted helper, since a newer commit had already added an equivalent for the style-choice buttons), regenerated+merged the GUI fragment, and updated the test's over-broad 'must never appear' assertions to the correct 'must appear, gated away from Pharos/Hagia' shape. `test_wonder_mechanics_rules.py` and `validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report` both passed after the fix. |
| advisory | gui | any | Gating GUI text on a generic state flag (`tv_wonder_ceremony_locked`, meaning only 'the ceremony style choice is now finalized/uneditable') as if it uniquely identified one specific cause of that state (here: 'this style was auto-reused from an earlier expansion'), when the same flag is set identically for the unrelated first-time-confirm case. | `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked` is set to 1 inside every generic ritual's `tv_wonder_mechanics_start_*_ritual_effect` (tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt, ~60 near-identical call sites, one per wonder/style) as soon as the player confirms and the ritual actually starts — this happens identically whether the wonder is being built for the very first time or is a resumed/expanded project reusing an earlier ceremony choice. The GUI card at `tv_engineering_department.gui`'s ceremony section showed `TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_LOCKED_BY_EXPANSION_TEXT` ('this is an expansion of an existing wonder, so it reuses its prior ceremony') whenever `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked.IsSet` and the wonder is not unique — with no check for whether it was actually an expansion — so every normal first-time generic wonder ceremony wrongly displayed the expansion message after confirming. The real distinguishing signal is `tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2` (already the established 'this project is a resume/expansion' marker used elsewhere, e.g. `tv_wonder_project_base_level`'s snapshot guard, docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 29), which persists unchanged from proposal time through the ceremony stage (only cleared on abandon/reset, never on normal completion). Fixed by ANDing `tv_wonder_project_mode == 2` into the expansion text's visibility and adding a sibling `TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_STYLE_CONFIRMED_TEXT` card (visible when locked but project_mode != 2) so the first-time-confirm case gets its own accurate message instead of silently falling through to the expansion one. General lesson: when a boolean 'locked/confirmed/done' flag is set from multiple different code paths for different reasons, GUI text that narrates *why* it's locked must check the specific reason's own variable, not just the shared flag. | 2026-07-16: user reported that after locking in a ceremony for what was actually a brand-new (non-expansion) generic wonder, the panel inexplicably showed '这是对已有奇观的扩建...' (this is an expansion of an existing wonder...). Traced `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked` to ~60 set sites, all inside per-ritual 'start' effects triggered by the confirm button for ANY generic ceremony, not specifically expansions. The GUI text's visibility only checked this shared flag plus non-unique, with no `tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2` (the actual resume/expansion marker) guard. Also fixed a related missing-prompt gap reported in the same session: the row of three ceremony-style-choice buttons had no heading text at all ('从如下三个仪式中选择一个' never existed — confirmed via `git log -S` across all localization history, not a regression) — added `TV_ENGINEERING_CEREMONY_CHOOSE_STYLE_PROMPT` as a new text_multi above the button row, visible under the same `Not(ceremony_locked)` condition as the buttons themselves. validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report passed with 0 errors/warnings after both fixes. |
| advisory | events | any | Deferring an option/button effect's reward to a `trigger_event_silently = { id = X days = N }` call, when the deferred event's `immediate` effect is not otherwise proven too heavy/unsafe to run inline, purely to 'match the async pattern used elsewhere.' | EU5's generic_action/event-option tooltip pre-evaluation only walks the option's own `effect =` body — it does not follow a `trigger_event_silently`/`trigger_event_non_silently` call into the target event's `immediate` block. So any reward effect placed only inside that deferred event is executed correctly at runtime but never rendered in the button's tooltip preview. The Unique Wonder Ceremony's shared 8-stage framework (docs/knowledge/risk_cards/wonders.md rule 20) had this exact asymmetry: stages 2/4 called their reward dispatch effects inline in `tv_wonder_ceremony_advance_to_stage_{2,4}_effect`, rendering both cost and reward in the tooltip, while stage 8 only scheduled a hidden completion event (`tv_engineering_department.9308`) whose `immediate` contained the real payoff — so stage 8's button showed only the cost. Before accepting a deferral like this as necessary (per rule 9/31's guidance for genuinely heavy/unsafe chains — `destroy_building`, `create_international_organization`, character-title promotion, or other operations documented to break tooltip preview), verify the deferred effect body actually contains one of those unsafe operations or a proven pre-evaluation error. In this case it did not: the completion chain was `add_country_modifier` plus variable bookkeeping and one `every_international_organizations_member_of` read-only loop — lighter than stage 4's own inline 66-branch `construct_building` switch, and the identical chain (`tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect`) already ran inline from several `on_action` hidden_effects for every ritual in progress. Fixed by calling `tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect` inline in stage 8's advance effect (matching stages 2/4) and deleting the now-unnecessary hidden event and `COMPLETION_EVENT_ID` constant. | 2026-07-16: user reported that in the 8-step Unique Wonder Ceremony (applies to 121 unique wonders), steps 2 and 4's options rendered both cost and reward in the tooltip, but step 8 only rendered cost. Traced to `append_advance_stage_effects()` in gen_tv_wonder_ceremony_effects.py: steps 2/4 call their reward effects inline, step 8 instead only called `trigger_event_silently = { id = tv_engineering_department.9308 days = 1 }`, whose immediate block held the real completion reward. When the user asked 'why is this heavy, isn't it just applying a country modifier,' traced the full completion chain (`tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect` -> `tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect` -> completion/static-modifier effects + `tv_wonder_finalize_effect`) and confirmed it really is just that (plus bookkeeping), with no `construct_building`/location loops/world-news calls, and confirmed via git history that the deferral had no stage-8-specific incident behind it — it was introduced for consistency with the async pattern used for stage 4's later building-finalization event and for Pharos/Hagia's bespoke rituals. Removed the deferral, deleted event 9308 and the unused `COMPLETION_EVENT_ID` constant, and updated `test_wonder_mechanics_rules.py`'s assertions accordingly. |
| advisory | events | any | Deferring a `destroy_building`/`destroy_building_forcefully` call out of a generic_action's own `effect =` body into a separately-triggered event's `option`, and assuming that deferral alone (without also using `hidden_effect`) is enough to escape tooltip/effect-preview pre-evaluation. | Event options get the exact same hover/tooltip pre-evaluation walk that generic_action buttons do — the fix for [[destroy_building_forcefully_in_button_effect_chain_fails_tooltip_preview_even_with_owner_link]] moved `tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect` out of `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` (a button's own effect body) into `tv_engineering_department.202`'s `option`, but the option still called it directly (not inside `hidden_effect`), so the same pre-evaluation error persisted unchanged. `hidden_effect` is the only block the pre-evaluation pass actually skips, regardless of how many scopes/events the real call is deferred through. Fixed by wrapping the call in `hidden_effect = { tv_wonder_destroy_labor_camp_effect = yes }` and adding a sibling `custom_tooltip = <key>` line so the option still shows correct static tooltip text — this is vanilla's own pattern (e.g. `earthquake_events.txt`'s `earthquake_events_minor.a`/`.b` options pair `hidden_effect` with `custom_tooltip` the same way). | 2026-07-16: user reported the wonder-finish worksite demolition still errored even after it had already been moved into event tv_engineering_department.202's option (per the rule 31 fix). The generalization is: deferring a heavy/unsafe effect call to *any* visible effect body — button, event option, whatever — never escapes pre-evaluation by itself; only `hidden_effect` does. Wrapped the call in `hidden_effect` and added `custom_tooltip = tv_engineering_department.202.a.tt` (with matching English/Simplified Chinese loc) so the option keeps a correct static tooltip. validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report passed with 0 errors/warnings after the fix. |
| lint | enum | character | Using `type = character` in a common/generic_actions definition | Generic actions cannot use the `character` EGenericActionType in the current engine build. Keep country/situation controls as supported generic actions, and define an action that appears on a character's lateral-view or context menu under common/character_interactions instead. Give that interaction a character select_trigger with target_flag = recipient, then use scope:recipient after the selector has supplied it. | The generic_actions readme still lists character in a stale type comment, but runtime rejects every `type = character` definition with `Failed to read enum! Type: EGenericActionType, Val character`. |
| needs_parser | scope | character | A targeted character interaction uses scope:recipient in allow or effect without a character select_trigger that sets target_flag = recipient | Add select_trigger = { looking_for_a = character source = actor target_flag = recipient ... }. Character-interaction registration does not automatically pass the viewed/menu character as recipient; target eligibility belongs in the selector visible/enabled blocks, and recipient-dependent effects remain guarded with exists = scope:recipient. | Without the selector, the engine rejects the action at load with `Targetted action <id> doesn't specify a recipient for the action`, even when its allow/effect body refers to scope:recipient. |
| lint | scope | character | Entering a saved Court Position character variable as bare `tv_court_*_character ?= { ... }` | Enter the character stored in a variable with `var:tv_court_*_character ?= { ... }`. The `var:` prefix is required for a variable scope link. | A bare variable name is parsed as an effect identifier, producing `Unknown effect tv_court_*_character`; `var:` enters the stored character scope safely when the variable exists. |
| advisory | localization | any | Defining a situation without localization keys matching its exact id and `<id>_desc` | For every situation id `<id>`, generate `<id>: "Display Name"` and `<id>_desc: "Description"` in every supported localization. Panel-only title/intro keys do not satisfy the situation database's own localization lookup. | A situation can render its custom panel title while still logging the fallback id and `TODO : Write a desc` for its database name/description when these standard keys are absent. |
| advisory | gui | any | Rendering per-entity dynamic inline text-icons (`@icon_name!`) at one shared `fontsize` on a `text_single`, and assuming every icon name renders at the same visible pixel size because the call site's fontsize is identical. | `reference_game_files/game/main_menu/gui/shared/font_icons.gui` defines every `texticon` block's `iconsize.size`/`iconsize.fontsize` independently per icon name, with no shared convention: e.g. `construction` is 14x14 native at fontsize 14, `wealth` is 20x20 at fontsize 18, `topography`/`army_tradition`/`prestige` range 24x24-26x26 at fontsize 16. The engine scales the glyph as `native_size * (call_fontsize / native_fontsize)`, so calling every icon at one shared fontsize (here, 17, in the Unique Wonder Ceremony's 8 status cards, one inline icon per wonder per stage picked from `data/unique_wonders.yaml`'s `ceremony.stages[i].icon`) produced visibly different pixel sizes per wonder (a ~14px icon next to a ~26px icon in the same 52x52 piechart slot), plus inconsistent vertical centering from each icon's own baked-in `offset`. Verified (Step 3, `reference_official_defines` + `reference_game_files`) that there is no per-instance size-override syntax for inline icon markup (no `@icon|size!` form), and no vanilla or existing-codebase precedent for resolving a GUI `texture` field via `Localize()`/`Custom()` to fetch a per-entity dynamic icon path (that path is text-only). The correct fix is NOT a new icon widget with a dynamically-resolved texture (would need ~650 dispatch widgets with long `Or`-chained `visible` expressions across 121 wonders x 8 stages x 2 piechart states — real GUI bloat and per-frame eval cost) and NOT curating icon choices to already-similar sizes (data-only triage, not pixel-perfect). Instead, define a PARALLEL set of `texticon` aliases — one per distinct icon name actually used — each reusing the same vanilla texture but with a uniform `size`/`offset`/`fontsize` matching the call site's own fontsize (so the render-time scale factor collapses to exactly 1 for every icon). Mods CAN declare their own `texticon` blocks reusing a vanilla texture with custom sizing (precedent: `reference_mods/3601047146/main_menu/gui/shared/glorpui_font_icons.gui`, `reference_mods/3599735023/in_game/gui/IV2_texticons.gui`). Implemented via `scripts_engineering_department/main_menu/gui/shared/gen_tv_ceremony_font_icons_gui.py`, which derives the distinct icon-name list from the ceremony data itself (not hardcoded) and looks up each name's real texture path from a new `FONT_ICON_TEXTURES` map in `wonder_mechanics/_core.py` (a texture-capturing sibling of the existing `FONT_ICON_DECL_RE`/`_load_font_icon_names()`/`SUPPORTED_CEREMONY_STAGE_ICONS` name validator). The two ceremony localization generators then emit `@tv_ceremony_<name>!` instead of `@<name>!` via a new `ceremony_icon_alias()` helper in `wonder_ceremony_lib.py` — zero changes to the `.gui` call sites, zero new widgets, zero runtime dispatch changes. | 2026-07-17: user reported the 8-step Unique Wonder Ceremony's left-side piechart center icon sizes were inconsistent and generally too small, asking whether inline text icons could be replaced with a normal icon component. Investigated the GUI icon display tier order first (a real `icon` widget needs a dynamic texture, which has no engine precedent via Localize/Custom — verification failed), then a grouped-conditional-icon-widget alternative (rejected for ~650-widget bloat given 121 wonders), before landing on the texticon-alias approach, confirmed by mod precedent. Icon size was set to 22x22 initially, then increased to 32x32 per user follow-up request (still fits inside the 52x52 piechart slot with margin). `test_wonder_mechanics_rules.py` and `scripts/validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report` both passed after the fix (0 errors/warnings). |
| advisory | script | any | Gating a button/option's own definite-completion effect through a shared "maybe complete" helper whose guard trigger re-reads a variable that same option's effect chain just wrote a few lines earlier, when the caller already knows the condition is true and does not need to re-derive it. | EU5's option/button tooltip pre-evaluation does not reliably thread a `set_variable` write across several nested `scripted_effect`/`scripted_trigger` call layers within the SAME previewed chain: even though the write and the read are both inside the one call being previewed, and both run correctly (in write-then-read order) at real execution time, the preview pass can still evaluate the nested trigger against the pre-write value, producing a false result with no error (a false `limit`, not a script fault). The Unique Wonder Ceremony's stage-8 button (`tv_wonder_ceremony_advance_to_stage_8_effect`) writes `set_variable = { name = tv_wonder_ceremony_stage value = 8 }` then calls `tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect = yes`, which routes through `tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect`'s `tv_wonder_selected_ritual_completion_requirements_met_trigger` guard — for the ceremony framework this trigger literally checks `var:tv_wonder_ceremony_stage ?= 8`, the SAME variable just written two lines above. The tooltip preview read back the still-7 value, so the whole `if` block containing the completion reward (`add_country_modifier`) rendered as unreachable and was silently omitted — cost showed, reward did not, with a clean error.log. Making the completion call inline (undoing the [[deferred_reward_via_trigger_event_silently_invisible_to_tooltip_preview]] deferral) was necessary but not sufficient; the real fix was to stop re-deriving "is this complete" via the shared trigger-gated helper when the caller already knows it is. Added `tv_wonder_mechanics_force_complete_active_ritual_effect`, a duplicate of `tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect`'s body without the `completion_requirements_met_trigger` gate, and had the stage-8 button call it directly. General lesson: when a button/option's effect chain establishes a fact about itself (by writing a variable) and then needs to act on that fact, do not route back through a shared "maybe/check-then-act" helper that re-derives the fact via a nested trigger read of the same variable — call the unconditional "definite" version of that helper directly. Reserve the trigger-gated shared helper for callers (monthly pulses, other ritual modes) that genuinely do not know in advance whether the condition holds. | 2026-07-16: after fixing the deferred-reward tooltip gap (see [[deferred_reward_via_trigger_event_silently_invisible_to_tooltip_preview]]), the user redeployed via build.bat and re-tested in game — the stage-8 tooltip still showed only cost, error.log clean. Traced the full guard chain for wonder 103 (ritual id 10301): tv_wonder_complete_active_ritual_effect -> tv_wonder_mechanics_maybe_complete_active_ritual_effect -> tv_wonder_selected_ritual_completion_requirements_met_trigger -> tv_wonder_selected_ritual_custom_completion_requirements_met_trigger's 10301 branch checks var:tv_wonder_ceremony_stage ?= 8, which the SAME advance_to_stage_8_effect had just set two lines earlier. Confirmed via git history and code reading that tv_wonder_ritual_in_progress, var:tv_wonder_stage (the separate overall build-stage variable, stays 4 throughout since tv_wonder_finalize_effect only sets it to 5 AFTER the static-modifier call), and the ritual-id/has_custom_completion_trigger cache variables (set once at construction-finalization time, never clobbered by other cache-refresh call sites which all key off the acting country's own tv_wonder_locked/tv_wonder_ceremony_style) were all genuinely fine — the only remaining suspect was the just-written tv_wonder_ceremony_stage read-back. First attempt at the effect edit accidentally landed in the wrong function in gen_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.py (this module exports several independent generate_*() entry points — generate() for tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_effects.txt, generate_ritual_effects() via append_ritual_effects() for tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt — and the new effect needed to go inside append_ritual_effects, not before it); running the wrong module's own main() regenerated the wrong output file and silently left the real target unchanged, confirmed by grep showing the new effect name absent from tv_wonder_ritual_effects.txt after the first regen. Relocated the edit, ran gen_tv_wonder_ritual_effects.py specifically, and confirmed the new tv_wonder_mechanics_force_complete_active_ritual_effect appears in the generated file and the ceremony stage-8 effect calls it. |
| advisory | script | country | A variable meant to gate "has this construction/ritual cycle already completed" is set once on completion and cleared only on full project abandonment, while a separate resume/expansion code path re-enters the SAME stage machinery for a NEW cycle on the SAME object without ever clearing that gate. | When a mechanic supports repeated cycles on one persistent object (a generic Engineering Department wonder's expand-then-hold-ceremony-again loop, `tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2`), any one-shot "this cycle is done" variable must be explicitly cleared at the start of each new cycle, not just on abandonment. `tv_wonder_ceremony_ready_trigger` (`tv_engineering_department_triggers.txt:418-423`) gates the ceremony conditions/rewards/hold-button display on `stage==4 AND NOT has_variable = tv_wonder_finalized`. `tv_wonder_finalized` is set once by `tv_wonder_finalize_effect` and was previously cleared ONLY by the full-abandonment cleanup (`tv_wonder_clear_project_state_core_effect`). The expansion-mode branch of `tv_wonder_select_construction_site_effect` (added by commit afd14d65, 2026-06-11, well before `tv_wonder_finalized` even existed as a concept the branch needed to interact with) resets `tv_wonder_stage = 3` and other construction counters to let the wonder build past its previous completed level and re-reach stage 4, but never cleared `tv_wonder_finalized` — so after a wonder's FIRST ceremony completion, every later expansion cycle reached stage 4 again with `tv_wonder_finalized` still set from the prior cycle, permanently failing `tv_wonder_ceremony_ready_trigger`. The GUI's downstream cache-populate effect then took its else-branch (`tv_wonder_index_clear_selected_ritual_cache_effect`) every time, wiping the cached ritual id/cost-type the conditions/rewards/hold-button widgets key off — while a SEPARATE, unrelated "ceremony style locked" static text (gated only on `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked.IsSet`, which is correctly meant to persist across expansions per design so the player cannot re-pick a style) kept rendering, making the wonder look permanently stuck with no way to ever hold the ceremony again. Fix: clear ONLY the one-shot completion gate (`remove_variable = tv_wonder_finalized`) inside the expansion branch (`if = { limit = { var:tv_wonder_project_mode ?= 2 } ... }`) — do NOT clear `tv_wonder_ceremony_locked`/`tv_wonder_ceremony_style`, since design intent is that an expansion reuses the previously-chosen style and only needs the player to click the hold/confirm button again, not re-pick. When two variables gate overlapping-looking UI (one meant to persist across cycles, one meant to reset per cycle), verify each one's clear points independently against the actual multi-cycle design intent instead of assuming a single "locked" flag's persistence also implies the completion gate should persist. | 2026-07-20: user reported that for a generic wonder, after choosing ceremony style 1, every EXPANSION of that wonder shows a static "style 1 locked" text with no conditions/rewards/hold-button, and the ceremony can never be held again. Traced via two background research passes plus direct git archaeology: the gap predates the 2026-07 Engineering Department mod split (c371a9af) entirely — `git log -S` on the pre-split file path shows `tv_wonder_finalized` was introduced by d394ac0a and the `project_mode ?= 2` expansion-resume branch was added later by afd14d65, which never accounted for the pre-existing completion gate. Not a regression from a single later change that broke previously-correct behavior; a design gap present since the expansion-resume feature's inception. User confirmed design intent (no re-pick allowed on expansion, but the hold-button must reappear) and confirmed the correct minimal fix is removing only `tv_wonder_finalized`, not the lock/style variables. Follow-up in the same session: fixing the gate alone was not sufficient — the GUI reads a SEPARATE cached layer (`tv_wonder_selected_ritual_id` etc.), populated only when something explicitly calls `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect`. That effect is not reactive; nothing recomputes it just because a raw variable changed. So the added `remove_variable = tv_wonder_finalized` fix (in the load-repair effect) was paired with an explicit `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect = yes` call right after it, and a THIRD, related but distinct gap was found and fixed the same way: `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` (`:1998-2016`) transitions a wonder to `stage = 4` and calls `tv_wonder_initialize_ceremony_runtime_state_effect`, but that callee entire body — including its own cache-refresh call — is gated on `tv_wonder_unique_locked_trigger = yes`, a no-op for generic wonders. A FRESH (not-yet-locked) generic wonder self-heals anyway, because its choose-a-style GUI buttons are gated on a live `NOT tv_wonder_ceremony_locked.IsSet` check (no cache needed), and the player click itself calls `tv_wonder_choose_ceremony_style_effect`, which does refresh the cache. But an EXPANSION whose style is already locked (design intent: no re-pick allowed) never shows those buttons and has no equivalent player click, so nothing would refresh the cache until the next `tv_engineering_department_wonder_auto_cache_monthly_pulse` (up to about a month of the ceremony UI looking stuck, self-resolving, not permanent like the primary bug). Fixed by adding an unconditional `tv_wonder_index_refresh_country_cache_effect = yes` to `tv_wonder_finish_construction_effect` itself — harmless no-op for unique wonders and for fresh generic wonders (which already self-heal), and closes the gap specifically for locked-style expansions. General lesson: when a cache/derived-display layer is populated only by specific named call sites rather than being recomputed reactively, enumerate every state-transition point that could make the underlying condition true and verify each one calls the refresh — a transition that happens to also be reachable via a player click that already refreshes elsewhere can mask a transition that has no such click. |
| advisory | gui | any | Placing a `TooltipStringPairList` inside a fixed-width column (`layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed`, `maximumsize = { W -1 }`) to show `ShowModifierEffect(...)`/`ShowModifier(...)` breakdown rows, without also bounding the row text itself. | Vanilla `TooltipStringPairList` (`reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/cooltip_types.gui`) renders each row through `TooltipStringPairListContent` -> two `TooltipTableColumn` children (label expanding, value fixed), each of which blockoverrides the shared `type TooltipTableColumn`'s `field_text` (`main_menu_cooltip_types.gui` ~L1771: `autoresize = yes` with no `max_width`) down to a `field_text_format` splice block that by default has zero width constraint. A parent's `maximumsize`/`layoutpolicy_horizontal = fixed` only bounds the StringPairList's own declared box; it does not clip or wrap the child `text_single`'s natural single-line width, so long English modifier-effect text (short in Chinese, long in English) renders past the column's right edge with no error in the log. Fix by extending the existing `blockoverride "field_text_format" { fontsize = ... }` (already present at every StringPairList call site in this codebase for font sizing) to also add `autoresize = yes`, `max_width = ...`, and `elide = right` — verified as a valid vanilla `text_single` combination at `reference_game_files/game/in_game/gui/shared/io_tooltips.gui:459-466` (`text_single = { autoresize = yes elide = right using = tooltip_max_width_title_wide text = ... }`). This truncates with an ellipsis instead of overflowing, and does not require touching the vanilla `.gui` template at all, since `field_text_format` is already the caller-facing splice point. IMPORTANT: the label and value columns are two SIDE-BY-SIDE `TooltipTableColumn`/`TooltipTableField` instances sharing the ONE declared column width, and the caller-level `field_text_format` override reaches BOTH columns' identically-named nested block simultaneously (confirmed by the pre-existing shared `fontsize` override applying uniformly). The first fix attempt set `max_width = column_width - 25`, accounting only for the label column's OWN `margin={10,0}` + `spacing=5` chrome (~30px) and ignoring that the adjacent value column reserves its own ~30px chrome plus its own (short but nonzero) rendered text width out of the SAME total — that left almost no headroom for the value column and the overflow persisted. The correct budget is `column_width - (label_chrome ~30) - (value_chrome ~30) - (value_text_reserve ~45)` = `column_width - 105`, not `column_width - 25`. | 2026-07-17: user reported the Engineering Department wonder construction card and the location-page wonder tooltip both call two-column ShowModifierEffect breakdowns inside a fixed ~223px/218px column, and under English localization the modifier text (short enough in Chinese to fit) overflowed past the column's right edge instead of being capped around the expected ~200px content width. Traced the render chain from the mod's own `TooltipStringPairList` call site down through vanilla `cooltip_types.gui`/`main_menu_cooltip_types.gui` to confirm the shared `TooltipTableColumn` type's `field_text` has no built-in width cap, then found `field_text_format` is the existing splice point already used in this codebase for `fontsize` overrides — extended it in both generator scripts (`preview_modifier_column`, `render_modifier_column`) with `autoresize = yes` / `max_width = column_width - 25` / `elide = right`, regenerated via each script plus their merge/compat steps (`merge_tv_engineering_department_wonder_mechanics_gui.py`, `scripts/compat/gen_tv_meiou_and_taxes_location_window.py`), and confirmed `scripts/validate.py --changed --fix --ai-report` passed clean. User then pointed out the chosen width was still too generous and asked for a more careful pass: `column_width - 25` only subtracted the label column's own margin/spacing chrome and ignored that the value column sitting next to it in the same row draws from the SAME declared column width — corrected to `column_width - 105` (label chrome ~30 + value chrome ~30 + value text reserve ~45), regenerated again, `validate.py` re-confirmed clean. User then tested in-game and asked to widen the label back out to a flat `max_width = 140` (wider than the derived ~118/113 estimate, still narrower than the original overflowing 198/193) — set as a literal constant in both generators rather than a `column_width`-derived formula, since the user's in-game check is the authoritative signal here, not the chrome arithmetic estimate. |
| advisory | script | country | Referencing a conditional/dynamic script_value (one with an `if =`, `min =`, `max =`, or any block reading `global_var:`/`var:`) as the RHS of `gold =` (or any other amount field) inside a `common/prices/` price definition. | Keep the price's `gold =` (or equivalent) field a compile-time-static number, or a reference to a simple flat named value (vanilla `build_price_age_of_tradition = 50` style — no braces, no conditional logic). To make the effective cost track a runtime value (a CMM setting, a country variable, ...), keep the base price static and apply the adjustment through the `<price_name>_cost_modifier` tag instead: register an `auto_modifiers` (or `static_modifiers`) entry with `scales_with = { value = global_var:X multiply = ... divide = ... subtract = 1 }` computing the desired percentage delta from the base price, and set `<price_name>_cost_modifier = 1` in that same block so the final applied modifier equals `scales_with`'s value directly. `scales_with` blocks ARE full script_value contexts (proven by vanilla `reference_game_files/game/in_game/common/auto_modifiers/country.txt`, e.g. `scales_with = { value = num_colonial_charters subtract = 1 min = 0 max = 1 }`) and evaluate at runtime in the owning scope, unlike `common/prices/` fields. IMPORTANT: `<price_name>_cost_modifier` is NOT engine-auto-derived — every price's cost_modifier tag, vanilla and TV alike, needs its own EXPLICIT `modifier_type_definitions` entry (`color = bad  percent = yes  game_data = { category = country }`; see `build_gravel_road_cost_modifier` at `reference_game_files/game/main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions/00_modifier_types.txt:802` and every sibling TV price in `src_engineering_department/main_menu/common/modifier_type_definitions/tv_engineering_department_modifier_types.txt`). Skipping that registration produces a SEPARATE, later error: `pdx_persistent_reader.cpp: Unknown modifier type: <name>. It is either invalid, or a potential dynamic modifier type definition missing from the database`, and `price_database.cpp: Missing modifier type for price. <name>` even once the price's own `gold =` field is fixed. | 2026-08-02: a CMM-driven direct-build price feature defined `tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price = { gold = tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price_value }` where the referenced script_value had `if = { limit = { has_global_variable = ... } ... }` branching to read a CMM global_var. In-game this produced `pdx_persistent_reader.cpp:289: Malformed token` plus `jomini_named_values.h: Matching scriptvalue [...], but of wrong type` for every affected price, plus a `price_database.cpp: Missing modifier type for price. <name>_cost_modifier` warning that read, at the time, like a cascading symptom of the malformed price. Fixed round 1 by reverting the price to a flat default and moving the CMM-driven adjustment to a new `auto_modifiers` file with `scales_with` feeding a `<price>_cost_modifier` tag — but that tag was never explicitly registered, on the WRONG assumption (based on `build_gravel_road_cost_modifier` appearing usable in advances/estate-privilege files with no obvious nearby registration) that the engine auto-derives `<price>_cost_modifier` from the price definition itself. In-game round 2 then produced a DIFFERENT, unrelated-looking error: `Unknown modifier type: tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price_cost_modifier ... a potential dynamic modifier type definition missing from the database`, plus the SAME `price_database.cpp: Missing modifier type for price` line recurring even though the price itself now parsed fine — proving the missing-modifier-type warning was never just a symptom of the malformed token; it was reporting a second, independent, still-unfixed gap. Re-grepping `00_modifier_types.txt` directly (rather than trusting the earlier failed reference_mods-wide search) found `build_gravel_road_cost_modifier` explicitly defined there at line 802, and every sibling TV price (`tv_wonder_labor_camp_price`, `tv_wonder_ritual_annex_small_price`, ...) already had its own explicit entry in `tv_engineering_department_modifier_types.txt` all along — the auto-derivation theory was simply wrong. Fixed round 2 by adding the three missing entries to that same file. General lesson: when an identifier 'just works' somewhere without an obvious nearby registration, grep the SPECIFIC registration file directly for that exact identifier before concluding the mechanism is implicit/auto-derived — a quick reference_mods-wide grep that returns zero hits is not proof of absence if the search pattern or directory scope was too narrow. |
| lint | workflow | any | scripts/validate.py's check_modifier_names treated every top-level-looking `word = number` line as a modifier-name assertion, including script_value operator keywords (value/add/subtract/multiply/divide/min/max/...) nested inside an auto_modifiers/static_modifiers entry's `scales_with = { ... }` or `limit = { ... }` sub-block. | check_modifier_names now tracks a per-line brace-depth stack and only applies the modifier-name whitelist check to lines outside any `scales_with`/`limit` nested block. (A separate attempt to also auto-whitelist every `common/prices/*.txt` entry's `<name>_cost_modifier` was reverted — that tag is NOT engine-auto-derived, it requires its own explicit `modifier_type_definitions` entry like any other custom modifier, so auto-whitelisting it would have hidden real missing-registration bugs from validate.py instead of catching them. See price_gold_field_rejects_dynamic_scriptvalue.) | Discovered while fixing the direct-build price bug: a correct, vanilla-proven scales_with block (`scales_with = { value = global_var:X multiply = 1000 divide = 10000 subtract = 1 }`) plus a legitimate `tv_wonder_direct_build_small_price_cost_modifier = 1` line both false-positived as unknown modifiers. Verified the fix against the full repo (`scripts/validate.py --ai-report`, no --changed filter): 288 files checked, 0 errors, 0 warnings — no other auto_modifiers/static_modifiers file relied on the old (incorrect) flat-line behavior. |
| lint | localization | any | Adding a new common/modifier_type_definitions, common/auto_modifiers, or common/prices entry without its required companion localization (and, for modifier types, a common/modifier_icons entry). | Every new modifier_type_definitions entry `<id>` needs MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME_<id> and MODIFIER_TYPE_DESC_<id> in every supported language (English, Simplified Chinese), plus a same-key entry in common/modifier_icons (see custom_modifier_type_missing_modifier_icon). Every new auto_modifiers entry `<id>` needs AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> in every supported language (no DESC key exists anywhere in this codebase — do not add one). Every new common/prices entry `<id>` needs a bare `<id>: "..."` localization key in every supported language (no `_desc` suffix). scripts/validate.py now runs check_modifier_type_definition_loc_and_icon_coverage, check_auto_modifier_name_loc_coverage, and check_price_name_loc_coverage automatically whenever the relevant common/ directories or main_menu/localization/ change. | 2026-08-02: after fixing the direct-build price/cost_modifier registration bugs (price_gold_field_rejects_dynamic_scriptvalue), the user tested in-game again and reported error.log still showed six TODO-placeholder loc warnings (three MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME_/DESC pairs, three AUTO_MODIFIER_NAME_ names) plus three 'Missing Icon for Modifier' warnings plus three bare price-name TODO placeholders — none of the three newly-created files (the modifier_type_definitions registration, the auto_modifiers file, and the price file itself) had been given companion localization, mirroring the EXACT gap the pre-existing STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_<id> check (check_static_modifier_name_loc_coverage) already catches for common/static_modifiers, but that check does not cover modifier_type_definitions/auto_modifiers/prices at all. User explicitly asked for a validator so this class of mistake (new script identifier defined, companion localization forgotten) cannot recur silently. Added three new validate.py checks modeled directly on check_static_modifier_name_loc_coverage's existing brace-aware block-iteration + per-language-key-set pattern (reusing _iter_top_level_blocks and a new shared _loc_keys_by_language() helper), registered in main() with the same use_changed/_paths_match guard style as the existing check. Verified the new checks against the pre-fix state first (confirmed they correctly flagged exactly the six missing loc keys and three missing icon entries the user reported, with zero false positives across the other 285 files), then filled in the actual English/Simplified Chinese localization plus the modifier_icons entries (reusing the existing masonry_positive.dds already used by the sibling tv_wonder_ritual_annex_*_price_cost_modifier entries), then re-ran validate.py --ai-report unfiltered to confirm 0 errors/0 warnings across all 288 files. The missing-icon check is a WARNING (baseline-trackable, does not block game load per the engine's own log severity), while missing NAME/DESC/price-name localization is an ERROR (a real player-facing 'TODO: Write a desc' defect), matching the severity split already established by check_modifier_names' issues-vs-warnings convention. |
| lint | script | any | Using `building_efficiency:` in any colon-chained form (`<scope>.building_efficiency:<X>`, `<scope>.building_efficiency:<type>:<X>`, or bare `building_efficiency:<X>`) as a numeric value source. | For numeric building-efficiency reads, use the quoted function-call-style event target link: `value = "<location_scope>.building_efficiency(building_type:<id>)"`, or `value = "location.building_efficiency(scope:<saved_building_type>)"` when the current scope is a building and the building type was saved as a scope. For trigger checks, the block form remains valid: `building_efficiency = { building_type = building_type:<id> value >= <N> }`. Do not use the colon-chain form; it is parsed as an event-target link and either fails to resolve the building id or rejects the extra colon. Do not use `building_efficiency` as input-goods access. | 2026-08-02: tv_wonder_labor_camp_input_goods_access originally used `value = var:tv_wonder_site.building_efficiency:tv_wonder_labor_camp`, treating building_efficiency as a colon-chained numeric data function. Two in-game failures ruled out the colon chain: the bare argument form failed to resolve `tv_wonder_labor_camp`, and the scope-prefixed argument form failed earlier with `More than one colon in event target link`. The user's discovery in reference mod 3735059838 (`set_local_variable = { name = epbm_bldg_eff value = "location.building_efficiency(scope:epbm_bt)" }`) shows `building_efficiency` can be read numerically, but only through the quoted parenthesized event-target-link syntax. This quoted+parenthesized style is not a reference-mod oddity: vanilla uses the same script-value/parser form for `estate_power(...)`, `market_price(...)`, `default_price(...)`, and `border_distance_to(...)`. Later same-day in-game validation showed `building_efficiency` matches the labor/staffing satisfaction represented by `total_effective_building_levels`, not the input-goods green bar. See `building_efficiency_not_input_goods_access`. |
| advisory | script | any | Using `building_efficiency` as the material/input-goods satisfaction green bar. | For labor/staffing, use `total_effective_building_levels:<id>` versus nominal `location_building_level(building_type:<id>)`, or the quoted `building_efficiency(...)` read when a building-efficiency value is specifically needed. The green input-goods bar is GUI `Building.GetInputGoodsAccess`; no script numeric event target with that value was found in official defines. In script, use building-scope `is_lacking_goods` as the authoritative boolean gate for whether an input shortage affects the building at all. When a numeric material-side approximation is needed, use the site's market-scope `goods_supply_in_market(goods:<input>) / goods_demand_in_market(goods:<input>)` for each required input only after that boolean gate is true; average the per-input satisfaction ratios across the building's fixed input list rather than taking the bottleneck. For a two-input recipe, one input short by x% produces an x/2% material penalty. Do not use `building_goods_input` as the green bar. | For `tv_wonder_labor_camp`, user in-game validation showed `building_efficiency` and `total_effective_building_levels` both track labor/staffing satisfaction. The vanilla green input-goods indicator is `Building.GetInputGoodsAccess`, but official docs expose that only as a GUI data function, not a script numeric accessor. `building_goods_input(goods:X)` is a building-scope required-input value; the 3735059838 reference mod comments that it is scaled by market access and building efficiency, which means it is entangled with the labor value the Wonder breakdown is already displaying separately. The Wonder construction cache therefore checks the active worksite building's `is_lacking_goods` first, then uses the selected site's market supply/demand ratios for the Labor Camp's two fixed inputs, `tv_wonder_materials` and `tools`, averaging the two satisfaction ratios. This matches the observed two-input behavior: if only one input is short by x%, the building's material/input penalty is x/2%, while small market imbalances that do not trip `is_lacking_goods` apply no penalty. This is not a general alias for `GetInputGoodsAccess`; it is a local approximation chosen because the exact green bar is GUI-only. |
| lint | script | any | A block marked with `# @validate_local_variable_cleanup` sets local variables without a top-level `remove_local_variable` after the final assignment. | For high-risk or newly edited blocks that use `set_local_variable`, add `# @validate_local_variable_cleanup` near the top of the block and clean every local scratch value before leaving it with direct top-level `remove_local_variable = <name>` lines. The cleanup must appear after the last `set_local_variable` for that name, outside conditional branches. | This validator is intentionally marker-driven rather than global: the existing codebase has historical local-variable usage that should be migrated deliberately. The marker lets newly touched high-risk effects opt in immediately without turning unrelated generated legacy files into this task's blast radius. |
| advisory | gui | any | Using an unnecessarily long Community Mod Menu `mod_id` so the same namespace is repeated inside every generated setting id, callback flag, and cache key, and the mod stops appearing in the in-game CMM list | Keep the CMM namespace short and distinct from gameplay ids. Use a compact registration id such as `tv_ed` for `cmm_register_*`, and keep gameplay-facing aliases under the longer `tv_engineering_department_*` namespace. The CMM `mod_id` is just the registration prefix; it does not need to match the gameplay mod id. | 2026-08-03: the Engineering Department CMM integration used the gameplay id `tv_engineering_department` as the CMM namespace at first. That namespace was then repeated in every generated setting key and callback flag and the project did not show up in the in-game menu. Shortening the CMM id to `tv_ed` fixed the visibility issue without changing the gameplay aliases. |

## Valid Enum Values

| Enum | Valid Values | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| location_rank | rural_settlement, town, city, megalopolis | Valid location rank type keys in EU5 1.x. EU4 names like village/hamlet/metropolis are invalid; use rural_settlement/town/city/megalopolis. |
| government_type | monarchy, republic, theocracy, tribe, steppe_horde | Government type IDs for trigger: government_type = government_type:X. Use exact IDs — not pluralized (monarchies/republics). steppe_horde is the horde subtype. |

## Quick Reference Rules

- **3-Step Rule**: Direct edit only if 100% certain → `reference_official_defines/` → `reference_game_files/` + `reference_mods/`
- **Mandatory Step 2/3** for: `blockoverride` names, `custom_tooltip` keys, `situation_card_common` structure, `location_rank:*`, modifier names, scripted triggers/effects, localization YAML encoding, GUI expressions
- **Verification format**: output `**Verification** — Step [2/3], Reference: file:line, Quote: "exact text"` before any code
- **Bug fix rule**: Replace syntax with verified form; do NOT remove the feature
- **Python runner**: In Codex, Claude subagents, or managed sandboxes, run scripts with `C:\Users\Hades\anaconda3\envs\eu5\python.exe`; do not run `conda run -n eu5` there
- **Pre-test**: Run `C:\Users\Hades\anaconda3\envs\eu5\python.exe scripts\validate.py --changed` before launching game in managed sandboxes
- **Float precision**: EU5 truncates all floats at 5 decimal places; never emit 6+ dp in mod files

## Symbol Quick-Reference

> Read `data/index/<name>.txt` for the full list (one entry per line).  
> For icon availability, check `data/index/icons.txt` before using `@icon_name!` inline syntax.

| Category | Count | Index file | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| GUI Icons (`@xxx!`) | 364 | `data/index/icons.txt` | Use `@name!` in raw_text/text fields and YAML values |
| Scripted Triggers | 3851 | `data/index/scripted_triggers.txt` | Verify name before using in `trigger = { ... }` |
| Scripted Effects | 4450 | `data/index/scripted_effects.txt` | Verify name before using in `effect = { ... }` |
| Static Modifiers | 2438 | `data/index/static_modifiers.txt` | Modifier name whitelist; validate.py checks these |
| English Loc Keys | 43986 | `data/index/loc_keys_en.txt` | All defined EN keys; cross-check before adding duplicates |

## Codegen Script Map

| Script | Input(s) | Output(s) | When to run |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `scripts/gen_brief.py` | anti_patterns.yaml + valid_enums.yaml + PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md + risk_cards/ | docs/knowledge/BRIEF.md | After editing any AI knowledge file |
| `scripts/gen_index.py` | reference_game_files + src/ | data/index/*.txt symbol indexes | Run by gen_brief.py automatically |
| `scripts/gen_scaffold.py` | --type argument | Scaffold .txt/.yml file in dir | When creating a new EU5 file |
| `scripts/ai_context.py` | --changed / --files | Compact AI task context + risk cards | At AI session start or before editing a task domain |
| `scripts/test_lint_rules.py` | anti_patterns.yaml + tests/fixtures/anti_patterns/ | Fixture test report for lint regexes | After adding/changing detectability: lint rules |
| `scripts/gen_victory.py` | data/victory_paths.yaml | 13 victory-path game files (see below) | After editing data/victory_paths.yaml |
| `scripts/in_game/common/customizable_localization/gen_character_title.py` | data/io_leaders.yaml | both mod roots' full-copy character_title.txt | After editing IO leader title data |
| `scripts/gen_messagetypes.py` | vanilla messagetypes + generic actions and action data | both mod roots' full-copy messagetypes.txt | After adding or moving a generic action |
| `scripts/validate.py` | src/ mod files + knowledge YAML | Console report (exit 0/1) | Before launching game; --changed for quick check |
